## Friday, 3rd November 2023: Session - 1 : FREE PAPERS : “ONCOLOGY”: ABSTRACT NO. : 145O: Challenges of Management and Outcomes of Recurrent Wilms’ Tumor in India : Experience from a tertiary care centre

Category : ONCOLOGY

Sheetal Uttaray, Sandeep Agarwala, Vishesh Jain, Anjan Dhua, Sameer Bakhshi, Devendra Yadav, Prabudh Goel, Ahitgani Biswas, Manisha Jana, Devasenathipathy K., Priyanka, M. Bajpai

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), New Delhi Email ID : drsheetaluttaray@gmail.com

Background and Aims: The incidence of recurrence in unilateral Wilm’s tumor(WT) is about 15%. Most recurrences occur within 2 years from diagnosis. However the overall survival for patients with recurrent WT (RWT) is reported to be approximately 50 %. This study evaluates the problems in management of children with RWT in India.
Methods: Prospective study from tertiary care center in India including freshly diagnosed unilateral WT (UWT) patients treated with AIIMS WT-99 protocol from August 1999 through July 2017. Final evaluation was done in December 2022 with at least 5 years follow-up for the last included patient. All patients who developed recurrence were included and evaluated for the time of recurrence, site, alternate chemotherapy and surgical treatment offered and outcome.
Results: During this period 273 UWT were treated, of whom 268 patients were operated. Amongst these 268, 46(17.2%) developed recurrences. Fifteen (32.6%) patients with recurrences refused for further treatment. Thirty-one(67.4%) with RWT were treated. Six of these 31 (19.5%) abandoned treatment after one or two courses of alternate chemotherapy. Of these 31, 17 (17/46=37%) were reoperated and overall 12(12/46= 26.1%) achieved disease free status (DFS). Of these 12, five further died (2 from sepsis, 3 from re-recurrences) ultimately leaving only 7 (7/46=15.2%) alive with DFS. Overall 27/46 (58.7%) have died (5 after DFS; 22 with progressive disease) and 19/46 (41.3%) were alive(7 DFS; 12 with progressive disease).
Conclusions: Survival of patients with RWT in India is much lower than reported. Around one third patients refuse treatment once recurrence is diagnosed and one-fifth who start treatment abandon it early. Though one-fourth achieve disease free status ultimately only 15% were alive and disease free. As outcome of RWT is poor, partly because of refusal or abandonment of therapy all efforts should be made to decrease the incidence of recurrences in WT.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 162 O: Robotic surgery for the management of pediatric solid tumors: A decade of experience at a single center

Category : ONCOLOGY

Ashitosh Pokharkar, Sujit Chowdhary, Deepak Kandpal, Aditya Musham

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indraprastha Apollo Hospitals, Delhi Email ID : ashitoshpd106@gmail.com
Minimally invasive surgery (MIS) is a common approach for various surgeries in infants and children. Its use in pediatric oncological surgery remains debatable, but robotic surgery has the potential to overcome some of the technical constraints of conventional MIS. This study reports a decade of experience managing pediatric solid tumors with robotic surgery, analyzing its role evaluating its benefits and complications, and comparing results with the published long term outcomes of pediatric solid tumors.
A prospective single arm study was conducted at Indraprastha Apollo hospitals, New Delhi, over ten years, from 2013 to 2023, to include all patients with solid genitourinary tumors <5cm maximum diameter irrespective of pre and post chemotherapy status.
Twenty children representing twenty one solid tumors meeting the inclusion criteria underwent surgery. The main bulk of patients consisted of Wilms tumor followed by adrenal masses. The median age at surgery was 3.5 years (0.5-13years) and the median operative time was 114 min (30-150min). The length of hospital stay ranged from 2 to 5 days with the median of 3 days. Two of the twenty procedures (10%) required conversion to open surgery, and one patient with Wilms tumor and one with malignant pheochromocytoma died more than a year following surgery accounting for 10 % of deaths in the series.
The study concludes that robotic surgery for pediatric solid tumors is safe, feasible and effective in selected cases without adversely affecting outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 148 O: Lymph node dissection in Wilms’ tumor. A surgical template to improve sampling compliance.

Category : ONCOLOGY

Saswati Behera, Nitin James Peters, Ram Samujh, Amita Trehan, Kirti Gupta, Munner A. Malik, J. K. Mahajan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Bathinda, Punjab Email ID : drshaswati67@gmail.com
Background: Radical nephroureterectomy (RN) and Lymph node (LN) sampling is central to the multidisciplinary approach in the management of Wilms (WT). LN positivity has been correlated with the upgradation of the tumor stage and the addition of enhanced adjuvant therapy. The extent of LN stations explored is usually individualized and the most common deviation from the surgical protocol. We hypothesized that a tailored protocol may lead to increased compliance and adequate LN sampling in patients of WT.
Methods: This was a single center, ambi directional case-control study on patients of WT who underwent radical nephrectomy from January 2017 to May 2022 in a single surgical unit. The prospective group was recruited from January 2019 to May 2022, where a predetermined template was followed The adequacy of lymph node yield (LNY) and lymph node density (LND) affecting the overall survival and relapse rate as compared to previously operated patients was assessed.
Results: A total of 56 children met the inclusion criteria. The average number of LNs sampled was found to be significantly higher in patients of the prospective group with p-value <0.001 as compared to the retrospective group, thus showing a greater uniformity in stationed LNs harvesting. However, the overall disease-free survival was similar in both groups. Patients with more than 7 LNs sampled had slightly higher median survival time and fared better than those with patients less than 7 LNs sampled (median survival time of 23 months vs 20 months).
Conclusion: We demonstrated that following a surgical template results in higher LNY and thus LND which could affect overall survival (OS) and recurrence rate. Such a uniform approach would prevent under-staging and inadequate treatment, with increased risk for local recurrence or suspicion leading to over-staging with unnecessary chemoradiotherapy.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 143 O: Irinotecan as salvage therapy for recurrent hepatoblastoma (HB): Outcome from a tertiary care centre in a low and middle income country over a 15 year period

Category : ONCOLOGY

Sampreeti Mukherjee, Apoorv Singh, Anjan Dhua, Vishesh Jain, Sameer Bakhshi, Devasenathipathy Kandaswamy, Manisha Jana, M. Srinivas, Veereshwar Bhatnagar, Sandeep Agarwala

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : sampreetimukherjee@gmail.com
Aim: There is no consensus on best salvage therapy for recurrent hepatoblastoma (HB) and the survival for these patients is dismal. This study aims to evaluate the outcome of children with recurrent HB treated with Irinotecan, a semi-synthetic camptothecin analogue topoisomerase-I inhibitor.
Method: A Retrospective review from records of children with recurrent HB from 2007-2022, treated with irinotecan. Overall response to Irinotecan treatment and the outcome of patients was evaluated. Kaplan Meier survival estimates for a 2-year overall survival (OS), Median survival time (MST) and event free survival (EFS) was calculated. Events being re-recurrence, progression, or death.
Results: Seventeen children with age range of 6-60 months (median 16) were enrolled. They were 2(11.7%), 3(17.6%), 11(64.7%) and 1(5.8%) patients of PRETEXT 1,2,3 and 4 groups respectively. Five (30%) were standard risk while 12(70%) were high risk at initial presentation. Recurrence occurred during adjuvant chemotherapy in 4(23.5%) and after completion of chemotherapy in 13 (76.4%). Median time to recurrence from diagnosis was 11months (range 2-36m). The recurrences were local in 9(52.9%), pulmonary in 7(41.1%) and both in 1(5.8%). After salvage chemotherapy, 3(17.6%) good response, 4(23.5%) partial response, 10(59%) progressive disease. One achieved complete response on chemotherapy. Nine (53%) underwent resection of recurrence of whom, 3 are alive and disease free while 3 died on therapy; 3 alive with progressive disease. Remaining 7 could not be resected (2 died on therapy; 5 progressive disease). 2-year EFS was 27.8% (95 CI:1.8-53.8); 2-year OS 57.8% (95 CI:27.6-88.0), with Median Survival Time from diagnosis was 16 months (6-185). At last follow up, 4(23.5%) were alive and disease free.
Conclusion: Irinote can salvage therapy for recurrent HB resulted in good or partial response in 42% cases and disease-free survival in 23.5% of cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 154 O: Perioperative problems in extra-gonadal germ cell tumor in children

Category : ONCOLOGY

Naseera Koya, Pujana Kanneganti, Basant Kumar, Vijai D. Upadhyay, Ankur Mandelia

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : naseerakoya@gmail.com
Aim: To present our experience with extra gonadal abdominal GCTs and reported the perioperative problems and morbidity associated with management of these tumors.
Method: From July 2012 to January 2023, all patients with primary abdominal GCTs were retrospectively reviewed. Demographic features, clinical, laboratory, radiographic, intraoperative findings along with perioperative complications and histopathology results were assessed.
Result: A total of 26 patients with abdominal GCTs were managed. Diagnosis were teratoma in 15 (Mature- 10, Immature- 7) and yolk sac tumor (YST) in 11 patients. Out of 26 patients, 8 were female. Age ranged from 2 months to 11 years. Presentations were abdominal distention, palpable lump, fever, pain, respiratory distress, vomiting and sub-acute bowel obstruction. Serum alfa-feto protein (AFP) was raised in all patients. Complete excision was achieved in 23 patients. 12 patients were managed by chemotherapy with surgery while in 14patients, only surgery was performed. Perioperative problems include excessive blood loss (3), excision of adjacent organ (2), incomplete resection (3), hypothermia (1), respiratory problems (4), and adhesive bowel obstructions (6). Re-intervention needed in 5 patients [Re-exploration for adhesive bowel obstruction-4; and radiological drainage of retroperitoneal collection-1] within 3 months of first surgery. One patient was died while 3 patients were lost in follow-up. Follow-up ranged from 6 – 96 months.
Conclusion: The management of retroperitoneal or abdominal GCT is challenging. Various surgical difficulties and morbidity associated with resection of these tumors depend on its nature, size and site of tumor and on the age of the patient.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 161 O: Study of the impact of tumor rupture in Wilms’ Tumor

Category : ONCOLOGY

Vinay Kumar M. N., S. Ramesh, Pooja Chebbi

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute Of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : vinaykumarmn95@gmail.com
Introduction: Even though the overall outcome of Wilms tumor exceeds 90%, rupture of tumor upstages the tumor and can adversely impact the outcome.
Aim: To study the impact of tumor rupture in Wilms tumor
Material: We collected the details of children with Wilms tumor from 2014-2022 with particular reference to rupture of Wilms tumor and analyzed the possible factors for rupture and impact on the outcome
Results: We had a total of 88 children’s of Wilms tumor from 2014-2022, 5 of these children’s had tumor rupture – 2 preoperatively, 3 intraoperative. These children had very large and friable tumors, contributing to rupture. All these children were up staged to stage 3 and treated with additional chemotherapy and radiotherapy as per COG guidelines. 1 out of 5 defaulted on further treatment and died few months later. The other 4 who were compliant to the treatment and are free from relapse. On follow up (ranging from 6 months to 6 years, mean of 3.8 years). The outcome was comparable to the group with no rupture of the tumor. However, the long-term effects of additional chemotherapy and radiotherapy remain to be seen.
Conclusion: Rupture of Wilms tumor certainly upstage the tumor but the outcomes are good if treated appropriately.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 151 O: Outcomes in Neuroblastoma at a Pediatric Oncology centre

Category : ONCOLOGY

Pallav Kumar, MM Zameer, Vinay C, Sanjay Rao, Ashley D’Cruz

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health City, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : kumar.pallav97@gmail.com
Aims: To present the outcome of Neuroblastoma.
Material and methods: All children with neuroblastoma, who presented to us from January 2015 to January 2021 were included in study. Patients were stratified into different risk groups as per INRG risk stratification. They received treatment as per COG protocol as per the risk stratification (low risk with surgery alone, intermediate risk with surgery and chemotherapy, high risk was given induction chemotherapy, surgery and then high dose chemotherapy followed by autologous stem cell transplant(ASCT) and radiotherapy). Outcome parameter assessed was event free survival and overall survival.
Result: In our cohort of 44 children 54% were male. The median age at diagnosis was 22 months and 36% of the children were less than 18 months. Most of the children in our study presented with abdominal symptoms(39%). Retroperitoneum was the most common site of tumor(68%). In tumor histology as per INPC grading 60% had unfavourable histology at presentation. Metastasis was present in 66% of the patients, the most common site of metastasis was bone/bone marrow(82.7%). NMYC amplification was known in 30 patients out of which in 26 % patients it was positive.
Survival: Out of 44 children, 6 were lot to follow up so 5 year overall survival(OAS) and Event free survival(EFS) was calculated as per Kaplan Meier curve for 38 children.
 The minimum survival period was for 2 months(the child was still on treatment). The maximum survival period was for 120 months. The mean survival period was 32 months. Five-year overall survival and event-free survivals were 58% and 52% respectively for all the patients. Survival was almost similar as compared to INRG Task force data. Our study reported better outcome than previous Indian studies.

## Session - 2 : FREE PAPERS : “UROLOGY” - “Dr. SWAPNA DUTTA AWARD PAPERS”: ABSTRACT NO. : 193U: Role of emerging urinary biomarkers in predicting progressive deterioration of kidney function in congenital anomalies of kidney and urinary tract: Alpha soluble Klotho, trefoil family factor and microalbuminuria

Category : UROLOGY

Ayushi Vig, Manish Pathak, Shailja Sharma, Aliza Mittal, Kirtikumar Rathod, Rahul Saxena, Avinash Jadhav, Shubhalaxmi Nayak, Arvind Sinha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : ayushivig@gmail.com
Aims : Children with Congenital anomalies of kidney and urinary tract are at high risk of developing Chronic renal failure early. The current standards of chronic kidney disease (eGFR, urinary albumin) are less sensitive in early phases of the disease and become evident later when irreversible renal damage as already taken place. We intended to identify suitable urinary biomarkers which can identify early renal damage and predict progression to chronic kidney disease.
Methods : We performed a prospective study in 90 patients of CAKUT at our institution. On first visit, these patients underwent routine evaluation and we collected a urine sample and stored it at -80 degree temperature. After centrifugation we used commercially available ELIZA kits (BioAssay Technology Laboratory, China) to assess the initial levels of Urinary biomarkers in these patients, followed them up for 1 year and serially calculated their GFR, every 3 months. We have calculated and compared the levels of TFF3, Alpha soluble Klotho and microalbuminuria in the urine samples of patients of CAKUT having stable versus progressive renal disease. We have also compared these values to 90 age matched controls.
Results : We had 44 patients of PUV, 17 PUJO, 16 Vesicoureteric reflux, 6 Duplex collecting system, 4 MCDK and 3 Bladder exstrophy. Progression of renal disease (defined by decline in Glomerular filtration rate) was noted in 18 patients and 3 progressed to Chronic kidney disease. 4 patients had progression of the grade of CKD. The levels of urinary biomarkers were significantly higher in the patients with progressive renal disease as compared to those with stable renal disease and age matched controls.
Conclusion: Novel Urinary biomarkers can predict progression of renal disease early. These patients if identified early, can be closely monitored and can undergo early intervention to prevent progression of renal disease.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 197U: Usefulness of renal cortical transit time in assessing post-pyeloplasty recovery

Category : UROLOGY

Siddharth Pandian, Arun Prasad, Ramesh Babu Srinivasan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust Hospital and Sri Ramachandra Institute of Higher Education & Research, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : siddharth199511a@gmail.com
Aims: Diuretic renogram plays an essential role in the evaluation of recovery post pyeloplasty. Renal cortical transit time (CTT) has been studied and a cut-off value of 4 has been proposed by authors to mark recovery. The aim of this study is to validate this CTT cut-off.
Methods: We retrospectively analyzed all patients who underwent pyeloplasty from a period of Jan 2021 to Dec 2022. Post-operative renogram of these patients was compared with pre-operative status. A 10% improvement in split renal function (SRF) was considered as definite marker of recovery. CTT was compared with functional results.
Results: During the study period a total of 42 patients were analysed (median age 1.2 year; M:F = 4:1). Mean CTT was 10.4 (4.9) pre operatively and 4.1 (1.2) postoperatively; this difference was statistically significant (p=0.001). Thirty seven out of 42 patients (88%) had >10% SRF recovery. Among those who had recovery 11/37 (30%) still had prolonged CTT (>4 as per the cut-off proposed). When a CTT cut-off value of 4 was used to define success, the sensitivity, specificity, positive and negative predictive values were 70%, 40%, 89% and 15%.
Conclusion: While CTT could be a useful marker in pre-operative setting to diagnose obstruction, in the post-operative setting is a poor marker of recovery. Up to 30% of those who had functional recovery still had a prolonged CTT. Conventionally functional recovery remains as a gold standard, and in those with borderline functional recovery CTT recovery may act as an additional indicator of improvement. However CTT alone remains as a poor marker in the post-operative setting to determine recovery.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 226U: Delayed surgical repair of pediatric pelvic fracture urethral injury: Critical Analysis of outcomes at a Tertiary Care Centre

Category : UROLOGY

Ashish Ranjan, Kunj Bihari, Anwesa Chakraborty, Priyank Yadav, M S Ansari

Department of Urology & Renal Transplant (Division of Pediatric Urology), Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drashishranjan2209@gmail.com
Introduction: Management of pelvic fracture urethral injury (PFUI) in children remains a challenging task. In this study, we review in our experience of pediatric PFUIs and have formulated an algorithm for the need for pubectomy during surgical repair.
Materials and Methods: Relevant history, investigations, and treatment documented of 70 boys with PFUI managed at our institute between January 2001 and March 2022 were reviewed. Success was defined as restoration of urethral continuity and normal voiding (peak flow rate > 12ml/s).
Results: Mean age was 10.4 years. Mean length of distraction defect was 3.41 cm. Success when urethral manipulation was performed before anastomotic urethroplasty was similar to those without, overall being 80%. Access was perineal in 69 children. 10% required total pubectomy while 41.4% required inferior pubectomy. Recurrent strictures occurred in 15.7% of the cases which required direct visual internal urethrotomy (72.7%) or redo anastomotic urethroplasty (27.3%). Length of the stricture/distraction defect was a predictor for need for pubectomy. In boys younger than 5 years, pubectomy was needed in all cases with a defect > 3 cm. Likewise, the critical length was 4 cm between 5-10 years, and 5 cm >10 years of age. If the verumontanum was not visible on antegrade cystoscopy or exposure of proximal segment was inadequate, a pubectomy was required regardless of the distraction length.
Conclusion: Most PFUI in children can be managed through the perineal route with high success rate with pubectomy being reserved for more complex cases. Our algorithm can help decide if a pubectomy is necessary to perform tension free anastomosis.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 244U: Deep learning and Computer Vision to identify Ureteric Orifice on Cystoscopy

Category : UROLOGY

Vinayak S Rengan, Vankam Aseesh Varma, Pradeep Gupta, Vinita Chaturvedi, Arvind Shukla

Department of Pediatric Surgery, SMS Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : vinayak92@gmail.com

Aim: Identification of ureteric orifice in Pediatric cystoscopy using artificial intelligence, deep learning and computer vision techniques
Materials & methods: We annotated ureteric orifices from 20 cystoscopies in children using computer vision tools. The annotation was done by 2 Pediatric surgery residents supervised by a Pediatric surgeon with>10 years experience. The annotated images from the cystoscopies qualified as the ground truth. 80% of the data was used for training and validation. 20% data was used for testing. The novel deep learning model algorithm which consisted of convolutional neural networks (CNN) had been developed along with deep learning scientists. This algorithm was fed with the training and validation data.
Results: Our deep learning model was able to identify the ureteric orifices with an accuracy of 70% when compared to a manual annotator. Intersection over union (IOU) is a measure of accuracy in computer vision.
Conclusions: Identification of the ureteric orifice especially in children can be a challenge even for experienced Pediatric surgeons and urologists. The evolution of deep learning techniques have made the identification of obscure structures possible. Our team’s novel deep learning algorithms have been effective in identifying ureteric orifices. This could play a role in reducing the learning curve of early career Pediatric surgeries and up-skilling general surgeons in rural areas of the world where general surgeons perform the bulk of urological procedures. This also represents one of the first attempts by Indian Pediatric surgeons to enter the area of artificial intelligence.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 222U: Vesicostomy button as an alternative to Mitrofanoff bladder drainage

Category : UROLOGY

Abhinav Sihag, Mohammed Jaseel, Senthil Ganesh Kamaraj

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust Hospital, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : sihagabhinav9@gmail.com
Introduction: In children with poor bladder emptying, when clean intermittent catheterization (CIC) cannot be initiated for reasons of age, sensation or urethral anatomy, vesicostomy button is emerging as a safe and effective alternative with better acceptance amongst patients and caregivers for bladder management. We report our short-term experience with the vesicostomy button as a substitute to Mitofanoff bladder drainage, highlighting its benefits and complications.
Materials and methods: Retrospective study was conducted on children who had a vesicostomy button (13 in number) placed previously. Placement was through existing vesicostomy or initial placement of suprapubic catheter and replacement with button. We evaluated the procedure, compliance, efficacy of bladder emptying and immediate and long term complications.
Results: 13 children have had a vesicostomy button placed at our institution in the 5-year period, age range of 2 years to 14 years. Indications for placement included anatomic abnormalities (3), neurogenic bladder(4), non-neurogenic neurogenic bladder(2) and valve bladder(1). Complications were minor including UTI, wound infection and temporary button malfunction/leakage.
Conclusion: The vesicostomy button is an acceptable and effective alternative for good bladder drainage. It scores over the Mitrofanoff in terms of ease of placement and paucity of complications.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 192U: Kelly’s Procedure: First report of short term outcomes

Category : UROLOGY

Pallav Kumar, Lajja Jivani, MM Zameer, Vinay C, Sanjay Rao, Ashley D’Cruz

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health City, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : kumar.pallav97@gmail.com
Aim: To describe short terms outcomes in children who underwent Kelly’s Procedure.Outcomes were described for,a. Continence - dry period (with/without CIC)b. Penile cosmesis reported by patient/parentsc. Renal function testd. USG KUB for Upper tracts
Material and methods: Nine children underwent Kelly’s procedure and all were included in the study. Eight children had Exstrophy episapdias complex(EEC) and one had incontinent episapdias. Redo cases underwent Kelly’s procedure with augmentation and mitrofanoff as required. All intraoperative and post-operative complications were noted. All patients were followed up for the parameters as described in the aim.
Result: Out of nine children, eight were boys. Age ranged from 1 -19 years (mean age of 9 years). Four children had primary repair (three EEC and one epispadias), while five were redo procedures. Three redo procedures were only kelly while, two redo procedures additionally required augmentation with mitrofanoff. There were no perioperative complications noted.

a. Continence- Dry period

 Pre op Post op
Primary repair Continuous dribbling 45 to 60 mins
Redo procedure 20 to 30 mins 90 to 120 mins
b. Penile cosmesis is good, increased penile length by 2-4cm was reported
c. Renal function test was normal for all the children
d. Two cases had upper tract dilatation on USG KUB
Conclusion: This is an initial experience of Kelly’s procedure. There were no major complications. All repairs were successful. Continence has been satisfactory and is expected to improve with time. Penile cosmesis has been good. Most patients reported increased penile length by 2-4cm. Hence, we feel it is a promising technique for managing continence in exstrophy and episapdias.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 220U: Unaided visual inspection is inaccurate for penile curvature assessment and correction in the clinical setting of hypospadias repair

Category : UROLOGY

R. Satyanarayana, Khyati Kiran J, Arun Prasad, VVS Chandrasekharam, Ramesh Babu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Ankura Hospitals for Women and Children, Hyderabad, Telangana Email ID : khyati.kiranj@gmail.com
Introduction: Surgeons often rely on unaided visual inspection (UVI) to assess penile curvature (PC) during hypospadias surgery. This study aimed to compare UVI with Software App Measurement (SAM) for PC assessment.
Methods: A questionnaire containing 7 clinical pictures of PC (10°-60°) taken during hypospadias repair was answered by 101 pediatric surgeons, who were asked to assess the angle in each pictureby UVI and indicate their preferred method of correction. For each picture, the angles measured by UVI were compared with angle measured by SAM. The choice of PC correction technique for each picture was compared with standard techniques (dorsal plication for PC < 30° and urethral plate division for PC> 30°). Statistical analysis was done using One-sample t test and Fischer exact test. The ‘p’ value of<0.05 was taken as significant.
Results: Most surgeons (90%) preferred UVI for PC assessment. However, UVI accuracy was <40% for six out of seven pictures. Mean UVI estimate significantly differed from SAM angle (p<0.001), regardless of surgeon experience. Errors in UVI estimation were >10° for PC > 30°. Majority (69%) of surgeons chose incorrect correction method for 35° PC.
Conclusions: Despite surgeons’ preference for UVI, its accuracy in PC assessment during hypospadias repair is limited. UVI errors were prominent, particularly for PC in the 30°-60° range (>10° error). Underestimation of PC was common, regardless of surgeon experience. There were significant errors in choosing the correct technique for PC correction, especially for 35° PC. This study recommends objective PC assessment using SAM during hypospadias repair.

## Session - 7 : FREE PAPERS : “CONSULTANT SESSION”: ABSTRACT NO. : 98M: Clinical audit of the determinants of school drop-out and the impact of preventive measures undertaken in children undergoing major surgeries

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Mamta Sengar, Niyaz, Sauradeep Dey, Ankur Bharadwaj, Chhabi Ranu Gupta, Vivek Manchanda, Parveen Kumar, Raghav Narang

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : mamtasengar@gmail.com
Background and Aim: Drop-out from school is a disconcerting issue for children suffering from various diseases requiring major pediatric surgical interventions. The aim of the clinical audit was to find out the key factors responsible for such drop-outs in school-going patients and the impact of the strategies that were undertaken.
Materials and methods: School-going children, aged 6 years and above, who underwent major surgeries at the pediatric surgery department of a tertiary care government hospital, were included. The clinical audit was performed in two phases. In the first phase, parents of previously operated patients were interviewed and administered standardized questionnaire to gather information regarding any dropout from school and the reason for such dropout. Diagnosis and surgical procedure performed, educational and economic status of the parents were recorded. In the second phase, bed-side counseling was provided by health -care personnels to parents of every patient undergoing major surgery. The school dropout in the counseled group was then assessed by similar questionnaire.
Results: In first-phase audit, school drop-out rate in the 279 operated patients (over 3 months period) was 18%. Parents’ educational and socio-economic status, the surgical condition itself and the nature of surgery performed were found to be key determinants. Most of the drop-outs were seen in patients who belonged to lower socio-economic group, patients with external stoma, patients with urinary / fecal incontinence, long waiting period for those awaiting surgery, patients on cancer-chemotherapy or prolonged morbidity arising out of surgery itself (including prolonged hospitalization). In the counseled group (second-phase audit), the drop-out rate in 232 operated patients (over 3 months) was found to be 7.5%.
Conclusion: Pre-emptive guidance / counseling in children undergoing major surgery can help to reduce drop-outs from school.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 91M: EXIT procedure for complex head and neck fetal masses: Technical details, surgical experience and multidisciplinary approach in 2 cases

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Ankur Mandelia, Tarun Gupta, Priya Mathew, Mandakini Pradhan, Neeta Singh, Anita Singh, Kirti Naranje, Ashish Kanojia

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences (SGPGIMS), Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh drankurmandelia@gmail.com

Objectives: In fetuses who are predicted to be at risk of catastrophic airway obstruction at delivery, the ex utero intrapartum treatment (EXIT) procedure is useful for securing the fetal airway while maintaining fetal oxygenation via placental circulation. Herein, we report our experience of the EXIT procedure in 2 fetuses with complex neck masses. Methods: Case 1: A 26-year-old primigravida presented to our fetal medicine unit at 38 weeks of gestation with an ultrasonogram (USG) showing a large, heterogeneous neck mass in the fetus. Fetal MRI revealed a large 12 cm × 11 cm × 9 cm, solid–cystic mass in the left cervical region, completely engulfing and displacing the fetal airway. Case 2- A 32-year-old G3P1 woman was referred to us at 33 weeks of gestation. Fetal USG revealed a large multiseptated cystic lesion of size 6 x 7 cm, occupying whole of left side on neck, crossing midline, with infra temporal and base of skull extension, causing mass effect over airway and pharynx. In both patients, an EXIT to airway procedure in conjunction with cesarean section was planned under a multidisciplinary team with detailed informed consent.
Results: After induction of anaesthesia, uterine relaxation was maintained with sevoflurane combined with continuous intravenous infusion of nitroglycerin for maintaining utero-placental circulation. Uterus was opened by a low transverse incision, bag of membranes was left intact, controlled artificial rupture of membranes was performed and amnioinfusion of 1L of warm ringer lactate was started. After hysterotomy, the head and right upper limb of the fetus were partially delivered, and fetal heart tones were monitored with a sterile stethoscope. After oropharyngeal suctioning to improve the visual field, the fetus was intubated successfully using a sterile video-laryngoscope (VL). Immediately after the fetal airway was secured, the fetus was fully delivered with umbilical cord clamping and shifted to NICU on mechanical ventilation. Cesarean delivery was successfully performed without any problems in both the cases.

Case 1: neonate successfully underwent surgical excision for removal of the neck mass under general anesthesia on the 5th day after delivery. Histopathology revealed a mature teratoma.
Case 2: neonate underwent two sessions of USG guided sclerotherapy for a diffuse veno-lymphatic malformation and there was partial clinical response noted initially. On day 40 of life, baby expired due to sepsis.

Conclusions: The EXIT procedure is feasible, safe and effective for securing the fetal airway for complex head and neck fetal masses. It requires detailed planning and a multi-disciplinary team approach for a successful outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 64H: Role of PS6 in Choledochal Cyst Malformations: An Immunohistochemical Evidence

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Ajay Verma, Ajay Verma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), New Delhi Email ID : talk2ajayverma@gmail.com
Aim: Cycloxygenase-2 (COX-2) and phosphorylated 40s ribosomal protein S6 (PS6) are two pro-inflammatory and proliferative molecules. This study aims to show the expression of these molecules in choledochal cyst malformations.
Methods: A study on specimens of patients operated for choledochal cyst malformations (CM). Immunohistochemical (IHC) stains with antibodies to COX-2 and PS6 were done both on CM and gall bladder (GB) specimens following proper protocol. For standardization, intensity of IHC were scored: 0 (negative), 1(weak), 2 (moderate) and 3 (strong) positives and distribution was scored from 0 (negative), 1+ (<10 to 25%), 2+ (26-50%), 3+ (51-75%) and 4+ (76-100%) of cells. All the specimens were analyzed by a single pathologist. Chi square test was applied for the discrete variables and analyzed by STATA version 17.
Results: Specimens from twelve (n=12) patients were analyzed. Mean age of the patients was 6.9 years with a female preponderance (M:F-1:3). PS6 IHC showed an over expression, with high intensity signals from 7 specimens of CM versus 1 of GB specimen (p=0.001). There was a 4+ distribution in 9 CM specimens versus 1 in GB (p=0.0003). COX-2 however, was not expressed in most of the CM and GB specimens. 8 of CM and 10 of GB specimens showed 0 intensity of COX-2 (p=0.47). Similarly, 8 of CM and 11 of GB specimens showed 0 cell distribution of COX-2 (p=0.23).
Conclusion: Immunohistochemical staining demonstrated the over expression of PS6 in specimens of choledochal malformations. COX-2 however, was not over expressed in the specimens. This finding provides an insight for the inflammatory and proliferative etiology of PS6 in choledochal malformations.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 124N: Use of Rhomboid Flap and its modifications in closure of open Neural tube defects: Closure technique and lessons learnt

Background: Management of Open neural tube defects is challenging and outcome is demanding.

Material and methods: The data between June 2017- Dec 2022 was analysed. Those with incomplete data, not giving consent, closure done using other technique or lost to followup were excluded. Patient included in the study underwent closure using Rhomboid flap and dufourmentel modification of limberg flap(DMLF). Dura closure was achieved with native dura augmented with thoracolumbar fascia. The outcome was analysed with respect to duration of surgery, blood loss, Wound infection, Flap blackening, CSF leak and features of sepsis.
Result: 65 cases formed the study group. Mean age at presentation was 4 days (Range 0-28days). Mean duration of surgery was 2 days( Range 1-3 days). CSF leak was seen in 2 cases in each group. Wound dehiscence and sepsis were seen in 1 and 8 cases respectively.
Conclusion: Management of open neural tube defect requires adequate planning. CSF shunting and flap closure is often required.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 234U: Primary Vesicoureteral Junction Obstruction (PVUJO) – Experience from a Pediatric Nephrourology Clinic

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Kanishka Das, Kiran Mahadevappa, Prasanna Kumar, Akash Bihari Pati, Amit Kumar Satpathy, Shalini G. Hegde, Rajkiran S. Raju, Priya Pais, A. M. Shubha, Anil Vasudevan, Arpana Iyengar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Bhubaneswar, Odisha Email ID : kanishkadas@hotmail.com
Aims: Analyse the clinical spectrum, diagnosis and management of PVUJO at two tertiary teaching hospitals.
Methods : Rétrospective review of clinical presentation, investigations, diagnostic dilemmas, surgical management and long termoutcome at a speciality clinic.
Results : Of 40 children (31 males, 9 females ; 18 infants) with PVUJO (34 unilateral , 6 bilateral), 20 were diagnosed antenatally. Four were asymptomatic ; the rest had febrile UTI (25), pyonephrosis - urosepsis (4), abdominal pain (6) & hypertension (2) and acute renal failure (2). Associated anomalies were genitourinary (12, PUJO-3) or multisystemic (2) ; two siblings had CAKUT. A palpable renomegaly was occasional (5). Diagnoses was confirmed with a diureticrenogram in all. After excision of the abnormal VUJO, the ureters were reimplanted primarily (30) or diverted (12, diameter> 2.5 cm, tortuous) and subsequent reimplanted. Four ureterostomies await réimplantation.The technique of ureteral tapering -réimplantation was individualised. Post operatively, seven had 1-2 episodes of UTI, four required revisional surgery (2- reimplantation ,3 –ureterostomy) The follow up (36/40) spans 2 - 20 years, all are asymptomatic. Post operative renal growth, parenchy malfunction and drainage is normal in 40/46 renal units ; two were unsalvageable and excised. Mild non obstructive urinary tract dilatation persists in many.
Conclusion : PVUJO is amenable to antenatal diagnosis, early postnatal confirmation and surgical intervention. Both reconstructive approaches (préliminary ureterostomy – secondary réimplantation and primary ureteric reimplantation) ensured renal parenchymal preservation and long term growth.

Better cosmesis
Parental & child satisfaction
Immensely heightened self confidence and self esteem of the boys

## Saturday, 4th November 2023: Session - 9 : FREE PAPERS : “NEONATAL”: ABSTRACT NO. : 128N: Management and Outcomes in Stage IIIB Necrotizing Enterocolitis: A Retrospective Study

Category : NEONATAL

Karthik S. A., Veerabhadra

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : karthiksagowda@gmail.com
Aim: To assess the need for surgery and predict outcomes in newborns diagnosed with stage IIIB necrotizing enterocolitis (NEC).
Methods: A retrospective analysis was conducted in the Department of Pediatric Surgery at tertiary care centre. The study included neonates with stage IIIB NEC who were treated between Jan 2015 and Dec 2022.
Results: A total of 70 neonates were treated for stage IIIB NEC during the study period. Among them, 60 neonates were included in the study, with 39 being male. The mean age at presentation was 23 ± 14 days, and the mean weight was 2056 ± 615 grams. Among the neonates, 8 (13%) were early preterm, 33 (55%) were late preterm, and 19 (32%) were term. All 60 neonates underwent glove drain placement, with 16 (27%) of them surviving without requiring further interventions. Out of the remaining 36 neonates, who underwent laparotomy, 27 (45%) survived. Preterm neonates (28/41 vs. 8/19; p = 0.05; chi-square test) and neonates with a weight ≤ 2 kg (22/29 vs. 14/31; p = 0.015; chi-square test) had a higher likelihood of requiring surgery. Female gender (19/21 vs. 24/39; p = 0.018; chi-square test), term gestation (17/19 vs. 26/41; p = 0.04; chi-square test), higher mean weight (2151 ± 615 g vs. 1819 ± 563 g; p = 0.05; Student t-test), and weight > 2 kg (37/43 vs. 6/17; p < 0.001; chi-square test) were associated with increased survival.
Conclusions: Approximately 60% of neonates diagnosed with stage IIIB NEC required surgical intervention. However, term newborns and those weighing over 2 kg could be managed with glove drain placement alone. Female neonates, term gestation, and neonates weighing more than 2kg demonstrated improved survival rates.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 131N: Factors affecting survival of newborns with gastroschisis from a tertiary care children’s hospital

Category : NEONATAL

Pute U. Losu, S. Roy Choudhury, Vikram Khanna, Partap S. Yadav, Rajiv Chadha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : drputelosu15@gmail.com
Objective: To study the factors affecting survival of gastroschisis patients in a tertiary care children’s hospital.
Materials and methods: A retrospective chat review was done, wherein the data of neonates with gastroschisis managed between December 2018 to June 2023 in our centre were analysed. The parameters recorded were centre of delivery, gestational age, birth weight, mode of delivery, associated intestinal atresia, surgical technique of closure, need for post-operative ventilation, time to full feeds. The effect of the above factors on survival was analysed.
Results: Out of the total 27 patients,19 (70%) patients survived, wherein M:F was 15:4; 14 were delivered in-house and 5 were referred following delivery elsewhere; the gestational age ranged from 36 weeks to term; 17(89%) patients had a birth weight of >1.5 kgs. 12 (63%) patients were delivered by caesarean section. 7% had associated intestinal atresia.7 patients underwent surgery on day 1 of life. Majority (84%) of the patients were operated by primary skin closure; All patients received NICU care and were kept on ventilator support. The mean time to full feeds was 18 days in the survivors. Of the 8 patients who expired, M:F was 7:1; 6 were referred following delivery elsewhere;5 patients were preterm; birth weight ranged from 1.2 to 2.4 kgs;6 patients were delivered by caesarean section;4 patients had primary skin closure; All patients received NICU care and were kept on ventilator support.
Conclusion: In-house delivery, near term gestational age,birth weight >1.5 kgs,early surgery, primary skin closure, absence of associated intestinal atresia and early feed tolerance were associated with higher survival in gastroschisis.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 135N: Glycopyrrolate in the management of anastomotic leak following repair of esophageal atresia Vogt type 3B

Category : NEONATAL

Ashok Kumar Chopra, Rahul Gupta

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sawai Man Singh, Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : ashokchopra1993@gmail.com
Aims: Anastomotic leak after repair of esophageal atresia with tracheoesophageal fistula is a well-known complication and can represent a challenging clinical scenario. The aim of the present study was to evaluate the role of glycopyrrolate in the treatment of anastomotic leak after repair of esophageal atresia Vogt type 3b.
Methods: A retrospective study was carried out in our tertiary care teaching institute from January 2016 to December 2022. A detailed clinical and radiological evaluation were performed in each case. The study comprised neonates of esophageal atresia, who had undergone primary surgery and developed anastomotic leak. The study included patients with major, minor, and radiological leaks. The patients were administered glycopyrrolate in the dose of 4 μg/kg 8 hourly. The end points of the study were either resolution or progression of the leak.
Results: There were 21 patients who were managed with glycopyrrolate. All the cases had anastomotic leaks with either clinically detectable in the chest tube(15) or radiological leak(6).out of these 15 patients there were 5 patients with major leak in whom there was no resolution of leak and were subjected to diversion procedures. in 10 patients with minor leak there was complete resolution of anastomoticleak; there was 1 mortality and 1 diversion procedures. There was complete resolution in 5 radiological patients.
Conclusions: Administration of glycopyrrolate in patients of minor anastomotic leak after primary repair of esophageal atresia resulted in reduced oral secretions, which helped in healing of the anastomotic dehiscence in a significant number of patients. Glycopyrrolate may be a useful adjunct in the management of anastomotic leak after tracheoesophageal repair.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 140N: Outcomes of Gastroschisis at a Tertiary Centre in UK

Category : NEONATAL

Frederica Cranfield, Agana Gnanakuruparan, Chandrasen Sinha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, St George’s University Hospital NHS Foundation Trust, UK Email ID : cksinha@btinternet.com
Introduction: The aim was to investigate the outcomes of neonates born with Gastroschisis, with special reference to the maternal co-morbidities and type of surgical closure, along with other factors.
Methods: This was a retrospective study for 15.5 years (2007-2022), sourced from electronic record. Details like, operative findings, type of closure, time to full, complications and mortality, etc. were recorded. Data were quoted as median unless indicated otherwise. Data were analysed using SPSS software Desktop 22.0, using Mann-Whitney U and Chi-Squared tests, with a consideration that a P≤0.05 was significant.
Results: A total of 132 patients with gastroschisis were included. Of those 96.8% (n=125) were antenatally diagnosed. The median age of the neonate on day of closure of gastroschisis was day 6. The method of closure was sutureless in 49% and sutured closure in 51%. The median day to start feed was day 14 and the median day to establish full feed was day 27. The most frequently associated maternal co-morbidities were depression in 13% and asthma in 12%. The most common post-surgical complication at follow up was incisional hernia, found in 29% of patients. Mortality was found in 3% of cases.
Conclusion: Neonates with Gastroschisis have 97% survival at our centre. Incision hernia was found to be the commonest postoperative complication. Depression and asthma were found to be important associated co-morbidity maternal factors, along with other known associated factors like smoking and early maternal age.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 132N: Para-oesophageal hernia in neonates

Category : NEONATAL

Aravindan Chandrasekaran, Manikandan Ulagamuthu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Meenakshi Mission Hospital and Research Centre, Madurai, Tamilnadu Email ID : scaravindu@gmail.com
Aims: Para oesophageal hernias are uncommon in children and very rarely they present in the neonatal period. They are very different from sliding hiatus hernias . We want to share our experience of neonates who presented with para oesophageal hiatus hernia.
Methods: study of 4 cases of neonatal para oesophageal hernias presented during five year period from March 2018 to April 2023.
Results: All the four cases ( male-2 and female- 2) presented with respiratory distress from birth. All of them underwent Chest X ray which showed mediastinal mass in the lower part of mediastinum. Barium studies and CT Scan were performed . All of them underwent underwent laparotomy and surgical correction by reducing the contents and derotation of the gastric volvulus if present. Diaphragmatic crural repair, fundoplication and anterior gastropexy were done in all the cases. There were no significant complications post operatively.
Conclusion: Congenital paraesophageal hernia, is very rare in neonatal age group and usually presents with respiratory distress or gastric volvulus. Early diagnosis and prompt surgical repair are required to avoid mobility and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 134N: Mesenteric Lymphangioma’s: Presentation spectrum, management options and lessons learnt

Category : NEONATAL

Nitin Sharma, Shipra Sharma, MA Memon, Mini Sharma, Arti Pandey, Sushma Ramteke

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Dau Kalyan Singh Post Graduate Institute and Research Center, Raipur, Chattisgarh Email ID : scaravindu@gmail.com
Background: Presentation of mesenteric lymphagiomas is varied and most cases require exploration.
Aims: To analyse the mode of presentation, management options and outcome of patients operated for mesenteric Lymphangioma.
Material and methods: The data between June 2017- May 2023 was analysed. Those with incomplete data, not giving consent, intraoperative diagnosis other than mesenteric Lymphangioma or lost to followup were excluded. Patient included in the study underwent ultrasound and X Ray abdomen. After stabilisation exploratory laparotomy was done. Lymphangioma was excised either by resection of the bowel with mesentery or with preservation of bowel. The outcome was analysed with respect to mode of presentation, duration of surgery, blood loss, Wound infection, days of starting oral feeds, duration of hospital stay, condition at first follow up.
Result: 17 out of 39 cases formed the study group. Mean age at presentation was 4 days (Range 0-240 days). Mean duration of surgery after admission was 2 days (Range 1-3 days). Wound dehiscence and sepsis were seen in 1 and 3 cases respectively. All the patients were stable in last follow up. There was no mortality in our study.
Conclusion: Management of mesenteric Lymphangioma often require resection and anastomosis. Adequately planned procedure gives excellent results.
