## Session - 10 : AWARD SESSION : “U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS”: ABSTRACT NO. : 232U: Comparison of urinary biomarkers in diagnosis of ureteropelvic junction obstruction

Category : U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS

Ashwin Rajkumar, Ramesh Babu, Sangeetha Perungo

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sri Ramachandra Institute of Higher Education & Research, Chennai, Tamilnadu drrameshbabu1@gmail.com
Background & Aims: In children with antenatally detected hydronephrosisdifferentiation of non-obstructive dilatation (NOD) from uretero pelvic junction obstruction (UPJO) is a challenge. The aim of this study is to compare the utility of urinary biomarkers:carbonic anhydrase antigen (CA 19-9), neutrophil gelatinase associated lipocalin (NGAL) and kidney injury molecule (KIM) in diagnosing UPJO.
Methods: This prospective study was conducted after obtaining necessary ethical clearance between 2021 and 2022. Control/ Group 1 (n=30) had children with normal antenatal ultrasound and no other urinary symptoms. Study group (n=48): involved children with unilateral hydronephrosis divided into group 2a / NOD: (n=24) those who were stable on ultrasound/ diuretic renogram and group 2b/UPJO (n=24) those who worsened to grade 4 hydronephrosis on ultrasound/ reduced differential renal function (10% drop) on renogram / symptoms (abdominal pain) and who underwentpyeloplasty. A mid-stream clean catch urine sample was collected from all children; centrifuged at 3000 rpm for 10 minutes, transferred into cryotubes to store at − 80 °C till analysis. Urinary biomarkers NGAL, KIM-1, and CA 19-9 (Bio assay technology laboratory) were measured using ELISA. Children with bilateral hydronephrosis or associated urinary tract abnormalities were excluded.
Results: There was no significant age/sex difference between the groups. Urine CA 19-9 level was 140.1 (45) U/ml in UPJO group and this was significantly higher (p=0.001) than that of NOD, 62.3 (22) or control, 4.7(3.7). Urine NGAL level was 30.1 (17.1) pg/ml in UPJO and this was significantly higher than controls, 2.7(0.9) but not NOD, 25.9 (15.4). Urine KIM level was 749.5 (485) pg/ml in UPJO group and this was significantly higher than controls, 305(125) but not NOD, 767 (422). ROC analysis of CA 19-9 revealed a urine biomarker cut-off of 95 U/ml for diagnosing UPJO (sensitivity 95%; specificity 96%; AUC 0.99).
Conclusions: CA 19-9 is a superior marker compared to NGAL and KIM in differentiating UPJO from non-obstructive dilatation. Further studies should focus on usefulness of urine biomarkers in determining post-operative recovery.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 65H: Transplanting livers in young children - Looking back at 100 cases

Category : U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS

Reya Rachel Abraham, MM Zameer, Vinay C, Sanjay Rao, Ashley D’ Cruz

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health City, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : reya.r.abraham@gmail.com
Introduction: Despite advances in medical therapy, liver transplant continues to be the only definitive mode of therapy for children with end-stage liver disease. However, its acceptance in India has been discouraging mostly due to financial and logistic reasons than the availability of expertise. This report outlines the journey of a single pediatric surgical unit and highlights issues pertinent to our circumstances.
Aim: To review a single pediatric surgical center’s 100 case experience with liver transplantation.
Methods: This is a longitudinal cohort study of 100 children who underwent LT at our center. Data was acquired from a continuously updated structured database.
Results: A hundred children underwent Liver transplantation. Sixty-four were boys. The median age was 17 months (range 5 -144 months). The mean weight of the cohort was 7.5 kg (range 3.7 to 31.5kg). Sixty percent of our children weighed less than 10 kg. Biliary atresia was the most common indication (60%) for transplantation, others include metabolic disorders, PFIC, hepatoblastoma, and acute liver failure. Ninety-two children underwent living donor LT (mothers being the majority of the donors) and eight had cadaveric grafts. None of the donors had any major post-operative complications.
Post-operative complications included bleeding (8), vascular complications (21), biliary leak (12), intestinal complications (13) & sepsis (33).
Early deaths were 21, causes included vascular thrombosis and sepsis.
The mean survival time (years) was 14.6 years. The mean follow-up time (years) of patients was 8.5 years, with 4 patients lost to follow-up. The long-term survival was 65%.
All patients on follow-up were found to have good catch-up growth by 3rd and 5th-year post-transplant. The long-term sequelae include recurrent hospitalization, EBV viremia (38) & graft rejection (18), leading to attrition.
Conclusion: Our experience demonstrates the feasibility of LT in children with ESLD in India. With longer-living grafts, patients often struggle with other issues such as compliance with follow-up, financial issues, and recurrent infections. We are able to demonstrate satisfactory outcomes of long-term survivors of liver transplantation children and also good improvement in quality of life and appropriate psychosocial activities.
Close monitoring with regular follow-up of patients helps in early recognition and treatment of late-onset complications thus helping the overall long-term outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 201U: Role of urinary biomarkers (Transforming growth factor β1, Neutrophil gelatinase-associated lipocalin and Cystatin C) as a prognostic factor of renal outcome in PUV

Category : U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS

Nitesh Kumar Sharma, Shasanka Shekhar Panda, Simmi K Ratan, Sujoy Neogi, Binita Goswami, Rakesh Kumar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, Delhi Email ID : drniteshist@gmail.com
Aims: To compare the urinary levels of TGF-β1 (marker of inflammation), NGAL (marker of pelvic dilatation) & CysC (marker of proximal tubular injury) between PUV cases & age matched controls and to correlate these with renal structural & functional parameters.
Methods: A prospective study including children with a diagnosis of PUV made using standard investigations and an equal number of age matched controls with non-urological problems was undertaken. For study subjects, the urinary samples were collected at three different time points (pre & post-operatively at 3 & 6months) whereas for controls only single voided samples were studied. Urinary level of TGF-β1, NGAL, and CysC was estimated by standardized techniques using ELISA kits. Statistical methods were used to drive comparisons between cases & controls.
Results: Fifteen children with a median age of 10(5-48)months were enrolled in each of the two groups. The mean uTGF-β1 in case group was significantly higher at all three time points [43.20±6.13pg/ml, 43.33±11.89pg/ml and 40.71±9.01pg/ml] as compared to the control group [29.12±8.31pg/ml] (p≤0.001). The median uNGAL in case group was also higher (17.78ng/ml, 2.35ng/ml and 2.536ng/ml) as compared to the control group (1.31ng/ml). However, the difference was significant only preoperatively (p=0.02). The median uCysC in case group was similarly higher [0.347 μg/ml, 0.439μg/ml and 0.382 μg/ml) as compared to control group (0.243μg/ml) (p>0.05). The S. creatinine in case group (0.49mg/dl) showed no significant rise above that of control (0.24 mg/dl). A cut-off value of uTGF-β1=36.55pg/ml(p<0.001), uNGAL=0.879ng/ml(p=0.02) & uCysC=0.25μg/ml(p=0.22) was found associated with renal damage in PUV. A significant correlation was found between uNGAL & S. creatinine at 3months (r=0.43, p=0.017) & 6months (r=0.47, p=0.08).
Conclusion: The significantly raised biomarker in cases as compared to the controls precede the S. creatinine changes and can help in early diagnosis of renal injury. Post-fulguration a suboptimal decline in uTGF-β1 (5%) suggests an ongoing inflammation in renal parenchyma despite fulguration while a decline in uNGAL (85%) suggests an improvement in hydronephrosis. The rise in uCysC (10%) suggests persistent proximal tubular dysfunction which doesn’t resolve early post fulguration. The use of such biomarkers appears promising in PUV.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 2E: Untargeted maternal plasma metabolomics in hirschsprung disease

Category : U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS

Chetan Padashetty, Shalini G Hegde, Sarita Devi, Roshni M Passanna, Shubha A M, Arpita Mukhopadhyay, Anura V Kurpad

Department of Pediatric Surgery, St. Johns Medical College and Hospital, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : chetanpadashetty1610@gmail.com
Background and Aims: Hirschsprung Disease (HSCR) is a congenital disorder of unknown etiology affecting the enteric nervous system (ENS) of a child. The development of the ENS takes place during early gestation and hence can be affected prenatally by the metabolic environment provided by the mother. The objective of this pilot study was to explore the role of maternal plasma metabolites in the aetiology of Hirschsprung disease (HSCR)
Methods: This is a cross-sectional analytical study where plasma samples were obtained from mothers of children diagnosed with HSCR (n=7) and age-matched mothers of normal children (n=6). Plasma samples were analysed by ultra-high pressure liquid chromatography (UHPLC, coupled to an orbitrap mass spectrometer. Metabolites were identified by mzCloud using Compound Discoverer software and using an untargeted metabolomics workflow, the differences in case vs control groups were identified. Metabolic pathways were acquired by Kyoto Encyclopaedia Genes and Genomes (KEGG) pathway database.
Results: A total of 268 unique plasma metabolites were identified in maternal plasma and annotated. Of these, 57 were found to be significantly different between cases and control groups (p-value <0.05 by t-test). Using an FDR corrected cut-off of 10% to adjust for multiple comparisons, 19 metabolites were significantly associated with HSCR, including carnitines, medium chain fatty acids and glutamic acid. Amino acid, lipid metabolism and tryptophan-kynurenine pathways were found to affected.
Conclusion: Disordered metabolic pathways in the mother may be involved in the etiopathogenesis of HSCR in the child. This is the first study to assess the maternal plasma metabolomics in HSCR.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 136N: Topical application of Salt versus silver Nitrate for Umbilical Granuloma in infants (SNUG) trial – A single centre, open label, randomized controlled trial

Category : U. C. CHAKRABORTHY AWARD PAPERS

Arka Banerjee, Gayatri Munghate, Pradnya Bendre

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital for Children, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : arkabanerjee6989@gmail.com

Introduction: Treatment of umbilical granuloma in infants with silver nitrate is effective but associated with adverse effects like skin irritation/burning. Common salt is proposed as a safer alternative that is less painful and as effective.
Methods: We conducted a randomized controlled trial of infants with umbilical granuloma who presented to our hospital between October 2022 and May 2023. They were randomly assigned, using the android app Randomizer®, to receive either common salt or silver nitrate (AgNO3). The common salt group received thrice daily applications of a pinch of common salt by the parents for 5 days, while the other group received a single application of AgNO3 by a doctor, with an occlusive dressing, for 48 hours. The primary outcome measure was the proportion of infants with complete healing of umbilical granuloma within 15 days of initiating treatment. The secondary outcome measures included adverse effects such as skin irritation, recurrence and infection.
Results: Out of 23 infants included in the study, 14 were randomized to salt treatment group and 9 to AgNO3. Mean age of presentation was 29.54 ± 38.64 days. Complete healing of umbilical granuloma within 15 days was seen in 100% (14/14) infants in common salt group versus 66.67% (6/9) in AgNO3 group (p=0.02). Only one recurrence was observed, in the AgNO3 group. There were no adverse effects in either group.
Conclusion: Topical use of common salt is more effective than silver nitrate for treatment of umbilical granuloma in infants. There are no significant differences in the safety profile.

## Session - 11 : FREE PAPERS : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - “K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS”: ABSTRACT NO. : 16G: Peri-operative management of esophageal substitution with stomach in children

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Chandra Vibhash, Subhasis Roy Choudhury, Pratap Singh Yadav, Rajiv Chaddha, Kanu Kapoor

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College & Associated Kalawati Saran Children Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : chandravibhash@yahoo.com
Aims : The goal of this study was to evaluate the efficacy of our peri-operative management protocol for esophageal substitution with stomach in children.
Methods: This is a retrospective analysis including all patients of pediatric esophageal substitution using the stomach at our institution over the last 22 years. A peri-operative management protocol was followed and results analyzed.
Results: Forty six cases of gastric substitution of the esophagus in children were done; gastric pull-up (GPU) and a gastric tube was made in 40 and 6 patients respectively. Posterior mediastinal and retrosternal routes were used in 21 & 19 cases of GPU. Except 7 patients operated before year 2005, all other patients received pre-operative beta-blocker in the form of metoprolol 2-3 days before surgery. In addition to general anesthesia, a thoracic epidural for pain management was sited in 37 patients. Thirty four patients were ventilated postoperatively, which included all patients with posterior mediastinal GPU; the mean duration of ventilation was 48.5 hours. Twelve patients (6-retrosternal GPU, 6-tube) did not require post-operative ventilation. Significant intraoperative and postoperative tachycardia was seen in 85.7% and 71.4% of patients respectively without beta-blocker (p = 0.004, p < 0.001). Overall mortality was (n=5) 10.9 % but mortality due to cardiac arrhythmia was 42.9% in the patients not receiving beta-blockers (p = 0.001).
Conclusions: Our peri-operative management protocol resulted in a satisfactory outcome. Ventilation with ICU care was required mostly for GPU through post-mediastinal route. Peri-operative use of beta-blockers reduced the incidence of fatal tachyarrhythmias and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 33G: Estimation of Glycosylated Hemoglobin in pediatric patients with Chronic Pancreatitis

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Keshava Pavan K., Raghunath BV, Veerabhadra R

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru Email ID : kpavank123@gmail.com
Aims: To determine elevated glycosylated haemoglobin (HbA1c) levels in pediatric patients with chronic pancreatitis.
Methods: This was an observational cross-sectional study conducted in the department of Pediatric Surgery which included patients diagnosed with chronic pancreatitis aged <18 years. Peripheral blood samples were analysed for HbA1c levels. HbA1c level of <5.7 was considered normal, those between 5.7 and 6.5 were considered as ‘pre-diabetes’ and those above 6.5 were considered as ‘diabetes’.
Results: A total of 20 patients were included as a pilot project. 6 (30%) of patients had diabetes and 6 (30%) had prediabetes. There was a female preponderance of chronic pancreatitis with 13 (65%) being female. Out of the 12 patients with prediabetes or diabetes (60%), only 1 patient (8%) had symptoms of pancreatic exocrine insufficiency. 4 out of the 6 patients with diabetes have had symptoms of pancreatitis for <1 year of age. 12 out of 20 (60%) have undergone some type of drainage procedure, and 7 out of them (58%) have prediabetes or diabetes. 3 patients (15%) had pancreas divisum, and 2 out of them (66%) were pre-diabetic. Fischer’s exact test was used to find whether pre-diabetes and diabetes in chronic pancreatitis was impacted by gender, duration of pancreatitis, presence of intraductal or intraparenchymal calcifications, presence of pseudocyst, presence of pancreas divisum and none were found to be significantly impacting the presence of diabetes.
Conclusion: Many patients of chronic pancreatitis, without any symptoms of pancreatic insufficiency have elevated levels of HbA1c, and early identification can help in prevention of further progress and timely treatment of diabetes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 49G: Attrition rate among patients of Anorectal Malformations on colostomy- Is it high?

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Parveen Kumar, Vivek Manchanda, Mamta Sengar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : parveenkumar_maan@yahoo.co.in
Aims: To find out attrition rate in ARM patients and to find out causes for same.
Methodology: This was a retrospective observational study done at the Pediatric surgery department of Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitasalaya, New Delhi. All the files with final diagnosis of ARM who underwent diversion stoma at our centre from January 2018 to December 2019 were retrieved. The parents were contacted telephonically to find out if the children had underwent definitive surgeries at some other centre, was awaiting surgery, or had died. The attrition was calculated as the difference between the number of patients for whom stoma was done for ARM and the number of patients who were lost to follow up after 6 months post-operative period.
Results: A total of 105 patients were included in the study with male preponderance. Majority presented in neonatal period (77/105). The cloacal malformation was found in 7, rectal atresia in 6 while 9 had congenital pouch colon entity. The age at stoma creation for ARM ranged from 1 day to 9.3 years. The cardiac and renal anomalies were among major associations. 28 of neonates had stomal complications. Overall stomal issues reported in 32.4 %. Total mortality was 37 patients, with 35 in neonatal period. The attrition rate in our study was found to be 24.76% (26 out of 105). And major causes for attrition included anemia, long waiting list, PAC unfit due to URI and Family issues.
Conclusion: There was high attrition rate in operated patients of ARM with stoma. The main reasons of the same were anemia and long surgical waiting list. This study highlights the need for early surgery or primary PSARP as soon as possible to avoid potential morbidity and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 25G: Single stage PSARP for RVF: A single centre experience

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Priyanka Warde, Rakesh Joshi, Jaishree Ramji, Aniz Ratani, Nirkhi Shah

Department of Pediatric Surgery, B. J. Medical College, Ahmedabad Email ID : wardepriyanka2@gmail.com
Aims and Objective: To evaluate the outcomes of single stage surgery for patients with recto-vestibular fistula in past 10 years.
Materials and methods: This is a retrospective study of 182 patients who underwent single stage PSARP for RVF between January 2013 and December 2022 in patients of ages ranging between 2.5 months to 4 years. Anovestibular and rectovaginal fistula not included in this study. Investigations done included Ultrasound KUB, Xray LS spine and 2D Echo in all patients. Fistula washes were started as and when necessary and patient kept on liquid diet as and when required. After adequate bowel preparation, the patients under went PSARP. Postoperatively, patients were kept nil orally for 4 days, with indwelling catheter for 3 days. Anal calibration was started after 3 weeks and increased gradually as per the age. Outcomes was evaluated on stool pattern, anal caliber, cosmesis and complications.
Results: Mean age at surgery was 1.2 yrs (range 2.5 months-4 years).Out of 182 patients 42(23%) patients developed constipation which was managed with dietary modification and extended use of laxatives. 5(2.7%) patients developed anal stenosis who required dilatation for prolonged period upto 4-6months . There was perineal wound dehisence in 16 (8.7%) patients, out of which 12 patients required diverting stoma for complete dehiscence. Four patients with superficial wound gap could be managed conservatively. Redo PSARP done in 12 patients (6.4)% . Overall 164(90%) patients had a good cosmetic result.
Conclusion: Single stage surgery for RVF is technically feasible with good results in terms of continence and cosmesis in expert hands. Wound dehisence is due to late presentation with dilated rectum . Posterior sagital anorectoplasty is has made mobilization of rectum easy and less complication of prolapse Or stenosis.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 29G: Progress in technique and philosophy of oesophageal replacement in tracheo-oesophageal fistula with oesophageal atresia

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Garvita Singh, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Kriti Mittal, Clovey Singhal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : garvitasingh429@gmail.com
Introduction: We present our experience and modifications of oesophageal replacement in patients with trachea-oesophageal fistula (TEF) and oesophageal atresia.
Materials and Methods: A review of records was conducted for 22 patients (14 girls, 8 boys) who underwent oesophageal replacement between Feb 2016 and May 2023. Colonic replacement was performed in two patients, while gastric pull-up was performed in the remaining 20. Fifteen out of 20 patients of gastric pull up had a diagnosis of TEF-EA Salient features of complicated cases are discussed.
Cases details: 12 out of fifteen patients were long gap TEF-EA diverted soon after birth. Three patients underwent primary repair initially and required gastric pull up later on. First had major anastomotic leak and mediastinitis post primary repair requiring diversion. We did bronchoscopic cauterization of fistula followed by retrosternal gastric pull up later. Second patient was referred to us after diversion following the anatomostic leak. During intra op bronchoscopy, silk granuloma was found at carina, 4 silk suture pieces were removed bronchoscopically and granuloma was cauterized and gastric pull up was performed at a later stage. Third patient, also referred to us after diversion, was taken up for gastric pull up and was found to have a large pseudocavity in mediastinum. TEF ligation and cauterization of pseudocavity was done and is planned for gastric pull up.
A 7 month old female underwent initial diversion for long gap EA-TEF and then transhiatal gastric pull up whom we were unable to wean off from ventilatrory support. On further bronchoscopy and imaging, a persistent right lung collapse was there due to compression of trachea from abnormal course of right innominate artery.
A 3 months old female underwent fistula ligation, oesophagostomy and gastrostomy elsewhere. During the procedure bronchial transection had occurred which was repaired. She presented to us with hyperinflated right lung on X ray and complete occlusion of left bronchus on bronchoscopy with fistula at carina. We did a left posterolateral thoracotomy and left pneumonectomy. Gastric pull up was done later.
We would also like to discuss the basic techniques of diversion, role of pre op metoprolol and use of plastic sleeve to pull up the stomach. Thoracic gastrostomy in a wide gap EA-TEF is also an established alternative technique for feeding gastrostomy.
Conclusion: A multidisciplinary approach, guided by bronchoscopy, oesophagoscopy, and imaging, is crucial for individualized surgical planning and improved outcomes in complicated TEF-EA cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 57G: Complication and outcome laparoscopic-assisted anorectal pull through: A long-term study

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Farheen Rehana, Farheen Rehana

Department of Pediatric Surgery, JIPMER-Jawaharlal Institute Of Postgraduate Medical Education and Research, Pondicherry Email ID : farheenrehana1510@gmail.com
Introduction: There are different techniques available for the correction of Anorectal malformation, allwith their specific morbidity. By avoiding division of the striated muscle complex as in PSARP, Laparoscopic-assisted anorectal pull-through (LAARP) is also impacting and has shown promising results. It has a steep learning curve which is not without complications. We have discussed our experience in LAARP.
Methods: Retrospective study of ARM operated between May 2014 to July 2022.A total of thirty-five patients with a high or intermediate anorectal malformation underwent LAARP at our center. We have looked forthe time is taken, complications, and advantages during surgery were noted. The minimum follows up is six months, and the maximum iseight years.For the continence rate, we used Kelly’s Score.
Results: During the study period, thirty-two males and three females, with a mean age of 29 months were operated on. Out of 35 patients,32 underwent successful LAARP, and three converted to open. Early postoperative complications were one right-sided ureteric injury which was managed with a Boari flap; one urethral stricture, and another persistent recto bulbar fistula. So, around 10% of patients had an early complication. Among the late complications, five patients(15%) had mucosal prolapse, and three patients had anal stenosis. Late complications were managed without any significant morbidity. Out of these thirty patients, twenty-five patient have no constipation or soiling with good continence and 10 patient has occasional soiling of stool. In our series, most of the female patients, including three with rectovaginal fistula, have a high or intermediate ARM. More than Seventy present patients have good continence without bowel management. The mean duration of surgery was 250 minutes, which is high.
Conclusion: Refinements of the technique may prevent complications and improve outcomes in LARRP. It has the advantages of a shorter hospital stay, less analgesic requirement after surgery, and a more rapid return to normal activities with good long-term continence.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 38G: Corrosive-injuries of the upper gastrointestinal tract: Surgical management at a tertiary care centre

Category : “GASTRO INTESTINAL” - K. K. SHARMA AWARD PAPERS

Priya Mathew, Basant Kumar, Vijai D. Upadhyaya, Ankur Mandelia, Tarun Gupta, Pujana K.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Postgraduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drpriyamathew@gmail.com
Aim : Corrosive ingestion (accidental or intentional) poses significant problem. Substances with extremes of pH are very corrosive, create severe injury in the upper gastrointestinal tract. Early interventions are important to prevent progression of complications and long-term sequelae.
We present our experience of the surgical management of corrosive induced esophageal and gastric injuries over a period of 9 years.
Methods: Patients’ medical records were retrieved retrospectively from December 2014 to May 2023. Collected data included mode of injury, clinical presentations, operative procedures, complications, outcome, etc.
Results: Cohort includes 29 patients (M: F- 11:18) having isolated esophageal injury (9), isolated gastric/pyloric injury (11) both (9). Age ranged from 1 to 19 years (median 12 years). Post endoscopic perforations observed in 5 esophageal, 4 gastric strictures cases. Surgical procedures include Cervical esophagostomy (4), gastrostomy / jejunostomy (19), Billroth-I (3), antrectomy with Billroth I (4), Billroth II (1), antrectomy with Billroth II (5), Gastric pull-up (5), Colonic interposition (3). Complications includes FJ dislodgement (2), subacute bowel obstruction (2), wound infections (2), Transient dumping (1). Definitive procedures were performed in 21 patients. 5 patients are awaiting definitive surgery. 2 patients are lost to follow-up. 1 mortality due to jejunostomy tube perforation and sepsis. Rest are under follow-up and gained adequate weight. Mean follow-up is 4.5 years.
Conclusion: Surgery is the mainstay of management for corrosive-induced upper GI tract strictures (not amenable to endoscopic dilatation) or perforation. Good outcome is achieved by adapting appropriate surgical procedures according to extent, degree of stricture, patient’s general condition.

## Session - 14 : FREE PAPERS : “MISCELLANEOUS”: ABSTRACT NO. : 87M: Patient-specific three-dimensional printed models of aberrant anatomy for surgical planning of anorectal malformation

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Anjan Kumar Dhua, Prabudh Goel, Vishesh Jain, Devendra Kr. Yadav, Ajay Verma, Sachit Anand, Sandeep Agarwala

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : dhuaanjan@gmail.com
Objectives: To explore the impact of 3D models on the surgical decision change of 20 male patients with ARM and generate evidence on the utility of 3D printed models for planning definitive surgery for anorectal anomalies.
Methods: A prospective case-crossover study was conducted involving 20 male ARM patients (median age 12 months, IQR [Q3-Q1] = 14 - 8 months). Computed tomography was used to acquire and segment the 3D pelvis anatomy with contrast in the urinary and terminal bowel. The models were fabricated by additive manufacturing using Acrylonitrile Butadiene Styrene filaments, and the dimensions were compared with medical images. Decisions after evaluating routine colostograms were compared with those after inspection of the 3D model and intraoperative findings. A subjective satisfaction questionnaire was provided.
Results: 3D models accurately replicated the anatomy with a mean bias of -0.12 ±0.175 mm. In 18 of the 20 cases (90% of the cases, confidence interval 38.5–70.7%), the information provided by the 3D models did not change the surgical decision based on conventional imaging planning. Also, the surgical approach was maintained after evaluation of the 3D model in 95% (n-19) of the cases. 89% felt that the 3D models were a useful tool and could aid communication with other colleagues to plan the intervention, and 86% felt that they were useful to communicate with parents and improved their understanding of the disease and the surgery.34.3% of the consultants and 54.3% of the residents agreed that the 3D model was useful for the education of trainees.
Conclusions: 3D models are accurate replicas of the ARM anatomy. 3D models did not change the surgical decision in most cases (18 of 20 cases, 90% of cases). Residents and consultants were satisfied with the 3D models and found potential utility in planning, patient education, and training while managing ARM cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 97M: Sirolimus in treatment of Pediatric vascular malformations – Our experience

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Vinay Prasad M. H., Priyanka Hegde, Arun Kumar, Tarun John Jacob, Susan Jehangir

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : drvinayprasadmh@gmail.com
Background : Sclerotherapy and surgery form the mainstay of treatment of vascular malformations. However, in complex, large or diffuse lesions neither can serve as a feasible option. Sirolimus, an mTOR inhibitor is the newest proposed treatment modality for these refractory cases.
Aim: The aim of our study was to determine the suitability and response to sirolimus of different types of vascular malformations.
Materials and methods : Prospective observational study was conducted over the last two years in our institution. Sirolimus was administered orally according to the recommended dose of 0.8mg/m2 BSA to children with complex vascular malformations. Response to sirolimus was recorded as decrease in size or relief of symptoms.
Results: The study included 29 children with mean age of 5.7 years with the youngest child being just one month old. Majority of patients had slow flow malformation while three patients with fibroadipose vascular anomaly (FAVA) were also included. The maximum response of therapy occurred between 6 to 12 weeks of initiation of therapy. Near complete resolution was seen in children with purely lymphatic lesions. 86% of patients had received sclerotherapy prior to initiation of sirolimus, however none of them required a further session. 17% had no or minimal response and all of them had predominately venous malformation. One patient developed derangement of lipid profile. Remission leading to discontinuation or reduction of dosage was possible in three patients.
Conclusion: Sirolimus is useful in patients with slow flow malformations with maximum response seen in predominantly lymphatic malformations and is safe in pediatric age group.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 106M: Surgical challenges in pediatric and neonatal parathyroidectomy

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Deesha Shajil, Gayatri Munghate, Pradnya S. Bendre

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital for Children, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : dr.deesha.shajil@gmail.com
Background: Primary Hyperparathyroidism (PHPT) in children is extremely difficult to diagnose and treat, especially due to the minute size of the parathyroid glands. Neonatal severe hyperparathyroidism (NSHPT) is an even rarer (autosomal recessive) disorder of calcium homeostasis, requiring considerable surgical expertise for complete excision.
Aim: To present our experience with pediatric PHPT, highlighting the surgical difficulties
Methods: Retrospective review of children with PHPT who underwent parathyroidectomy at our institute between 1st January 2020 and 31st December 2022. Preoperative localization was done using a combination of ultrasound, Tc99-sestamibi parathyroid scan and a 4-D CT (Computed Tomography) scan. Intraoperative ultrasound, frozen section and serial PTH levels aided surgical localization.
Results: Four children with PHPT underwent parathyroidectomy - a 17-year-old boy with right inferior parathyroid adenoma who required a right hemithyroidectomy to remove an intrathyroidal parathyroid adenoma, a 15-year-old girl with parathyroid adenoma with hyperplasia requiring total parathyroidectomy, a 1-year-old boy with NSHPT who needed partial thymectomy to achieve a total parathyroidectomy and a 2-month-old girl with NSHPT who didn’t improve after total parathyroidectomy and required re-exploration and thyroidectomy for an intrathyroidal parathyroid. This baby expired of sepsis-related complications 1 month after the second surgery. The first 3 patients are surviving after a follow up of 14.33 ± 6.66 months.
Conclusion: Pediatric parathyroidectomy is highly challenging as preoperative localization is often inconclusive in children. Uncommon locations of parathyroids, such as intrathyroidal, intrathymic, etc., must also be considered, especially if PTH levels do not sufficiently fall intraoperatively after removal of the orthotopic parathyroids.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 118M: Impact of Informative Videos in Improving Proxy Consent by Parents for Pediatric Surgery: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Shreyas Dudhani, Bindey Kumar, Amit Kumar Sinha, Rashi Rashi, Amit Kumar, Gaurav Shandilya, Keerthana Bachagala

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : shreyas.dudhani45@gmail.com
Aims: A consent is considered truly informed only when the person giving the consent understands the advantages, disadvantages, risks and benefits, expected outcome and possible complications of the procedure. Studies conducted in adult surgeries have tried various interventions to improve consent with mixed results. However, similar studies with regard to proxy consent in Pediatric surgery are numbered.
Methods: A Two Arm, Parallel Design, Randomised Controlled Trial was conducted from March 2020 to March 2022 to analyse the effect of an educational video in comparison to an informational leaflet in parents giving proxy consents for various listed Pediatric surgical procedures. We expected an increase in preoperative knowledge and decrease in preoperative anxiety scores.
Results: Parents in the video group had a higher mean knowledge score [6.97 vs 6.77, p = 0.40], comparable satisfaction scores [27.45 v/s 27.58, p = 0.88] and statistically insignificant difference between anxiety scores. We noted highest levels of satisfaction were in the parents with the lowest educational status. Knowledge scores were lowest in Pelvi-Ureteric Junction Obstruction (5.1/10) patients.
Conclusions: Our study, done over a period of 2 years included a variety of diagnosis and the videos and information leaflets were self-designed. It showed no significant improvement in anxiety, knowledge or satisfaction in parents. Studies with more participants would be needed to take this research forward.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 94M: Feasibility and safety of Minimally Invasive Surgery in infants: A retrospective study

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Karthik S. A., Veerabhadra, Keshav Pavan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru Email ID : karthiksagowda@gmail.com
Aim: This study aimed to assess the feasibility and safety of minimally invasive surgery (MIS) in infants.
Methods: A retrospective study was conducted in the Department of Pediatric Surgery at tertiary care centre. The study included all infants (< 1 year) who underwent MIS between Jan 2015 and Dec 2022.
Results: A total of 110 infants were included in the study. The MIS procedures performed included Laparoscopic procedures (83) - 57 pyloromyotomy, 5 intra abdominal cyst excision, 6 orchidopexy, 1 herniotomy, 7 laparoscopic-assisted bowel pull-through, 4 laparoscopic-assisted ureterostomy, 2 Ladd’s procedure. Additionally, one laparoscopic procedure involving multiple biopsies for Hirschsprung’s disease was performed and thoracoscopic procedures (27)- 16 thoracoscopic repairs of congenital diaphragmatic hernia (CDH), 4 thoracoscopic plications of diaphragmatic eventration, 5 thoracoscopic cyst excision, and 2 video-assisted thoracoscopic surgery (VATS) for empyema.
Among the procedures, 2 eventration of diaphragm, 1 CDH and 1 Ladd’s procedure required conversion to open surgery, while the remaining procedures were successfully managed using MIS. Complications observed included two cases of postoperative leak managed through open procedures following laparoscopic pyloromyotomy, and two cases of pneumothorax managed conservatively following thoracoscopic cyst excision. One case of CDH repair had a recurrence and required open surgical intervention. There were four mortalities, including three cases of CDH and one case of pyloromyotomy.
Conclusion: Minimally invasive surgery is a feasible and safe approach for infants. However, careful consideration should be given to potential complications

## ABSTRACT NO. : 110M: A clinico-etiologic study of symptomatic umbilical remnants in infancy and childhood

Category : “MISCELLANEOUS”

Meshram Rohit Bhashkar, Rajiv Chadha, Subhasis Roy Choudhury, Chandra Vibhash, Pute Losu, Kanu Kapoor

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : rohitmesh2905@gmail.com
Aim: To study clinico-etiologic profile in children with symptomatic ‘umbilical remnants’ and efficacy of preoperative ultrasound (US) in diagnosing etiology and predicting surgical need.
Material and Methods: The study was a prospective, observational study from July 2021 to February 2023 in children 2 months to 12 years (n =65)with clinical findings suggesting presence of umbilical remnants. Clinical evaluation, abdomino-pelvic/ local US in all cases was performed. Umbilical granulomas(UG) and umbilical polyps were excised with suture-ligation/ electro-cauterization and Mini-laparotomy for symptomatic urachal /VID remnants, if diagnosed on clinical grounds / US correlation of US and operative findings performed.
Results: Etiologies were UG [n=38; 58.41 %]; umbilical Polyp (n= 17; 26.15 %), PVID (n= 3; 4.61%), umbilical-cyst (n= 1; 1.53 %); urachal-cysts (n=3; 4.61 %), patent-urachus (n= 3; 4.61 %). UG and polyps were distinguished histologically. Outpatient excision of the UG/ polyp was performed in 49 (75.38%),16(24.6 %) underwent mini-laparotomy. None had recurrence. In 7/ 16 (43.75 %) US findings correlated with operative findings. Of 13 patients undergoing surgery based on US findings, 6 were false positive .3 false negative consisted PVID. Sensitivity of US was 70 %, specificity 89 %, PPV 53 %,NPV 94 %. and diagnostic accuracy 84 %.
Conclusions: Majority of children had UG / umbilical polyp, in which outpatient excision was effective .Investigations like USG are unnecessary unless symptoms persist/recur .In PVID, diagnosis is clinical. In suspected urachal anomalies, US is an effective diagnostic modality. Histopathology should be performed for final etiologic diagnosis.

## Session - 16 : VIDEO SESSION : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”: ABSTRACT NO. : 245V: Difficult laparoscopic cholecystectomy in children: surgical challenges and management strategies

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

Rohit Kapoor, Ankur Mandelia, Prathibha B Naik, Naseera Koya, Tarun Gupta

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences (SGPGIMS), Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : rohit.kapoor13081988@gmail.com
Objectives: Factors contributing to a “difficult” laparoscopic cholecystectomy (LC) may be ductal or vascular anatomical variations, stone impacted in cystic duct, adhesions, cholecystitis, poor surgeon experience etc. We aim to discuss our experience with “difficult” LC in children, their surgical challenges and management strategies.
Methods: A retrospective review of electronic medical records and operative videos of all children undergoing LC in a single surgical unit over the past 7 years was performed. 4 patients were identified to have undergone a difficult LC owing to variant vascular anatomy (n=2), variant ductal anatomy (n=1) and stone impacted in the cystic duct (n=1).
Results: Case 1&2- During LC in 2 patients, we encountered a large anteriorly crossing right hepatic artery (RHA) in the Calot’s triangle with a very short cystic artery length. The cystic duct and artery were carefully dissected from the RHA, doubly clipped and divided. Case 3- a 10 year old girl with symptomatic cholelithiasis and suspected choledocholithiasis was identified to have an aberrant segment V duct joining the junction of cystic duct and common hepatic duct (CHD) on pre-operative MRCP. LC was performed successfully by the “fundus first” approach. An intra-op cholangiogram (IOC) was performed which confirmed the aberrant anatomy. Case 4- During LC in a 17 year old girl, we encountered dense adhesions at Calot’s triangle due to a large stone impacted in the cystic duct. The stone was disimpacted and held above the Calot’s triangle by an endoloop passed around the neck of gallbladder. All cases were successfully completed laparoscopically by meticulous dissection following all the principles of ‘critical view of safety’. There were no major intra-op or post-op complications. Median operating time was 158 (130 – 190) mins and length of hospital stay was 2 (2-4) days. After a median follow up of 15 months, all patients are asymptomatic.
Conclusions: Difficult laparoscopic cholecystectomy is often associated with serious complications and a high conversion rate. No single technique can manage all cases of difficult LC due to the diversity of factors influencing LC. Meticulous surgical technique, subtotal cholecystectomy, antegrade or fundus first approach, per-operative cholangiogram are some of the recommendations to significantly reduced the complications and conversion rate.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 246V: Type VII biliary ductal anatomical variation in a case of congenital choledochal malformation: Video demonstration of robotic surgical management with the aid of ICG

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

Priya Mathew, Ankur Mandelia, Basant Kumar, Vijai D. Upadhyaya, Prathibha B. Naik, Naseera Koya, Pujana K.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences (SGPGIMS), Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drpriyamathew@gmail.com
Objectives: Biliary ductal anatomical variations are common and pose a technical challenge for the minimally invasive treatment of congenital choledochal malformations (CCM). Type VII variations are unclassified and extremely rare, as in our case where the right posterior sectoral duct (RPSD) had a very low insertion into the common bile duct (CBD). We aim to demonstrate the surgical technique of biliary reconstruction of this aberrant anatomy in a case of CCM with the surgical robot aided with indocyanine green (ICG) fluorescence imaging.
Methods: A 6-year-old girl presented to us with recurrent episodes of pancreatitis for the past 2 years. USG and MRCP revealed a Type 1 fusiform CCM with an abnormally long pancrato-biliary malunion (PBMU) with RPSD inserting low into the CBD (Type VII ductal variation). The patient was planned for robotic excision of CCM with Roux-en-Y hepatico-jejunostomy (HJ). ICG was given 4 hours before induction of anaesthesia in a dose of 0.5 mg/kg to aid in the identification of biliary anatomy.
Results: 4 robotic ports and 1 assistant port were used with the daVinci Xi robotic system. The dilated choledochus with the aberrant duct were dissected together from the portal vein and hepatic artery. ICG fluorescence imaging (Firefly) was used throughout to delineate the biliary anatomy. The choledochus was dissected distally in the intra-pancreatic portion till it tapered where it was clipped and divided. On opening the distal CBD, insertion of the aberrant RPSD was identified and cannulated with a stent. Calot’s triangle dissection was then done separating the cystic duct from the aberrant RPSD. Common hepatic duct (CHD) and RPSD were then transected at an appropriate level for HJ. Ductoplasty was then performed with 5-0 polydiaxanone between CHD and RPSD to achieve a single ostia. Intra-corporeal jejuno-jejunostomy was performed with an endo-GIA stapler. Roux limb was brought to hepatic hilum in a retro-colic fashion and a wide single-ostia HJ was performed with 5-0 polydiaxanone in an interrupted fashion. Post-operative course was uneventful and the patient is asymptomatic at follow-up with normal LFT’s and imaging.
Conclusions: In cases of CCM with ductal anatomical variations, the surgical robot and ICG fluorescence aid in precise dissection and delineation of biliary anatomy with appropriate reconstruction of aberrant ducts and avoiding serious intra and postoperative complications.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 247V: Windows in the Bladder- Minivesicostomies for Transvesicoscopic Ureteral Reimplant

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

Khyati Kiran Janapareddy, K. R. Satyanarayana, VVS Chandrasekharam

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Ankura Hospitals for Women and Children, Hyderabad, Telangana Email ID : khyati.kiranj@gmail.com
Introduction : Transvesicoscopic Ureteral Reimplant(TVUR) is an effective cure for primary reflux but is infamous for its uphill learning curve, prolonged duration, and significant rates of conversion. This is partly attributed to the difficulty in port placement and frequent trocar displacement. Guided by our experience with nearly 400 ureteral reimplants, we present a novel technique of “ temporary Minivesicostomy” for port placement for TVUR.
Technique: Patient is placed in dorsal lithotomy. Bladder is partially distended with saline. Port sites are marked. A 4/7 Clutton urethral dilator is introduced through the urethra to tent the bladder at the port site. Layers of the abdomen are dissected sequentially till the bladder wall pops through the incision. The bladder(full thickness) is hitched to the anterior abdomen wall. The tented bladder wall opened to visualise mucosal margin all around the circumference of the incision. 4 sutures secure the everted bladder wall to the incised skin. Trocar is rail-roaded onto the dilator and position is confirmed with egress of saline through the port. Trocars are fixed at the port site. Closure is achieved by sequentially releasing the vesicocutaneous hitch stitches and closing the bladder incision with a purse- string followed by anatomical layer by layer closure of abdomen wall.
Conclusion: Minivesicostomy for TVUR is a simple, safe, effective, reproducible technique which ensures reliable access for transvesicoscopic procedures with minimal trocar displacement. This procedure also enables closure in anatomical layers hence allowing reliable bladder healing and early removal of catheter and discharge.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 248V: Seeing is Believing-The Confirmatory Intraoperative sign of PUJO

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

M. P. Divakar, Rengarajan, S. Prabakaran, V. Muthulingam, L. Senthil Kumar, K. Srinivasan, S. Jayaprakash

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Coimbatore Medical College Hospital, Coimbatore, Tamilnadu Email ID : alphagery@gmail.com

Aims: With this Video Presentation we attempt to Supplement The diagnosis of PUJO Intraopertively. Just like a Dynamic Renal Isotope Scan, we Demonstrate a Physiologically viewable Pathological sign of Pujo rather than the usual visual Confirmation by a Narrowed Pujo.
Method: A ten Year old Male child presented with Left Loin Pain was diagnosed as Left Pujo (APD 4cm) with Renal Function of 36%.The Child Underwent Laparoscopic Left Pyeloplasty with DJ Stenting. Intraoperatively there was a demonstrable Movement of the Renal Pelvis struggling against the Pujo. This was Entirely Intrinsic and not caused by the administration of Diuretics or by External Transmitted Movement. Post Operatively the Child is doing well with DT stent being removed after 4 week. The APD has come down to 1.7cm at three month Follow up.
Result & conclusion: The Management of Pediatric Urological cases Especially Pujo varies according to the Institution . What we have attempted to Demonstrate in this single case is a Supplement to the existing Evidence Based Medicine Protocols. As this is a Digital Era We put forward this Digital Pelvic Dance Sign.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 249V: Antenatal diagnosis of congenital megalourethra & its postnatal management

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

Abhinav Sihag, Mohammed Jaseel, Ramesh Babu

Department of Pediatric Urology, Sri Ramachandra Institute of Higher Education and Research (SRIHER), Chennai Email ID : sihagabhinav9@gmail.com
Aims: Congenital megalourethra is a rare anomaly characterized by a cystic dilatation and elongation of the penile urethra resulting from the absence and hypoplasia of the corpus spongiosum and corpus cavernosum, or anterior urethral valve. Antenatal diagnosis is rare. The aims are to report antenatal diagnosis & present a video of its post natal management.
Materials and methods: The fetus of a 36 year old gravida-2 mother at 32 weeks fetal sonograms was found to have bilateral hydroureteronephrosis and dilated urethra involving entire phallus. The child underwent laying open of the megalo urethra with excision of obstructing distal valve like fold at the meatus. At one and half years of age, megalourethra repair was performed.
Results: The post-operative period was uneventful and the child passed urine in good stream without any fistula. The child also had a left multicystic dysplastic kidney with grade 5 VUR/ diverticulum for which a laparoscopic nephrectomy was performed.
Conclusion: We report this case because of the rarity of the disease and to highlight the steps of primary reduction urethroplasty. Urethral tapering, tubularisation, and spongiosus cover with double breasting of dartos tissue are essential steps in megalourethra repair.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 250V: Laparoscopic management of Hydrosalpinx in children

Category : “VIDEO PAPER PRESENTATION”

Abhinav Sihag, Lakshmi Sundararajan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust hospital, Chennai Email ID : sihagabhinav9@gmail.com
Introduction: Hydrosalpinx is a rare condition in premenarchal children which can present with nonspecific abdominal pain due to fallopian tube torsion or ovarian torsion. Hydrosalpinx induced fallopian tube torsion is difficult to diagnose and needs immediate surgical intervention.
Case presentation: We report 2 cases of premenarchal girls (age 13 years and 12 years respectively) who presented with acute abdominal pain with nausea and vomiting. On ultrasound found to have a tubular cystic structure in right adnexa suggestive of hydrosalpinx. In one case, imaging showed normal ovaries on either side while in other case, it was associated with features of ipsilateral ovarian torsion as well. On Laparoscopy, both the patients had enlarged and ischemic right fallopian tube twisted along its own axis with hemorrhagic fluid in it. Laparoscopic detorsion and salpingectomy was done in one patient while laparoscopic detorsion, aspiration and open salpingectomy was done for other patient preserving both the ovaries. Histopathology confirmed the diagnosis of hydrosalpinx with features of torsion. Follow up period was uneventful with normal ovaries in imaging.
Conclusion: A high index of suspicion is necessary for early diagnosis and management of fallopian tube torsion in cases of hydrosalpinx to prevent complications like severe infection, peritonitis and for fertility preservation. Laparoscopy is the gold standard for the diagnosis and management of this condition with good outcome.

## Sunday, 5th November 2023: Session - 18 : FREE PAPERS : “M.I.S.”: ABSTRACT NO. : 75L: Physiological and Anaesthetic Considerations of Safe and Optimal Pneumoperitoneal Pressures for Laparoscopic Surgery in Children

Category : M.I.S.

Ankur Mandelia, Preeti Joon, Sanjay Dhiraaj, Tapas Kumar, Chetna Shamsherey

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences (SGPGIMS), Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drankurmandelia@gmail.com
Aims: In this study we aimed to determine the safe and optimal pneumoperitoneal (PP) pressures for laparoscopic surgery in children aged 1-5 years, along with the technical ease for the surgeon at different PP.
Material and methods: This was a prospective, randomised, single-blinded study conducted by departments of anaesthesiology and pediatric surgery. Children aged 1-5 years were randomised into Group I (n=24): PP = 6-8 mmHg and Group II (n=24): PP = 9-10 mmHg. Hemodynamic, ventilatory and blood gas changes were measured before CO2 insufflation (T0), 20 mins after insufflation (T1), before desufflation (T2) and 10 mins after desufflation (T3). Surgeon’s technical ease of surgery, postoperative pain, requirement of rescue analgesia, time to resume feeding and complications were recorded and analysed.Results: There was significant difference in PaCO2 values between Group I and Group II at T1 (43.42±34.19 vs 46.45±2.18; p < 0.005), T2 (38.52±2.44 vs 40.17±2.52; p< 0.05) and T3 (36.28±2.71 vs 37.9±1.41; p<0.05) respectively. There was a significant difference in the EtCO2 at the T1 time point (41.08±3.3 vs 44.38±2.37; p<0.005). The number of times ventilatory settings were changed in Group II was 7.12 ± 1.7 which was significantly higher as compared to Group I (4.42 ± 1.79) (p <.0001). Postoperative pain scores were higher in Group II at 1,6 and 12hrs, requiring rescue analgesia. Surgeon’s scores and haemodynamics were similar in both the groups.
Conclusions: Higher PP in Group II caused significant changes in PaCO2, EtCO2, and postoperative pain requiring rescue analgesia but blood gas changes were clinically insignificant and there were no significant changes in hemodynamic parameters. Since the surgeon’s ease of performing surgery was similar in both the groups, we recommend that laparoscopy in children aged 1-5 years can be started with lower pneumoperitoneal pressures of 6-8 mm Hg which can be increased if needed based on surgeon’s comfort and the patient’s body habitus.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 78L: Does laparoscopic pyeloplasty in infancy effect the development of a child?

Category : M.I.S.

Khyati Kiran Janapareddy, K R Satyanarayana, VVS Chandrasekharam

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Ankura Hospitals for Women and Children, Hyderabad, Telangana Email ID : khyati.kiranj@gmail.com
Purpose : Exposure to general anesthesia in early childhood may have neurotoxic effects on the developing brain. In addition, laparoscopy may alter cerebral oxygenation, thus may affect the future brain development . In the present study, we analysed the neuro developmental outcome of children > 1year of age who underwent Laparoscopic Pyeloplasty (LP) as infants.
Patients and Methods: Children who had LP as infants were assessed for neuro developmental outcome at 1-5 years of age using the Ages and Stages Questionnaires (ASQ-3). Children who were preterm, LBW, suffered birth asphyxia, received NICU care, bilateral PUJO, associated urological anomalies like VUR, etc and other major systemic anomalies were excluded. Development was considered delayed when the child scored less than the cut off in at-least 1 domain. The results were compared with published studies on developmental delay in our general population.
Results: 77 children (71 boys) who underwent LP in infancy were assessed at follow-up. The mean age at LP was 4.3 (1-8) months, while the mean follow-up age at developmental assessment was 38 months (12-60). Eight of 77 children (10.4%) were found to have developmental delay requiring intervention. The delay was in Fine motor( 8.5%), Personal Social( 2.7%) and Problem Solving domains(1.4%). A review of published literature revealed that the prevalence of developmental delay in our general population was 10%(2.5-19%).
Conclusion: In this study, the incidence of developmental delay in infants undergoing LP was comparable to published studies on developmental delay in our general population. Most affected children had delay in fine motor domain.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 79L: Outcome analysis of single stage orchidopexy vs staged orchidopexy in children with non-palpable testis over a 10-year period-a single center study

Category : M.I.S.

Pydi Chaitanya, Gowrishankar, Narendra Babu M, S. Ramesh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : pchaitanya.91@gmail.com
Aim: To analyze and compare treatment outcomes between single stage vs staged Stephen Fowler orchidopexyin children with nonpalpable testis (NPT).
Methodology: Retrospective study over 10years (January-2013 – December-2023) analyzing outcomes of laparoscopic intervention for NPT. All cases grouped into unilateral or bilateral NPT and further into single stage and staged orchidopexy procedures and analyzed. Cases with palpable testis under anesthesia, atrophic/vanishing-testis or defaulted follow-up were excluded. Follow up was at 3 months, 6 months, and annually thereafter. Follow-up ranged from 6months to 10years with mean period of 52 months. Parameters assessed were size, consistency and location of testis. Overall findings classified as good, fair or poor outcomes. Outcomes compared between 2 groups children with unilateral and bilateral NPT and between single and staged orchidopexy.
Results: Of 273 children 223 were unilateral, of which 134 were left sided (60.08%). 50 had bilateral NPT (100 units) with age 1-5years (45.78%). Out of all 323testicular units(TUs), after exclusions276(TUs)were included. 22 children had syndromic associations. For 41 TUssingle stage orchidopexy was done, while in 191 TUs staged Stephen Fowler orchidopexy was performed. Success rates were as follows – 87.4%(130/159) in unilateral NPT, 69% (52/73) in bilateral NPT, 75%(31/41) in single staged orchidopexy and84% (160/191) in staged Stephen Fowler orchidopexy.
Conclusion: In our experience, Staged Stephen-Fowler orchidopexy had better results when compared to single stage orchidopexy (84% vs75%). Children with unilateral NPT had better results compared to bilateral NPT (87.4% vs 69%). Overall, children with syndromic associations had poor outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 80L: Single port laparoscopic hernia repair in girls - How I do it

Aim : To demonstrate the technique of single port laparoscopic hernia repair in girls.

Results : We have operated upon 32 girls for single port laparoscopic hernia repair over a period of 6 months from June 2022 to December 2022. The mean age was 2.8 + 0.76 years. ## ABSTRACT NO. : 76L: Laparoscopic Adrenalectomy for Pheochromocytoma in pediatric population

Category : M.I.S.

Ashwin Rajkumar J., Prakash Agarwal, Madhu R, Jegadeesh, Selvapriya

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sri Ramachandra Institute of Higher Education and Research, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : ashwindoc1994@gmail.com
Aim: Pheochromocytomas in pediatric population are rare neuroendocrine tumours that secrete catecholamines. Although a rare cause of secondary hypertension in children, hypertension is characteristic and surgical treatment is definitive.
Method and results: We report single institute two cases of Pheochromocytoma operated and managed via minimally invasive laparoscopic resection.
Patients presented with complaints of palpitations, excessive sweating, intermittent headache and blindness in one of them. On evaluation blood pressure and 24-hour Urinary VMA were characteristically elevated. Following which proceeded with Radiological imaging with contrast enhanced computed tomography and Ga68 Dotate Scan showed features suggestive of Suprarenal mass highlighting a functional Pheochromocytoma.
Preoperative Blood pressure was stabilized using alpha blockers, beta blockers and proceeded with Laparoscopic Resection. Intraoperative Blood pressures were controlled with Sodium Nitroprusside. Specimen was retrieved through in house endobag through the umbilical port. Post operatively blood pressures were monitored and antihypertensives were tapered accordingly.
Conclusion: Laparoscopic surgical excision can be accomplished with rapid recovery time, low surgical morbidity and good oncological outcomes in selected patients. Preoperative evaluation and optimization of Blood pressure should be given importance to plan for the surgical approach to prevent intraoperative complications. Multidisciplinary team approach allows optimal health care delivery to these pediatric population.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 77L: Retrocaecal appendix - A challenge for robotic mitrofanoff and augmentation ileocystoplasty

Category : M.I.S.

Anwesa Chakraborty, Priyank Yadav, M S Ansari

Division of Pediatric Urology, Dept. of Urology & Renal Transplantation, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : anwesa1989@gmail.com
Introduction : Mitrofanoff appendicovesicostomy assists children in bladder emptying. A retrocaecal appendix poses many technical challenges. We here in share our technique and approach to overcome such challenges in a child with spinal dysraphism needing both Mitrofanoff and augmentation.
Materials and method: A 7-year-old boy, a known cases of spinal dysraphism had storage lower urinary tract symptoms. The bladder capacity was < 65% for age with a compliance 10 cm/H2O. The appendix was located in retrocaecal position and couldn’t reach the bladder template in spite of generous pedicle mobilization. To overcome this bladder was mobilized and hitched to psoas. Appendix was implanted along the left anterolateral wall and the bowel patch was anastomosed along the right anterolateral wall. The da Vinci Xi system was used.
Results: The operative time was 310 minutes (excluding docking) with blood loss of 80 ml. The hospitalization time was 7 days. No intra or postoperative complications noted. On follow up the bladder capacity improved (>90% for age) and symptoms improved as per the criteria laid down by international children continence society.
Conclusion: Retrocecal appendix poses technical challenges. Bladder hitch to PSOAS makes it simple to bridge the gap between high lying retrocaecal appendix with short pedicle. Both appendix and bowel can be easily implanted and anastomosed respectively to the bladder template.
