## ABSTRACT NO. : 62H: Serum Matrix metalloproteinase-7 as a diagnostic and prognostic biomarker for extrahepatic biliary atresia

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Chiranjiv Kumar, Shasanka Shekhar Panda, Simmi K Ratan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : chiranjivkumar5@gmail.com

Aims:

To evaluate the role of serum MMP-7 in differentiating neonatal hepatitis from EHBA
To correlate serum MMP-7 levels in infants with EHBA with the degree of liver damage upon histopathology
To evaluate relative utility of serum MMP-7 levels in predicting severity of liver damage vis-a-vis APRI and FIB-4 scores.

Methods: Consecutive infants less than 6 months of age with obstructive jaundice (defined as a serum direct bilirubin>1 mg/dl and 20% of total bilirubin) presented to the Pediatric Surgery OPD (n=20) at MAMC from April 2021 to March 2023 were enrolled. Age and sex-matched controls (n=20) were also taken. Serum MMP-7 (using ELISA Kits) and Serum GGT were tested in all 40 infants. In Case group, additional tests performed were, CBC, LFT to derive APRI and FIB-4 scores, Ultrasound Abdomen, Hepatobiliary scintigraphy, Per-operative cholangiogram, Pre and/or intraoperative liver biopsy. Staging of fibrosis and activity assessment were done using the METAVIR and Ishak’s score.
Results: The participants were substratified into EHBA (n = 11) and NH (n = 9) based on postpriming hepatobiliary scintigraphy and POC. Median serum MMP-7 level was 23 ng/mL in EHBA, 4 ng/mL in NH and 1.8 ng/mL in normal infants was 1.8 ng/mL. At a serum cut-off off of 4.99 ng/ml, nearly 100 % of patients could be correctly classified as EHBA, with a sensitivity of cent percent and a specificity of 67% with standard error of 0.1(p=0.012). From among the Metavir & Ishak fibrosis group which did not have any fibrosis, 33.3% had MMP-7 >4.99, whereas, cent percent from among early and advanced stages had MMP-7 >4.99 (P value ~ 0.000). Mean plot of APRI and FIB-4 score versus serum MMP-7 showed distribution around the middle reference line.
Conclusion: The high concentrations of MMP-7 in EHBA may be used to substratify patients of neonatal cholestasis into EHBA and NH. The strong correlation of MMP-7 with histopathological injury has also suggested that MMP-7 may be used as a prognostic biomarker of liver fibrosis in EHBA.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 67H: Liver resections in children: Our outcomes & experience from a single surgical center

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Reya Rachel Abraham, Punam Singh, MM Zameer, Vinay C, Sanjay Rao, Ashley D’ Cruz

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health City, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : reya.r.abraham@gmail.com
Introduction: Liver resection, the surgical removal of a portion of the liver, is a complex procedure commonly performed in children for various indications. This abstract provides a comprehensive review of the outcomes of liver resections in children, encompassing the surgical techniques, indications, complications, and long-term outcomes in single-center experience
Aims: The primary objective of this study is to evaluate the outcomes of liver resections in pediatric patients,

To study the perioperative complications: immediate and long term
To identify factors influencing surgical outcomes, such as age, underlying liver disease, tumor characteristics and tumor-free margin, and surgical approach.
To look at long-term survival rates/disease-free condition

Materials and methods : Between January 2015 and January 2023, children admitted to our hospital for benign or malignant liver masses and contemplated curative liver resection were retrospectively analyzed. The data were collected from medical records at our center. All children undergoing pediatric liver resections were included in the study. (We excluded patients with hepatic resection followed by liver transplants, and those patients with missing data). Due to its retrospective nature, informed consent was waived.
All patients underwent routine preoperative assessment. Preoperative diagnosis and etiology were based on Doppler ultrasound, triple-phase enhanced computed tomography angiography or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), biopsy.
Results: Between January 2015 and January 2023, children with various liver diseases requiring liver resections were included in this retrospective study. A total of sixteen children were enrolled in this study. The study included 11 males and 5 females. The median age at operation was 3 years (range- 2 months-10 years). Of the 16 children, 14 children had primary malignant liver tumors [12 hepatoblastoma, 1 embryonal Sarcoma, 1 malignant hepatoblastoma of liver]. The other two children had benign tumors: mesenchymal hamartoma and hemangioma of liver. Hepatoblastomas were staged according to PRETEXT staging: 4 patients with PRETEXT III and 4 Patients with PRETEXT II staging and required pre and post-resection chemotherapy with SIOPEL 3 High risk protocol. Prechemotherapy and post-chemotherapy TRIPHASIC CT done for all 13 patients with hepatoblastoma showed a marked reduction in size of the tumor.
The resections performed were central hepatectomy (2), right extended hepatectomy (7), right hepatectomy (6), and Left lobectomy (1) .
Complications associated with liver resections in children included sepsis in 6 patients which responded well to upgradation of antibiotics, biliary leak requiring pigtail drainage in 1 patient, and recurrence of the tumor in 1 child, requiring post op chemotherapy. Three children required postoperative blood transfusions.
Of the 14 malignant tumors, clear margins were obtained in 13 patients. One child had positive liver margins for which she underwent 2 cycles of chemotherapy. She also had pulmonary metastasis, underwent thoracotomy and pulmonary metastasis excision. Post which child has been well on follow up.
There was no perioperative or post-operative mortality. Multidisciplinary approaches involving pediatric surgeons, hepato-gastroenterologists, radiologists, and oncologists have also contributed to good post-operative outcomes.
Conclusion: In conclusion, liver resections in children are associated with favorable outcomes, particularly when performed by experienced pediatric surgical teams in specialized centers. Perioperative management continue to improve overall health and minimize complications and optimize the outcomes of liver resections in pediatric patients. The outcomes:1) There were no perioperative or post operative mortality
2) Thirteen resections for malignancy had negative margins. The one child with positive tumor margins undergone further chemotherapy after excision of pulmonary metastasis excision.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 70H: Analysis of prevalence of different practices and creating consensus regarding postoperative management of EHBA after Kasai portoenterostomy – A Delphi method

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Palak, Shailesh Solanki, Ravi P Kanojia

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Siddhartha Medical College, Vijayawada, Andhra Pradesh Email ID : vutukuru.sravanthi@gmail.com
Background: Extrahepatic biliary atresia (EHBA) is seen in infants with an incidence of 1 in 15,000 live births. They present with persistent jaundice and clay-colored stool since birth. Kasai portoenterostomy (KPE) is the commonly performed surgical procedure in these patients. It buys time for liver transplantation. However, cholangitis is a dreadful complication post KPE and is associated with fibrosis and poor long-term outcome. Postoperatively, phenobarbitone and ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) are given to improve the bile drainage and steroids are given to prevent inflammation and fibrosis. However, a definitive protocol regarding the drugs, dosage and duration is lacking.
Aim: To know the prevailing postoperative management of EHBA by subject experts and using Delphi process to know if the experts want to change their practice based on the results from the first survey.
Methodology: A questionnaire was made after discussing with two experts in the field of EHBA. The questionnaire (Google form) was mailed to 25 subject experts in the field. Reminders were sent 2-4 weeks later. The first survey data was analyzed and was sent to the responders. In the second survey, change in the management based on the results from the first survey was assessed.
Results: The first Delphi questionnaire was answered by 17 pediatric surgeons. Post-operatively, prophylactic antibiotic was prescribed for 6-12 weeks by around 40%, >12 weeks by 30%. Phenobarbitone was prescribed for less than 3 months by nearly 50%. UDCA is prescribed for a period of less than or equal to 3months by almost 40% and less than or equal to 6m to 1 year by 20% each. Nearly 50% prescribed steroids, preferably Prednisolone, postoperatively to reduce the inflammation, facilitate bilio-enteric drainage and reduce fibrosis. Interestingly, only a third of them have prescribed steroids for a duration of 6-12weeks. In children who are CMV IgM positive, around 60% prescribe antivirals drug, usually ganciclovir. Liver function test (LFT) and HIDA scan was repeated after 1 month by the majority, to monitor the progression of liver disease and the failure of KPE. In our survey, 50% are doing 5-10 KPE per year, and 15% are doing 10-15 and >15. From the second survey, it was noted that a majority of responders dint want to change their practice. None of them wanted to change when LFT or HIDA was done postoperatively.
Conclusion: From our survey using Delphi process, an overview on the postoperative, management of EHBA could be made. However, multi centric studies are required for uniform protocol on the post-operative management of EHBA.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 63H: Progressive Familial Intrahepatic Cholestasis (PFIC) - Our journey so far

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Tarun Gupta, Basant Kumar, Vijai D. Upadhyaya, Ankur Mandelia, Priya Mathews, Pujana K

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : tarun.gupta245@gmail.com
Background: Partial biliary diversion by either internal or external method is a safe method to ameliorate pruritus and improve quality of life in patients with PFIC.
The Aim of the study is to share our experience in management of Progressive familial intrahepatic cholestasis (PFIC).
Methods: From January 2018 to March 2023, 10 patients with PFIC underwent Partial biliary diversion, of which 5 (50%) underwent external and 5 (50%) underwent internal diversion.
Results: The 10 biliary diversions included 5 external partial biliary diversions (cholecysto-jejuno-cutaneostomy) and 5 internal diversions (cholecystojejunocolic anastomosis). Median age was 3.5 years and two patients were female. The relief from pruritus was better in externally diverted than internally diverted patients but had compliance issues to the stoma whereas internal diversion had better patient acceptability.
Conclusion: Partial biliary diversions (external/ internal) are safe methods to relieve pruritus and improve quality of life in patients depending upon the type of PFIC.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 68H: Choledochal Cyst converting into EHBA

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Varsha Verma, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Garvita Singh, Chanrika Kalagotla, Kriti Mittal, Shubham, Clovey Singhal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Gangaram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : varsha_verma18@yahoo.com
Introduction: Cystic biliary atresia is a well known entity. However, a choledochal cyst with confirmed intrahepatic ductal patency later converting into a biliary atresia is rare.
Materials and Methods: We present a case of antenatally diagnosed large choledochal cyst with confirmed postnatal patency of intrahepatic bile ducts (on MRCP) converting into a biliary atresia.
Cases details: A 40 days old female with antaenatally diagnosed cyst at porta hepatis and normal liver function was investigated further. MRCP revealed a large choledochal cyst with normal intrahepatic radicles. The child developed jaundice over the next 2 weeks with conjugated hyperbilirubinemia. At laparotomy operative cholangiogram revealed a large rounded cystic structure at the porta containing white bile. Gall bladder was distended, there was no communication with intrahepatic ducts or with the duodenum. Liver was cirrhotic with surface vascularity. Extra hepatic biliary apparatus was found atretic above and below the cyst. Around the cyst there were several lymph nodes. Cyst was excised along with the gall bladder and portoenterostomy performed. No flow was obtained on table. Child had repeated episodes of cholangitis and ascites, she is likely to require liver transplant.
Conclusion: Infantile choledochal cyst can progress into biliary atresia because of probable viral infection and resulting inflammation.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 73H: Choledochal Cyst : Our Perspective and Experience

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Surendra Singh, Rajeev Redkar, Rhul Deo Sharma, Sushma, Sonia Thakur

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lilavati Hospital and Research Centre, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : drsurendrasinghkgmu@gmail.com
Aim: To present Our Perspective and Experience of choledochal cyst.
Materials & Method: A retrospective observational study included 40 patients with choledochal cysts The surgeries were done using a standardized technique. A comprehensive analysis including patient demographics, symptoms at presentation, preoperative radio imaging, past treatment, operative outcome and complications was done. Follow up period ranged from 13 months to 252 months.
Results: Among 40 patients (24 females and 16 males), the female to male ratio is 1.5:1. Median age is 3.75 years (range -2 months to 15 years). Two ChCs were detected antenatally (n=2), Two ChCs were detected incidentally (n=3), The classical triad of symptoms of abdominal pain, jaundice and mass was detected only in 2.5%(n=1), rest of the other symptoms were individually noted. A transabdominal ultrasound was done in all the cases, USG was supportive in diagnosing ChC in 15% (n= 6), with no further imaging done. Of the remaining 34 cases, Magnetic Resonance cholangiopancreaticogram (MRCP) was used in adjunct to USG in 72% (n= 29), Computed Tomography (CT scan) was done in 12 % (n=5) to confirm the type of ChC. Hepatobiliary iminodiacetic acid scan (HIDA) was performed in 10 % (n=4) cases postoperatively. Eighty % (n=32) had associated conditions with ChC. Majority of the cases were Type 1 (61%). One case of Type VI B ChC is also reported in this series. The complication rate was 25% (n= 10/40 cases).
Conclusion:We conclude that our experience with choledochal cyst excision offers good surgical results but with significant complication rate. Newer variant of Type VI B ChC is seen in our experience. All the associated anomalies have to be looked and needs to be managed accordingly.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 71H: Scratch that Itch: Partial Internal Biliary Diversion for intractable pruritis in Alagille Syndrome

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Palak Singhai, Nitin James Peters, Jai Kumar Mahajan, Sadhana Bhasin Lal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute of Medical Education and Research, Chandigarh Email ID : palaksinghai17@gmail.com
Background: Alagille syndrome (AS) is a genetic condition characterized by cholestasis and jaundice. These children may present with severe debilitating pruritus refractory to medical management. Though external biliary diversion and transplantation have been described for treating intractable pruritis, limited evidence exists for partial internal biliary diversion (PIBD). We aim to study the effectiveness of PIBD in treating intractable pruritis in patients with AS.
Methods: PIBD was performed on patients suffering from AS with refractory pruritis despite maximal medical management at a tertiary care center in North India between 2022 and 2023. Pruritus was graded as follows: 0 = none, 1 = mild scratching/minimal, 2 = active scratching/moderate, 3 = abrasions/disfiguring, and 4 = mutilation/disabling. Pruritis scores and liver function tests were compared before and after surgery.
Results: Three children at a mean age of 6 years (4 to 8 years) underwent PIBD during the study period. One patient underwent cholecysto-colostomy, and two underwent cholecysto-jejuno-colostomy. The mean follow-up was ten months (5-14 months). Pruritus score decreased from 3.66 (3-4) to 0.66 (0-1). Post-operatively, all patients were free from oral medications for pruritus. There was no significant difference in liver function tests after surgery. There were no complications like an anastomotic leak, stricture, or cholangitis.
Conclusions: PIBD effectively reduces symptomatic pruritis in patients with AS but may not alter the course of the disease. Unlike external diversion, it avoids the morbidity of a stoma, dehydration, and electrolyte imbalances. PIBD in children with cholestasis is safe, effective for decreasing pruritis, and a technically feasible procedure.

## Session - 22 : FREE PAPERS : “THORACIC”: ABSTRACT NO. : 166T: Foreign Body Aspiration - A pediatric airway crisis

Category : THORACIC

Ankita, Abhishek Ranjan, Abhishek Kumar Singh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Rajendra Institute of Medical Sciences, Ranchi, Jarkhand Email ID : ankitag.26.12@gmail.com
Aims : This study describes the clinical presentation, relevant investigations, and treatment of foreign body aspiration (foreign body lodged in bronchus) in children and to formulate an acceptable line of management.
Methods : The records of children admitted to pediatric surgery ward of a tertiary care centre who had a history of foreign body aspiration over 2 years were reviewed. Symptoms, radiological findings, and bronchoscopic findings were assessed and compiled.
Results : Foreign bodies were detected in 54 children admitted. Children below three years of age were found to be the most vulnerable. The majority of the patients were boys. The most common objects were Bengal gram (37%) followed by peanuts(24%). Symptoms mainly included respiratory distress and coughing in 87% of the case. The most frequent radiological findings observed were localized air trapping, followed by atelectasis. Forty one percent of Bengal gram and 25% of peanuts were lodged at the sites suitable for removal by direct laryngoscopy alone. In 32 cases, foreign body was found in the right main bronchus. There were 3 disease-related complications and 1 mortality.
Conclusions : Efforts for prevention of ingestion of inanimate foreign body should focus on the preschool toddler group. More attention should be given to the need for a careful history and the use of radiographs as supplemental information to make the decision to perform a bronchoscopy. Absence of symptoms does not preclude presence of foreign body in children.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 167T: Pulmonary hydatid cysts in children : challenges in management and outcomes

Category : THORACIC

Arani Sen, Arun Kumar, Susan Jehangir, Ravi Kishore

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : aranisen@hotmail.com
Background and aims : Pulmonary hydatid disease is a common Pediatric surgical condition. Video assisted thoracoscopic surgeries (VATS) are widely preferred and have produced better outcomes in children along with the use of single lung ventilation. The aim of this study is to analyse the presentation, evaluation, anaesthetic management and outcomes in children with pulmonary hydatidosis.
Methods: Retrospective, observational study was conducted in a tertiary care hospital for children treated for pulmonary hydatid disease between 2012 to 2022. Relevant clinical data was obtained from electronic medical records and statistical analysis done.
Results: Total of 37 patients were recruited for the study out of which 24 (65%) were male. The median age was 9 years [Range : 3 - 15 years]. The most common clinical presentation was haemoptysis (43.2%) , six of whom had a ruptured cyst. CT thorax (62.9%) was the preferred imaging modality. Pre-operative albendazole was given in all but four patients. While thoracotomy was preferred initially there was a progressive transition to VATS in later years. Single lung ventilation was used in 64% of patients. Concomitant abdominal surgery was done in four patients. All children had a pneumonostomy tube placed which was removed after a mean period of 34 days. There were no anaphylactic episodes or prolonged post operative ventilation. Morbidity in the form of recurrence or persistent cyst was seen in six patients (16%) requiring redo surgery.
Conclusion: Pulmonary hydatidosis remains an anaesthetic and surgical challenge with significant morbidity if not appropriately managed. VATS with aid of single lung ventilation can be the preferred primary approach.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 172T: Bronchial sleeve resection: A report of 3 cases

Category : THORACIC

Michelle Fernandes, MM Zameer, Vinay C, Sanjay Rao, Ashley D’Cruz

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health City, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : mishumalc@gmail.com

Introduction: Sleeve resection is a type of lung resection procedure done for endobronchial lesions by resecting the lobar bronchus involved. This preserves the remaining bronchus and uninvolved lobe which is anastomosed to the proximal airway. It is an uncommon surgery in children
Case 1- A 12 year old boy, presented with recurrent episodes of LRTI for 2 years. On bronchoscopy he was found to have intrabronchial narrowing of the left bronchus with mass in left main bronchus. CT-suggestive of mass in left distal main bronchus adjacent to ostia of upper lobe. He underwent left upper lobectomy with bronchial sleeve resection. Post surgery good expansion of lower lobe was noted. He had no further episodes of dyspnea or respiratory distress. Biopsy revealed salivary gland neoplasm favoring (mammary analogue) secretory carcinoma with no metastasis. Child has been well on follow up after 6 months.
Case 2- 17 year old child presented with H/o RTA , sustained blunt trauma to chest and upper limbs. He presented with breathlessness, was diagnosed to have right hemopneumothorax with collapsed lung for which ICD was placed. CT revealed Abrupt narrowing of right distal main bronchus with concern for bronchial injury with fistula and nondisplaced fracture of a rib. He underwent emergency thoracotomy and bronchial sleeve resection of right intermediate bronchus. Post operatively he required HFNC support. He recovered well good chest physiotherapy and was weaned off to room air by POD 3. Post procedure good lung expansion noted and flexible bronchoscopy showed healthy intact anastomotic site without narrowing of airway. Child has been well on follow up after 6 months.
Case -3 13year old, child presented with Recurrent LRTI for 1 year and significant hemoptysis for 6 months. Evaluation revealed a persistently collapsed right lower lobe. On bronchoscopy there was a highly vascular lesion within right bronchus-intermedius, close to the origin of the right upper lobe bronchus. Excision of Right intermediate bronchus with exophytic mass along with right middle and lower lobectomy. Histopathology revealed Low grade mucoepidermoid carcinomaRight intermediate main bronchus surgical margin found to be free from the tumor.
Sleeve resection is an effective technique for managing complex bronchial lesions. It ensures preservation of lung and reduces morbidity associated with pneumonectomy in children. The procedure, though technically challenging, is safe and provides good outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 177T: A Novel Study on Quality of life and Musculoskeletal changes in Stage-III Pediatric Empyema Thoracis patients after Thoracotomy and Decortication

Category : THORACIC

Himanshu Menghwani, Satya Sree Balija, Enono Yhoshu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Rishikesh, Uttarakhand Email ID : himanshumeghwani96@gmail.com
Introduction: Empyema thoracis as a sequelae of pneumonia continues to be a significant health problem in children. The morbidity involved in Empyema Thoracis along with chest wall deformities that develop in such children affect their quality of life. Ours is aninitial study conducted in such children to showhow Open Thoracotomy and Decortication improve musculoskeletal deformities and quality of life of Stage III Empyema Thoracis patients.
Aim: To assess the musculoskeletal reformation after decortication and evaluate the Quality of life in Stage III Pediatric Empyema Thoracis patients
Materials and Methods: An observational study conducted in the Department of Pediatric Surgery, AIIMS Rishikesh over a period of 24 months where patients were assessed clinically and radiologically both pre and post-operatively. Quality of life assessment was done based on PedsQL questionnaire.
Results: A total of 25 children who satisfied the inclusion criteria underwent open Thoracotomy with Decortication. Musculoskeletal deformities were observed in all patients preoperatively like chest wall asymmetry(60%), chest walldeformity(60%), limitation of shoulder elevation(76%), limitation of arm abduction(44%), winging of scapula(8%), drooping of shoulder(28%), rib fusion(4%), rib crowding(96%), scoliosis(80%)and ribdeformity(4%) which got corrected in majority of patients.Deformities like scoliosis(12%), chest wall asymmetry(20%), winging of scapula(8%), difficulty in shoulder elevation(16%) and arm abduction(8%), drooping of the shoulder(4%), and rib crowding(4%) persisted in patients with prolonged duration of symptoms. There was asignificant improvement in quality of life of these patients postoperatively.
Conclusion: Timely presentation of pediatric Empyema Thoracis patients toa higher care center with prompt intervention in the form of thoracotomy with decortication decreases morbidity, reduces musculo skeletal deformities and improves the quality of life.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 165T: Management of Empyema Thoracis in children in COVID era

Category : THORACIC

Vidisa Bose, Abhishek Ranjan, Abhishek Kumar Singh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Rajendra Institute of Medical Sciences, Ranchi, Jarkhand Email ID : vidisa.bose042@gmail.com
Aims: To evaluate the management of empyema thoracis in children in the COVID era. An empyema is the accumulation of purulent fluid in the pleural cavity and complicates pneumonia in 1 of 150 affected children. Symptoms include shortness of breath, fever, dry cough and chest pain.
Methods: We retrospectively reviewed all the patients in our Department admitted between December 2020 to May 2023 who were diagnosed with empyema thoracis and surgically intervened.
Results: 22 children with empyema thoracis were treated. All the patients had parapneumonic effusion treated with parenteral antibiotics and chest tube insertion initially but symptoms did not subside. Pre operative imaging included Chest X ray in all the cases and HRCT chest in 16 cases. Four of them underwent Video Assisted Thoracoscopic Surgery (VATS) out of which 2 required conversions to open thoracotomy and 18 patients underwent open thoracotomy primarily. First post operative Chest X Ray was done on POD 1. Chest tubes were removed on post operative day (POD)-4 for VATS and POD 6 for open thoracotomy. One patient treated with VATS and two with Open thoracotomy developed small bronchopleural fistula which was managed conservatively. One patient for whom VATS was converted to open thoracotomy had to undergo prolonged ventilation and expired. Follow up was done for all the discharged patients, all of whom were symptom free and suffered no recurrence.
Conclusion: Early surgical management of empyema thoracis in Pediatric population is warranted for curative treatment with low morbidity.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 168T: Role of Video assisted thoracic surgery in early recovery in pediatric patient with empyema thoracic

Category : THORACIC

Akshay Kalavant B., Akshay Kalavant B

Department of Pediatric Surgery, SDM college of Medical Sciences, Dharwad, Karnataka Email ID : abkalavant2000@yahoo.co.in
Aim of the study: To determine the outcome of early VATS (video assisted thoracoscopic surgery) in cases of empyema thoracic in pediatric patients
Material and methods: Pediatric empyema patients presenting between May 1, 2018 to April 30, 2023 were included. Patient were assessed with chest- X ray and USG thorax and CT scan thorax. Inter costal drainage tube were avoided and were taken up directly to VATS mostly on elective basis. Initial assessment was done by inserting 10 mm telescope. If there were large area of necrosis then the procedure were converted to open thoracotomy.
Results: Forty-eight patients underwent surgery, in which 40 patient were operated with VATS. Eight patients were treated with ICD before surgical management. Mean age of children were 2.5 years. 33 patients were male. Average duration of illness in the patients before visiting us were 9 days. 38 presented with fever, 32 presented with respiratory distress and 6 children were operated for captive lung without any symptoms. Forty patients underwent USG thorax and 44 patient underwent CT thorax. There were no mortality in our series. Average days of ICD in situ was 7 days (ranging from 5 to 18 days). Average post operative stay was 14 days. None of the patient required repeat surgery. One patient had complication while inserting the port where port entered the abdomen due to raised diaphragm. 8 patient required open surgery where 4 patient required lobectomy due to extensive parenchymal disease. Two patients had persistent air leak after surgery even after 1 week but it was managed conservatively for two to three weeks which resolved spontaneously.
Conclusion: Early surgical by VATS helps in early recovery, without significant surgery related morbidity in management of empyema in children

## ABSTRACT NO. : 171T: Retained Tamarind Seeds in Tracheobronchial Tree: Anticipation of difficulties and their management

Revanth K., Nitinkumar Borkar, Charu Sharma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Raipur, Chhattisgarh Email ID : revanthkalla94@gmail.com
Aim: Foreign body aspiration in pediatric age group can cause significant morbidity and even mortality. Amongst all the various foreign bodies aspirated, organic matter in form of vegetable seeds and nuts account for 60-70% of aspirated material.
Methods: We present five patients with aspirated tamarind seeds and challenges encountered during their bronchoscopy removal.
Results: Two patients needed tracheotomy for removal of the tamarind as it was not possible to remove through the narrowed glottis. In other three patients, foreign body was removed piece meal after crushing with biopsy foreceps from bronchus itself as it has formed there a pseudo cavitation and was not moving from impacted area.All patients recovered uneventfully.
Conclusion: Removal of aspirated tamarind seeds from the tracheobronchial tree is challenging. Anticipating the difficulty and being prepared well will reduce intraoperative difficulty and allow successful removal with favourable patient outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 3E: Correlation of stress markers with clinical outcome in surgical neonates

Category : EXPERIMENTAL

Sheetal Upreti, Prema Menon, Shivani Dogra, Ram Samujh, Shailesh Solanki, amit Rawat

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute of Medical Education and Research, Chandigarh Email ID : sheetal.upreti@gmail.com
Introduction: Neonatal surgery often involves intervention in an emergency situation soon after birth. This is a period associated with some unique and rapidly changing physiology. The additional stress induced by surgery has not been studied adequately. In neonates these stress responses may be elicited by sepsis or surgical intervention.
Aim: To find the stress marker correlating best with the clinical outcome. Secondary objective was to compare preoperative and postoperative levels of stress factors and various other factors with clinical outcome.
Materials and methods: All neonates in surgical ICU undergoing emergency surgery between January 2021 and May 2022 were included. Preoperative clinic-demographic data and hemodynamic status was noted. Stress factors including serum cortisol, insulin, thyroid function tests, procalcitonin, lactate, blood sugars, growth hormone, IL6, sodium values, glucose values and CRP values were assessed in pre-operative period and repeated 12 hours after surgery. These parameters were compared to post-operative clinical course and outcome. Other factors like APGAR score at birth, inotropic and ventilator requirement, operative time, intra-operative hemodynamic changes were also noted.
Results and observations: Factors which significantly correlated with mortality rates were low APGAR score at birth(p=0.01), inotropic requirement in pre-operative period(p=0.02), ventilator requirement in pre-operative period(p=0.018), lengthy operative time(p=0.047), intra-operative desaturation(p=0.00), intra-operative shock(p=0.01), intra-operative hypothermia(p=0.00), raised pre-operative lactate(p=0.008),T3(p=0.029), T4(p=0.007), TSH (p=0.005)values and decreased sodium values(p=0.01), raised post-operative procalcitonin(p=0.043), CRP(p=0.02), T4(p=0.043) and lactate values(p=0.018), persistent postoperative tachycardia(p=0.03), and postoperative ventilator and inotropic requirement(p=0.00).
Conclusion: Among the clinical parameters, intra-operative hypothermia was the best clinical predictor of mortality. Among the laboratory parameters, low pre-operative sodium followed by raised pre-operative T4 were best predictors of mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 5E: To determine the relationship between preoperative nutritional status and postoperative complications categorized according to the Clavien-Dindo classification in pediatric surgical patients

Category : EXPERIMENTAL

Tripti Agrawal, Shubhalakshmi Nayak, Avinash Jadhav, Kirtikumar Rathod, Manish Pathak, Arvind Sinha, Rahul Saxena

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : doctriptiagrawal@gmail.com
Aim: To determine the relationship between preoperative nutritional status and postoperative complications categorised according to the Clavien-Dindo classification in pediatric surgical patients.
Methodology: This prospective observational cohort study included 650 patients from 6 months to 18 years undergoing elective surgery at our institution from 2021 to August 2022. Nutritional status was standardised using Z-scores for weight, length and BMI. Patients were followed up to 1 month after surgery to observe any complications and categorised into 5 grades according to the Clavien-Dindo classification. Secondary outcomes were length of Hospital stay and Readmission.
Results: During the study period, 627 patients of both sexes were included; 350 patients aged 6 months to 5 years ( Group 1 ), while 277 were aged between 5- 18 years ( Group 2). Wasting status was 47.71% in Group 1 and 41.52% in Group 2. In Group 1, 40 % of patients were stunted, while 83.75 % were in Group 2. Group 1 had 57.14 %, underweight patients. The complication rate was 39.14 % in Group 1 and 38.99% in Group 2. Grade 1 complications were most common( 27.2%), grade 4 were least common ( 0.1%), and grade 5 were absent in both groups. The comparison of anthropometric parameters of nutritional status with postoperative complications was not statistically significant ( p > 0.05 ). A prolonged hospital stay ( > 7 days ) was required in 20.8 % of Group 1 and 18.4% in Group 2. The readmission rate in Groups 1 & 2 was 2.8 % and 3.9%, respectively. Patients with complications had longer hospital stays ( p < 0.001 in both groups) and higher readmission rates in both groups ( Group 1 p = 0.016; Group 2 p = 0.008 ). Patients with prolonged duration of surgery ( > 2 hours ) developed more complications in both groups ( Group 1 – 67.2%, Group 2 – 82.6% ; p < 0.0001).
