## ABSTRACT NO. : 123N: Total Intestinal aganglionosis - A deadly masquerader!

Category : NEONATAL

K. R. Reshma, Beena S.V, Aravind C.S, Nidhi T. R, Liya Joseph, Sethunath S

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Government Medical College, Trivandrum, Kerala Email ID : krreshmak@gmail.com
AIM: To describe the varied presentations of total intestinal aganglionosis.
Method: Descriptive study-case series

Case 1: Term, female baby presented with abdominal distension and bilious vomiting soon after birth. Investigations were suggestive of meconium ileus. Laparotomy revealed dilated small bowel loops and distal collapsed colon filled with meconium pellets. A cutaneous enterostomy was done. However, the stoma did not function. The biopsy came as aganglionic. So relaparotomy+ multiple biopsies were done. All came as aganglionic. She succumbed to death.
Case 2: Term, male baby presented with features of intestinal obstruction on postnatal day2. On laparotomy, a transition zone was noticed at mid-jejunum. No other cause of obstruction was found. Jejunostomy along with multiple biopsies was done. All biopsies including the stoma site came as aganglionic. Bilious aspirate persisted and stoma revision was done twice. The baby expired despite all supportive measures.
Case 3: Extremely preterm, female baby, whose antenatal ultrasounds were suggestive of intestinal atresia, presented postnatally with proximal bowel obstruction. On laparotomy, the jejunum was dilated up to 15cm from DJ flexure with an intraluminal web. Jejunal web resection with end-to-end anastomosis was done. However, the baby deteriorated. Biopsies came as aganglionic. The baby expired while on treatment.

Conclusions: Total Intestinal Aganglionosis is a rare entity that can masquerade as other surgical conditions in neonates. In this case series, all three babies presented within 48hours of life with features of intestinal obstruction. The initial diagnostic dilemma was solved with the help of histopathology. It was uniformly fatal.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 125N: Accessory scrotum - Truly an appendage?

Category : NEONATAL

Neha Masrani, Shravya Shetty, Suraj Gandhi, Neha Sisodiya Shenoy, Hemanshi Shah

Department of Pediatric Surgery, B.Y.L Nair Hospital and Topiwala National Medical College, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : masranineha@gmail.com
Background: A perineal mass in a neonate often perplexes a clinician due to its rarity of occurrence .The differentials vary from accessory scrotum to a more malignant lipoblastoma-both of which are infrequently seen in clinical day to day practice. That combined with the occasionally seen congenital abnormalities of the scrotum -namely bifid scrotum , penoscrotal transposition , ectopic scrotum and accessory scrotum - makes the diagnosis even more challenging . The latter is the rarest of the scrotal abnormalities with only 43 cases reported in literature, defined as an additionally located scrotum in the vicinity of the normally anatomically located scrotum . The accessory does not have a testis within it but portrays both the clinical and histopathological characteristics of a normal scrotum.
Case presentation: In Our case, a 5 day old male child presented with a perineal mass - clinically mimicking an accessory scrotum . We excised the lesion after investigating for other anomalies and sent it for histopathology which confirmed our diagnosis.
Conclusion: Although perineal masses are extremely rare , they can be diagnosed antenatally .Axial imaging for any underlying soft tissue tumours has additional benefits but can be limited by its cost , availability and risk of anesthesia in a new born. Surgery is the treatment of choice.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 126N: Lumbosacral parasitic twin with meningomyelocele - A rare presentation

Category : NEONATAL

Shravya Shetty, Neha Masrani, Suraj Gandhi, Neha Sisodiya Shenoy, Hemanshi Shah

Department of Pediatric Surgery, B.Y.L Nair Hospital and Topiwala National Medical College, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : shravyas99@gmail.com
Background: Heteropagus or parasitic twin is a variant of asymmetric conjoined twins. The pathology entails an ill-formed parasitic twin relying on the otherwise normal intact autosite twin for survival.The incidence of heteropagus twins is approximately 1 per million live births. The parasitic twin may be an ectoparasite or lie within the fully developed fetus as an endoparasite (fetus in fetu). Sacral rachipagus, a rare variant of this pathology, is described by the presence of a parasitic twin over the lumbosacral vertebra on the dorsal aspect of the host twin.
Case Presentation: We are reporting one such rare phenomenon of a sacral rachipagus. A 1 day old male child presented with a mass over his back with evidence of fingerlike appendage along with components of mucosal tissue. The mass was assessed for a meningomyelocele, sacrococcygeal teratoma, a parasitic twin amongst various differentials.After excision histopathological examination of the mass revealed a parasitic twin.
Conclusion: There are very few reported cases of such sacral ectoparasites. One should be aware of the importance of differentiating the above from a sacrococcygeal teratoma by imaging and histopathological evaluation , followed by further follow up of the autosite.
Keywords: Parasitic twin, Sacral Rachipagus, conjoined twins, case report

## ABSTRACT NO. : 127N: Asymptomatic Biliary Pseudocyst – A Rare Presentation of Neonatal Spontaneous Biliary Perforation

Category : NEONATAL

Debalina Karmakar, Kalyani Saha Basu, Professor & Head of Department , Syamantak Basu, Junior Consultant, Nirban Basu, Senior Resident

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Nil Ratan Sircar Medical College And Hospital, Kolkata, West Bengal Email ID : drdkdebalina@gmail.com
Neonatal spontaneous biliary perforation (NSBP) is a well-documented albeit rare cause of neonatal obstructive jaundice. All cases reported in literature had an acute or subacute presentation with jaundice, acholic stools with intraoperative findings of EHBD perforation or stenosis.
We are presenting an unusual case of sealed NSBP with lesser sac pseudocyst. An asymptomatic 20 – day baby girl presented with gradual abdominal distention, tense cystic abdominal mass, no jaundice and normal liver function test. Ultrasonography and computed tomography scan of abdomen revealed a huge cystic intraabdominal mass. Guided aspiration of the cyst drained bile. Intraoperatively, 9cm x 8cm x 6cm bile containing cyst was found in the lesser sac, with a narrowing just left to the portal vein origin, but no connection with the biliary tree. Liver, EHBD and subhepatic area were normal. Cyst was drained and wall excised. Histopathology showed fibrous wall with no epithelial lining. Child recovered fully with no complication at 4 months follow up.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 129N: Morgagni hernia presenting as hypoplastic right ventricle in a neonate

Category : NEONATAL

Sheetal Upreti, Shailesh Solanki, J K Mahajan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute Of Medical Education And Research, Chandigarh Email ID : sheetal.upreti@gmail.com
Introduction: congenital diaphragmatic hernia is a defect in abnormal development of diaphragm. Morgagni’s hernia is a rare anomaly, diagnosis of which may be difficult.
Case Report: index case was 5 weeks male child who was born pre term at 34 weeks’ gestation. Child was admitted with respiratory difficulty and required mechanical ventilation. There was no cyanosis and sepsis was ruled out. Chest X ray was suggestive of cardiomegaly and lung fields were clear. Child was admitted in some other hospital and was diagnosed as congenital heart disease with inflow restriction of right ventricle and shifted to our institute. Repeat echocardiography detected right ventricle inflow restriction due to external compression by mass of liver echogenicity. CT scan confirmed the diagnosis of Morgagni’s hernia with left part of left lobe of liver as content. Child was operated and findings were a sac of size 3x3 cm anteriorly just behind the sternum towards the right side. Sac content was part of liver which was reduced. Sac was excised and anterior and posterior lips of diaphragm approximated with silk. Child was extubated 4 days after surgery and had an uneventful postoperative recovery and discharged after 10 days on full feeds.
Conclusion: Morgagni’s hernia is a rare diagnosis which may be misdiagnosed as congenital heart disease. Chest radiograph may miss diagnosis when solid organ instead of bowel loops are herniated. Differential diagnosis of Morgagni’s hernia should be kept especially in cases with suspicious echocardiography findings.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 130N: Neonatal limb gangrene

Category : NEONATAL

Ganesuni Sidharth, Shreyas Dudhani, B. Keerthana, Amit Kumar Sinha, Bijay Kumar Suman

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : sidharth.ganesuni@gmail.com
Introduction: Upper limb discolouration at birth progressing to gangrene have been reported in very few instances in the available Medical Literature. Various Maternal and Fetal risk factors have been known to play a role in the occurrence of this. These include maternal diabetes, inheritable hypercoagulable states, neonatal sepsis Protein C and S deficiency and so on. Arterial thrombosis is also associated with acquired hypercoagulable states.
Case: A 32-week, baby of diabetic mother, born by emergency lower caesarean section due to Fetal distress has presented with discoloration and swelling of right upper limb till mid arm since birth. Clinically, bilateral upper limb pulses were not palpable. There was an associated history of late onset maternal diabetes but no history of attempted IV-line insertion or hypercoagulable state in mother or father. Ultrasound doppler of bilateral limbs showed absent flow in right brachial, radial, and ulnar artery. After resuscitation and trial of enoxaparin there was no improvement and amputation of right upper limb was done.
Result: Patient was discharged after stormy post op course. The prolonged ICU stay in post op period also led to bilateral retinopathy of prematurity for which laser photocoagulation was done. No specific etiological cause could be found. A whole genome sequencing sample has been sent and the results are awaited. Vascular flow had improved in left upper limb at the time of discharge.
Conclusion: Gangrene in neonate since birth is a very rare entity and may have occurred due to arterial thrombosis. An inherited genotype of congenital amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia and various risk factors like maternal diabetes and neonatal sepsis could have led to such a severe presentation requiring amputation of the limb to salvage the infant.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 139N: Unusual Surgical Emergencies of the Neonatal Genitalia

Category : NEONATAL

Yasir Ahmad Lone, Santosh Kumar Singh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Himalayan Institute Of Medical Sciences, Swami Rama Himalayan University, Jolly Grant, Dehradun, Uttarakhand Email ID : dr_yalone@yahoo.co.in
Aims: Surgical emergencies involving the neonatal genitalia can be very distressing for the parents with potentially catastrophic long term psychological and social implications besides sometimes being life threatening. The aim of this paper is to make neonatal care providers aware of some unusual surgical emergencies involving the neonatal genitalia based on our experience at our institute over the last two years.
Methods: The neonates admitted under the Department of Pediatric Surgery at our institute specifically involving the internal or external male or female reproductive tract organs were included in the study. Common neonatal surgical emergencies were excluded and the aim was to highlight only uncommon surgical emergencies involving the neonatal genitalia. The study period was from April 2021 to April 2023.
Results: An adrenal haemorrhage mimicking testicular torsion, an ectopic scrotum (with urethral duplication and anorectal malformation), a clitoral cyst, a labial hemangioma and a simple ovarian cyst necessitating surgery – these are unusual conditions requiring out of the box thinking and management. Early and appropriate intervention tailored to specific anomalies leads to favourable outcome.
Conclusion: Early suspicion and relevant intervention in these cases leads to good outcome and can save precious lives. This is besides alleviating parental distress and saving the baby from lifelong physical, psychological and social implications.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 141N: Outcomes of Exomphalos at a Tertiary Centre in UK

Category : NEONATAL

Agana Gnanakuruparan, Frederica Cranfield, Chandrasen Sinha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, St George’s University Hospital NHS Foundation Trust, UK Email ID : cksinha@btinternet.com
Introduction: Aim was to assess the outcomes of Exomphalos major and minor at our centre.
Methods: Demographic and clinical outcome data were collected retrospectively from electronic records for all exomphalos neonates admitted. Data were analysed using SPSS software Desktop 22.0, using Mann-Whitney U and Chi-Squared tests, with a consideration that a P≤0.05 was significant.
Results: Over a 12-year period (2010-2022), of 42 neonates, 45% (n=19) were Exomphalos major and 55% (n=23) were Exomphalos minor.
Survival rate in Exomphalos major group was 90% in comparison to 100% survival in Exomphalos minor group. Median postnatal age to establish full-feed was 18 days and 7.5 days in Exomphalos major and Exomphalos minor respectively. Incidence of complications was 32% in Exomphalos major and 4% in Exomphalos minor. Wound infection was the commonest (5%) complication.

68% (n=13) of Exomphalos major babies presented with concomitant anomalies, with the commonest being pulmonary defects.
78% (n=18) of Exomphalos minor group presented with concomitant anomalies, with the commonest being cardiac defects. There was a higher incidence of Beckwith-Wiedemann syndrome with Exomphalos minor compared to Exomphalos major (26% vs 16%, respectively). Chromosomal abnormalities were only seen with two Exomphalos minor neonates.

Conclusion: Survival in Exomphalos major neonates was found to be 90% in comparison to 100% survival in Exomphalos minor. Neonates with Exomphalos major took longer time to establish full feeds and had higher incidence of complications. Associated congenital anomalies were commoner with Exomphalos minor. Interestingly, Beckwith-Wiedemann syndrome and Chromosomal anomalies were also commoner in Exomphalos minor group.

## Short Paper Session (Virtual): “UROLOGY-1”: ABSTRACT NO. : 227U: Arteriovenous fistula in Pediatric and Adolescent Patients as an access for maintenance hemodialysis: Our experience at a tertiary care center

Category : NEONATAL

Aamir Hussain Usmani, Sumit Mandal, Priyank Yadav, M. S. Ansari

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences (SGPGIMS), Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : aamirhussain756@gmail.com
Aim: Asses Outcomes of arteriovenous fistula (AVF) as a permanent vascular access in pediatric and adolescent population at our center.
Materials and Methods: Data were collected for all patients aged 0 to 19 years who underwent AVF creation for hemodialysis between January 2012 and Jul 2020.
Results: Ninety-seven AVFs were created in 90 patients. Mean age was 15.6±3.3 years and mean body weight was 43.7±11.4 kg. Of the 97 AVFs, 78 were radio-cephalic AVFs, 16 were brachio-cephalic AVFs and 3 were basilic vein transpositions. With a mean follow-up of 42.7±39.2 months, primary patency was 70.5%, 61.4%, and 57.7% at 1, 3, and 5 years, respectively. Age, body weight, AVF type, use of anticoagulation therapy, and history of vascular access failure were not significantly associated with patency rates. There were 13 cases (13.4%) of primary failure; low body weight was an independent predictor. During follow-up, 21 patients (21.6%) underwent kidney transplantation, with a median interval to transplantation of 35 months.
Conclusion: AVF as an access for long term maintenance hemodialysis in pediatric ESRD patients awaiting transplantation has shown long term durability with acceptable primary failure rate.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 225U: Long term outcomes of Cohen’s Cross Trigonal Reimplantation for Primary VUR in Poorly Functioning Kidney

Category : NEONATAL

Ashish Ranjan, Anwesa Chakraborty , Priyank Yadav, M. S. Ansari

Department Of Urology & Renal Transplant (Division Of Pediatric Urology) Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drashishranjan2209@gmail.com
Introduction: Open Ureteric reimplantation by cross trigonal technique described by Cohen is amongst the commonest options for surgical correction of vesicoureteric reflux (VUR). There is a lack of evidence in literature to what happens to such kidneys, in the long run, particularly those are poorly functioning.
Methods: Children with unilateral primary VUR with relative renal function <35% who underwent open or laparoscopic ureteric reimplantation between January 2008 to December 2022 were included. Less than five years follow up were excluded. Preoperative evaluation consisted VCUG and DMSA scan. In the follow-up, patients underwent a diuretic scan and evaluation for proteinuria, hypertension and recurrent UTI.For assessment of cortical function, DMSA was repeated annually for 5 years after surgery. A paired-samples t-test was used to test the mean difference of DMSA between pre-post observations.
Results: 36 children underwent ureteric reimplantation. 31 were included in the analysis. Most were males (n=26/31, 83.8%). Patient’s age was 5.21 ± 3.71 years. Pre and postoperative DMSA was almost same. The median follow up duration was 82 (60-120) months. One patient had persistent reflux after, and the same patient developed recurrent UTI.The difference in the preoperative and postoperative DRF was less than 10% in 29 patients. Five patients were hypertensive before surgery and all of them continued to be hypertensive after surgery while none developed hypertension, significant proteinuria after surgery during the follow-up.
Conclusion: Children with unilateral primary VUR and poorly functioning kidney maintain the renal function over the long term in most cases. Hypertension and proteinuria do not progress over time.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 236U: Assessment of Cosmetic Outcomes following Urethroplasty using Pediatric Penile Perception Scale and Hypospadias Objective Penile Evaluation

Category : NEONATAL

Sugandh Chadha, Lakshmi Sundararajan

Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust Hospital, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : sugandhchadha@gmail.com
Aim: To assess the cosmetic outcomes following urethroplasty using PPPS and HOPE Score
Materials and Methods: A prospective study was conducted between 2019-2022. Boys who had completed all stages of repair were included in the study. The Parents and adolescent boys were explained and requested to fill PPPS Questionnaire along with the operating surgeon in the outpatient department. Photographs were obtained according to HOPE-score after obtaining informed consent, which was scored blindly by the urologist. The data was classified into excellent, good, and poor.
Results: We had a sample size of 64 children between the age group of 2 to 17 years (Median age 6yrs). We found that 91% of parents and 92% of surgeons scored the repairs as good-to-excellent by PPPS. However, only 67% of scores were good to excellent scores on HOPE by the blinded urologist( ). Scores of the parents and both surgeons showed a positive Pearson’s correlation (0.01) between good and bad repairs. Two older boys also scored themselves, were noted to be satisfied with the outcome. PPPS is an easy and accurate tool to assess the satisfaction levels of parents while the HOPE scale though tedious provides a detailed outlook of cosmetic outcomes as well as highlights residual correctible aspects. The opinion of a blinded urologist provides an impartial view by removing the prejudice of the operating surgeons
 Conclusion: Assessment of cosmetic outcome following hypospadias surgery should be a part of routine post-operative follow-up. Opinion of adolescent boys should be obtained, wherever possible, to get a deeper insight into either perspective.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 183U: Standard Snodgrass Urethroplasty Versus Snodgrass Urethroplasty with glans augmentation of Distal Hypospadias: A Comparative Study

Category : NEONATAL

Monish Raj, Ramesh Tanger, Vinita Chaturvedi

Sawai Mansingh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : monishraj007@gmail.com
Aim: Hypospadias is one of the most common congenital anomalies of the male genitourinary system. The Snodgrass urethroplasty is the most common worldwide accepted procedure for distal hypospadias. Various modifications have been made to reduce common complications such as urethra-cutaneous fistula and meatal stenosis.
In this study, we compare the results of hypospadias repair with the standard Snodgrass urethroplasty repair and Snodgrass urethroplasty with glans augmentation.
Methods: This is a single surgeon series from January 2020 to March 2023. One hundred patients with distal hypospadias were included in the study. Out of these, 50 were operated on by the standard Snodgrass repair and 50 were operated on by the Snodgrass urethroplasty with glans augmentation technique.
Results: The average follow-up period was 8 months. 4 patients who underwent the standard Snodgrass repair developed a fistula, and 3 had meatal stenosis. In comparison, none of the patients who underwent Snodgrass urethroplasty with glans augmentation developed a fistula, and only one developed meatal stenoses, which was managed by meatal dilatation.
Conclusions: Snodgrass urethroplasty with glans augmentation is an effective procedure with good cosmetic results and low complications.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 203U: Waterproofing layers for urethrocutaneous fistula repair after hypospadias surgery: Evidence synthesis with systematic review and meta analysis

Category : NEONATAL

Chandramouli Goswami, Prabudh Goel, Vishesh Jain, Devendra Kumar Yadav, Anjan Kumar Dhua, Sachit Anand, Minu Bajpai

All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : chandramouligoswami1234@gmail.com
Objective: To summarize the available evidence and to quantitatively evaluate the global results of different waterproofing layers in substantiating the UCF repair.
Material and methods: After defining the study protocol, the review was conducted according to the PRISMA guidelines. Studies published from 2000 onwards, reporting on the results of UCF closure after hypospadias repair were searched for on PUBMED, Embase and Google Scholar. Study quality was assessed using Joanna Briggs Checklist (JBI) critical appraisal tool. The results with different techniques were compared with the two samples independent proportions test with the help of Microsoft Excel, MedCalc software and an online calculator.
Results: Seventy-three studies were shortlisted for the synthesis; the final analysis included 2886 patients (71 studies) with UCF repair failure in 539. A summary of various dimensions involved with the UCF repair has been generated including time gap after last surgery, stent-vs-no stent, supra-pubic catheterization, suture material, suturing technique, associated anomalies, complications, etc. The success rates associated with different techniques were calculated and compared: simple catheterization (100%), simple primary closure (73.2%), dartos (78.8%), double dartos faps (81%), scrotal faps (94.6%), tunica vaginalis (94.3%), PATIO repair (93.5%), biomaterials or dermal substitutes (92%), biocompatible adhesives (56.5%) and skin-based faps (54.5%). Several techniques were identified as solitary publications and discussed.
Conclusions: Tunica vaginalis and scrotal faps offer the best results after UCF closure in the synthesis. There are a vast number of other factors (patient’s local anatomy, surgeon’s expertise, and technical perspectives) which influence the final outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 209U: Distal renal tubular acidosis in posterior urethral valve: A prospective study on Impact of alkali therapy on Bone Z-score

Category : NEONATAL

Anwesa Chakraborty, Priyank Yadav, M S Ansari

Department Of Urology & Renal Transplant (Division of Pediatric Urology) Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : anwesa1989@gmail.com
Aims: Growth retardation is very common in children with posterior urethral valve [PUV] as a consequence of persistent renal tubular acidosis [RTA]. This study intends to screen and diagnose complete and incomplete distal renal tubular acidosis (dRTA) as a consequence of PUV. We also hypothesized a therapeutic benefit from bicarbonate therapy on growth parameters in children with PUV.
Methods: A prospective observational study comprising 120 children diagnosed with PUV were screened for dRTA and were randomized for treatment with and without bicarbonate therapy respectfully. In a study group of 60 surgically treated patients with PUV and normal estimated glomerular filtration rate (Group A), we prospectively evaluated the change in height standard deviation scores(SDS) (Z score and WHO percentile score) while they were on bicarbonate therapy. Age and gender matched patients constituted the control group B (n=60).
Results: Complete dRTA was found in 14 [24.13%] and 12 (21.42%) of subjects in Group A and Group B respectively. Incomplete dRTA was found in 21(36.2%) and 20 (35.7%) of patients in Group A and Group B respectively. The mean follow-up period was 24.7±8.3 months. The increase in height SDS (∆ height SDS) was significantly greater in the bicarbonate group than in the patients without bicarbonate (P<0.001). From the baseline values, the ∆ height SDS in the PUV patients on bicarbonate and without bicarbonate therapy was 66% and 26% respectively.
Conclusions: This study emphasises the need for treating urologist to not only correct PUV, but also to have an high index of suspicion regarding dRTA specifically Incomplete dRTA. Bicarbonate therapy improves height SDS in dRTA associated with PUV.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 233U: Lower urinary tract dysfunction in follow-up cases of anorectal malformation

Category : NEONATAL

Kanika Sharma, Akash Mishra, Sarita Chawdhary, Sameer Trivedi, Pranaya Panigrahi, S. P. Sharma

Institute Of Medical Sciences- Banaras Hindu University, Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : kanika_medico@yahoo.com
Aim: To assess the nature of bladder dysfunction in follow-up cases of Anorectal malformation.
Method : A prospective observational study was done in Department of Pediatric Surgery in SSH-BHU, Varanasi from November 2019 to December 2021. All the operated patients of ARM less than 15 years of age presenting with lower urinary tract dysfunction were recruited in the study. Their urinary symptoms were documented. Urinalysis, urine culture, non-invasive free flow uroflowmetry, post-void residual urine volume measurement, micturating cystourethrogram and retrograde urethrogram were done for these patients. Urodynamic evaluation was done and parameters were recorded. Patients were managed depending upon the urodynamic findings.
Results: A total of 20 patients with mean age of 4.82±1.97 years with male-to-female ratio of 1.5 were included in the study. Sacral abnormalities were noted in 45% cases. Most of the patients showed Good compliance (75%), normal capacity symptoms (80%), normal pressure (45%) with insignificant post-void urine volume (80%). Uroflowmetery demonstrated continuous flow (65%) with normal adequate Qmax (50% patients). Abnormal urodymanics findings were collaborating with abnormal urflowmetery (p= 0.0045). The average pre-treatment UTI episodes; frequency and pad soakage of 4.47± 2.25/ year; 10.41± 3.63/ day; 7.44± 2.28/ day reduced to 1.11± 0.81/ year; 5.29± 0.44/day; 5.18± 2.87/day for treated patients (p<0.05).
Conclusion: An intensive comprehensive and early management of bladder dysfunction in anorectal malformation, can help alleviate and control the symptoms of neurogenic bladder, thereby protecting the upper renal tract. Uroflowmetery, an non-invasive modality can help in identifying the at risk cases in advance to clinical symptoms. Patients with normal sacrum are equally at risk for bladder dysfunction, thus equal vigilance should be done.

## Wednesday, 25th October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “ONCOLOGY”: ABSTRACT NO. : 155 O: Management and outcomes of Wilms’ tumor: Our experience in a tertiary care hospital

Category : ONCOLOGY

Ayushi Vig, Manish Pathak, Kirtikumar Rathod, Avinash Jadhav, Rahul Saxena, Shubhalaxmi Nayak, Arvind Sinha

All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : ayushivig@gmail.com
Introduction: Wilms’ tumour remains the most common renal tumour in children. Over the past 4 years we have managed a varied spectrum of Wilms tumor patients. Here we discuss the presentation, management and short term outcomes of these patients.
Methodology and Results: We reviewed the data of 35 patients of Wilms tumor, managed over the past 4 years. Male to female ratio was 1.2:1. We encountered 3 cases of syndromic Wilms tumor, 2 bilateral Wilms tumor, 2 recurrent Wilms tumor and 1 case of Wilms tumor with contralateral suprarenal metastatic deposit, which is a rare presentation. Eleven out of 35 patients had stage 4 disease as per COG staging. Eight patients had renal vein thrombus and three had IVC thrombus at presentation. All patients underwent treatment as per NWTS-5 protocol. Seventeen patients received pre-operative chemotherapy while 18 underwent upfront resection. Patients presenting with IVC thrombus, unresectable tumor, and bilateral Wilms tumor were candidates for neo-adjuvant chemotherapy.
We performed 3 laparoscopic Nephroureterectomy, 2 Lap converted to open nephrectomies and 3 partial nephrectomies. One had positive microscopic margins and three patients had Lymph node positivity. One patient of Laparoscopic Nephrectomy had jejunal gangrene in the post-operative period and underwent resection of 20cm segment of jejunum. The probable cause was laparoscopic handling of Superior Mesenteric artery causing SMA thrombosis. We had 3 metastatic site recurrences and 2 local recurrences in Stage 3 tumors. Two of our patients had rhabdomyomatous transformation, one had Diffuse anaplasia, 3 had focal anaplasia whereas the rest had favourable histology. We have 94% (33 out of 35 patients) one-year-survival rate.
Conclusion: The development of institutional Wilms tumor treatment protocols and multidisciplinary team efforts have resulted in improved outcomes. Although longer follow ups are required for Wilms tumor patients, however, the results of our 1 year follow ups are promising.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 152 O: Hepatic resection in Children : Our experience

Category : ONCOLOGY

Anju Verma, Basant Kumar, Ankur Mandelia, Vijai D Upadhyaya, Prathibha B Naik, Tarun Gupta, Priya Mathew, Pujana K.

Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : anju_v2006@rediffmail.com
INTRODUCTION: Hepatic resection is used to treat both malignant and benign pathologies in children. It requires proper preoperative planning and intraoperative tools to define anatomy, to know the amount of liver volume left and to minimize blood loss.
AIM: The Aim of the study is to share our early experience in surgical management of liver masses.
METHODS: From January 2018 to March 2023, 15 patients underwent hepatectomy. Ten patients underwent Right hepatectomy and 5 patients underwent Left hepatectomy. Out of these 15 patients, 13 patients were operated for hepatoblastoma and rest two, one for mesenchymal hamartoma and one for diffuse hemangioma were operated.
RESULTS: There was male preponderance (10 male, 5 female). Age ranged from 6 months to 3 years. Total operative time ranged from 180 minutes to 300 minutes. Blood loss ranged from 50 ml to 200 ml. Post operative morbidities included high drain output in one patient and surgical site infection in three patients. All patients are under regular follow up and doing well.
CONCLUSION: For a safe hepatectomy, thorough knowledge of the segmental anatomy of liver along with proper technique of liver parenchyma resection and vascular control is fundamental. Some Complications such as biliary leak and torsion of liver remnant can be avoided by simple measures.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 147 O: Role of thoracoscopy and considerations in surgical approach of pulmonary metastatectomy for Pediatric solid tumours

Category : ONCOLOGY

Nanditha Ananthakrishnan, Tejaswi Hemachandran, Arun Kumar, Susan Jehangir Homi, Ravi Kishore

Christian Medical College And Hospital, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : totogiffe@gmail.com
Background : Pulmonary metastasis occurs in 10 - 30% of Pediatric solid tumours with surgical excision becoming an accepted treatment option in recent years. There is limited evidence on use of video assisted thoracoscopic surgery (VATS) for metastatectomy in children. The common queries raised include the indications for VATS, the need for palpation of nodules and long term outcomes. We intend to answer these questions with our study.
Materials and methods : Retrospective review of electronic medical records of all children who underwent pulmonary metastatecomy in our institution between 2012 - 2022.
Results: The study included 42 procedures done in 33 patients. Mean age of the patients was ten years, with the youngest patient being two years. Osteosarcoma was the primary diagnosis in 70% of the patients, with metastasis being detected at presentation or during initial neoadjuvant therapy in 24 patients. Eleven patients had bilateral metastatectomy of which seven were done by VATS, while ten out of thirty one unilateral metastatectomy was done thoracoscopically. 30% of procedures involved multiple metastatic lesions. Inability to detect the nodules was the most common reason for conversion. The mean duration of surgery was 153 minutes for VATS compared to 215 minutes by thoracotomy. There were no immediate post operative complications in both groups. 60 % of these children required palliation in the long term with no difference in recurrence of metastasis or overall survival between the two groups.
Conclusion: Thoracoscopy is a safe, less morbid and efficacious approach for pulmonary metastatecomy especially in children with solitary metastasis which are peripherally located.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 153 O: Retrospective analysis of sacrococcygeal tumours (SCT) in pediatric patients

Category : ONCOLOGY

Tripti Agrawal, Shubhalakshmi Nayak, Rahul Saxena, Avinash Jadhav, Kirtikumar Rathod, Manish Pathak, Arvind Sinha

All India Institute Of Medical Science(AIIMS, Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : doctriptiagrawal@gmail.com
Background : Pulmonary metastasis occurs in 10 - 30% of Pediatric solid tumours with surgical excision becoming an accepted treatment option in recent years. There is limited evidence on use of video assisted thoracoscopic surgery (VATS) for metastatectomy in children. The common queries raised include the indications for VATS, the need for palpation of nodules and long term outcomes. We intend to answer these questions with our study.
Materials and methods: Retrospective review of electronic medical records of all children who underwent pulmonary metastatecomy in our institution between 2012 - 2022.
Results: The study included 42 procedures done in 33 patients. Mean age of the patients was ten years, with the youngest patient being two years. Osteosarcoma was the primary diagnosis in 70% of the patients, with metastasis being detected at presentation or during initial neoadjuvant therapy in 24 patients. Eleven patients had bilateral metastatectomy of which seven were done by VATS, while ten out of thirty one unilateral metastatectomy was done thoracoscopically. 30% of procedures involved multiple metastatic lesions. Inability to detect the nodules was the most common reason for conversion. The mean duration of surgery was 153 minutes for VATS compared to 215 minutes by thoracotomy. There were no immediate post operative complications in both groups. 60 % of these children required palliation in the long term with no difference in recurrence of metastasis or overall survival between the two groups.
Conclusion: Thoracoscopy is a safe, less morbid and efficacious approach for pulmonary metastatecomy especially in children with solitary metastasis which are peripherally located.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 156 O: Case series of functional adrenal tumors - Lesson learnt from each case

Category : ONCOLOGY

Ankit Kasundra, Pramod Kumar Sharma, Roshan Chanchlani, Narendra Kumar Chaudhary, Vidhya Gunasekaran, Sujeet Kumar

All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Bhopal, Madhya Pradesh Email ID : drkasupdumc@gmail.com
Background and purpose: Adrenal tumors are often diagnosed incidentally with the prevalence of adrenal incidentalomas being 0.3 to 0.4 % of all tumors. Functioning adrenal tumors are even less frequent than incidentalomas discovered unexpectedly with prevalence being 0.2%. We reportedthree cases of symptomatic functioning adrenal masses from 2021 to 2022 at Pediatric Surgery department of tertiary care center, central institute of India, Bhopal.
Cases description: Three cases of functional adrenal tumors, all of them aged between 4 to 6 years: two cases of Phaeochromocytoma presenting with intractable hypertension, one of them with recurrence of disease and one case of androgen secreting adrenal cortical tumor presenting with precocious puberty are described. The diagnoses were established on histopathology report of adrenalectomy specimens.
Discussion: Detailed pre-operative evaluation of functional adrenal tumors forms an integral part of management of adrenal tumors. After the surgery, close follow up using biochemical tests and radiological investigations wherever recommended are needed to detect any recurrence of disease.
Conclusion:
Lesson from 1st case:

Concomitant extra adrenal phaeochromocytoma-frequently seen
Minimal tumor handling intraoperatively avoids hemodynamic fluctuations during surgery.

Lesson from 2nd case:

Close follow up after surgery necessary.
Recurrence and meta-chronous disease both can developed togather.
Recurrence disease in previously benign tumour can be malignant.

Lesson from 3rd case:

Excessively increased sex hormones level may indicate malignancy

## ABSTRACT NO. : 160 O: Ovarian cyst – Presentation and management : A series of 10 cases

Category : ONCOLOGY

Sonia Thakur, Rajeev Redkar, Anant Bangar, Rahul Deo Sharma, Sushma, Surendra Singh

Lilavati Hospital and Research Centre , Bandra West, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : thakursonia2910@gmail.com
Background: Cystic ovarian masses can occur in any age group in females in infants, children, adolescent. Most present with vague signs and symptoms. Differential diagnosis varies with age. Early diagnosis is necessary to reduce the risk of ovarian torsion and to improve the prognosis with malignant neoplasm.
Aim: Ovarian cyst – Presentation and Management – A series of 10 cases
Material and methods: Retrospective analysis of 10 cases of ovarian cyst been done. All data of patients, their presentation, treatment and histopathological diagnosis were noted and analyzed.
Result: Ten patient’s mean age was 73.5 months, SD+/- 4.89. Patients presented with abdominal pain 4/10, abdominal fullness 2/10, incidental findings 4/10. Oophorectomy was done in 3/10, Detorsion with ovariopexy in 2/10, Exploratory Laparotomy with Cystectomy in 3/10 and Laparoscopic Cystectomy in 2/10 patients. Histopathological diagnosis, Mature teratoma 3/10, NHL 1/10, Ovarian cyst 4/10, Malignant Mixed GCT 1/10, Benign serous cystadenoma 1/10. 2/10 patients received chemotherapy. On follow up, all patients are doing well and are asymptomatic currently.
Conclusion: Early diagnosis and management can lead to good prognosis in cases of ovarian pathologies.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 159 O: Pediatric Pleuropulmonary Blastoma: A single-center experience

Category : ONCOLOGY

Deesha Shajil, Arka Banerjee, Pradnya S Bendre

Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital For Children, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : dr.deesha.shajil@gmail.com
Introduction: Pleuropulmonary blastoma (PPB) is a rare and aggressive primarymalignant intrathoracic tumor in children, arising from the lung, pleura, or both. It has alow prevalence, high rateof misdiagnosisand a poor prognosis. Early diagnosis and complete resection are essential toachieve a good outcome of this potentially fatal disease.
Aim: To report cases of pleuropulmonary blastoma (PPB) in children treated at our institute in the last 10 years (Jun 2013 to May 2023).
Methods: We retrospectively reviewed the clinical files of 4 children with PPB treated at our institute between June 2013andMay 2023 to report the age at onset, clinical manifestations,pathological types, surgical procedures and prognosis.
Results: Two male and 2 female patients were included – 2 solid/cystic (type II) PPB and 2 solid (type III). The patients were treated by surgery, chemotherapy and radiotherapy. Overall survivalwas50% after an average follow up of 23 months.
Conclusion: The clinical manifestations of PPB are nonspecific and it has a poor prognosis. If identified early, surgical excision followed by chemoradiation gives acceptable outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 157 O: Congenital infantile fibrosarcoma - A case report

Category : ONCOLOGY

Abhinav Sihag, Siddharth Pandian, Sridhar, Senthil Ganesh Kamaraj

Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust Hospital, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : sihagabhinav9@gmail.com
Introduction: Congenital infantile fibrosarcoma (CIFS) is a rare Pediatric soft tissue tumour that originates in the connective fibrous tissue most frequently on an extremity on the distal segments, followed by the head, the neck, and the trunk. It is usually located in the subcutaneous or deep soft tissue, and presents as a painless soft-tissue mass with extensive local invasion and rarely but metastases. Most children with CIFS are under the age of two, and up to 30% present at birth with the tumour. The etiology of CIFS is unknown. Imaging findings are non-specific and biopsy is essential for diagnosis. Standard treatment of CIFS is wide local excision. Recently, preoperative chemotherapy is found to be useful in unresectable tumours and in decreasing morbidity of extensive resection by reducing tumour volume.
Case: A 4-month-old female infant presented to us with complaints of swelling in the dorsum of the left hand which was noticed at birth but increased in size over the first month of life. Clinically a mass of size 3 x 5cms was present. MRI was suggestive of a soft tissue neoplasm. Subsequently USG guided trucut biopsy was done which showed features of infantile fibrosarcoma. Child received 9 cycles of neoadjuvant chemotherapy was given for 9 cycles and near total excision of the tumour was done.
We present this case for its rarity and to share our experience on its successful management without resulting in any significant loss of limb function.

## Short Paper Session (Virtual): “UROLOGY-2”: ABSTRACT NO. : 242N: Correlation between renal Parenchymal transit time with Antero-Posterior Diameter of renal pelvis and differential renal function in Pediatric Pelvi - Ureteric Junction Obstruction

Category : UROLOGY-2

Chandrika Kalagotla, Alpana Prasad, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Ritu Verma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : drkchandrika2k9@gmail.com

Introduction: Majority of patients with unilateral hydronephrosis (HN) detected on ultrasound do not require pyeloplasty. The measurement of the parenchymal transit time (PTT) has been demonstrated by several authors to predict the need for patients who may require pyeloplasty.
Objective: The study aimed to assess whether PTT would have predicted a drop in differential renal function (DRF) in patients with HN on the affected side and to assess whether PTT would differ on the first renogram between those patients who had a pyeloplasty and those who did not have a pyeloplasty
Materials and Methods: This is a prospective study conducted betweenSepetember 2017 and September 2021 inchildren with antenatal and postnatally detected PUJO. 107 patients of 0 – 18 yrs of age group were enrolled in the study. They were evaluated by ultrasonography and diureticrenogram. APPD measured on USG, the differential renal function and PTT was calculated on renogram. Renogram and USG were compared.
Results: Of the 107 patients, The PTT was recorded for each kidney. The mean of the three DRF measurements was used for analysis. The mean PTT of the surgical and observational groups were 8.0 min (range: 8 – 30 min) and 5.3 min(range: 2 – 8 min), respectively. The relationship between PTT and DRF as well as PTT and APPD in the first renogram of those patients who have a pyeloplasty was statistically significant (P < 0.05). All except six in observation group had a function > 40% with PTT < 8minutes. In these 6 patients, PCN was inserted initially and pyeloplasty done later. 73 patients in the obstructed category had a PTT of > 8 minutes, and function < 40% on initial evaluation with increased APPD necessitating pyeloplasty. Mean PTT in surgical group after 6 months is 4 min and that of observational group is 3 min. 6 months following surgery, PTT and APPD values reduced and DRF and GFR values increased.
Conclusion: USG with renogram can identify the children with PUJO requiring surgery. PTT is a useful predictor of need for surgery in children with PUJO. Post operatively, there is reduction of PTT to normal values with an improvement in percentage renal function. Observational group need continuous follow up with USG and renogram.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 188U: A comparitive study on uroflowmetry parameters between single stage and staged Hypospadias repair

Category : UROLOGY-2

Ashwin Kumar C, Nitin Borkar, Charu Sharma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Raipur, Chhattisgarh Email ID : drashwinc@yahoo.com
AIM: Uroflowmetry is a simple easy to use noninvasive test which can be used for the early diagnosis of urethral stenosis / stricture after hypospadias surgery. We in this study aim to compare the uroflowmetry parameters after single stage and staged hypospadias surgery done in our institute.
METHODS: All patients with distal hypospadias underwent single stage repair. Patients with proximal hypospadias and patients with chordee underwent staged hypospadias repair. Uroflowmetry was done on Day 10, 30 and 60 after removal of catheter.
RESULTS: 9 patients underwent single stage repair and 8 patients underwent staged hypospadias repair. Mean age was 6.6 years in single stage repair with range from (3-13 years) and 6.4 years in staged repair with range from (3.5-14 years). In single stage group 88.9% had bell shaped curve on D60 and 11.1% had statacto curve on D60. In staged repair group 50% had bell shaped curve 12.5% had statacto curve an 37.5% had interrupted curve on D60. Mean post void residual urine was 19.07ml in single stage repair group and 18.67ml in staged repair group. The mean Qmax was 13.5ml/s on D60 vs 9.8ml/s on D10 with 37.8% increase in single stage group and mean Qmax was 11.24ml/s on D60 vs 39.25ml/s on D10 with 71.3% decrease in staged repair group. The mean flow rate was 7.1ml/s on D60 vs 6.3ml/s on D10 with 12.6% increase in single stage group and mean flow rate was 6.2ml/s on D60 vs 6.3ml/s on D10 with 0.91% decrease in staged repair group.
CONCLUSION: Proximal penile hypospadias and hypospadias with chordee undergoing staged hypospadias repair have higher rate of worsening uroflow parametrical values compared to distal hypospadias undergoing single stage repair.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 190U: Urinary Continence in Isolated Female Epispadias : Long-Term Follow-Up

Category : UROLOGY-2

Sauradeep Dey, Mamta Sengar, C. R. Gupta, Niyaz Khan, Vivek Manchanda, Parveen Kumar Mann

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : deysauradeep@gmail.com

Abstract body:

Background and aim: Isolated female epispadias (IFE) is a very rare anomaly and continence is always an issue in IFE. Major goal of surgery in IFE is to achieve continence in these patients. The aim of the current study was to assess the long-term continence in operated patients of IFE.
Materials and methods: Operated patients of IFE on regular follow-up were included. Type of epispadias (as per Davis Classification) and details of surgical procedure performed were recorded. Current continence status of patients was assessed using standardized questionnaire. Radiological imaging findings suggesting any upper urinary tract affliction, and history of any urinary tract infection (UTI) were reviewed.
Results: Continence status of 6 operated patients of IFE, with a minimum follow-up period of 5 years, was assessed. 3 patients underwent bladder neck reconstruction with urethroplasty, while 3 other patients underwent urethroplasty alone. Urethroplasty technique performed was slightly different compared to that described in standard literature. 5 out of 6 patients were fully continent at present. Dry interval improved immediately post-surgery and further improved later. No episodes of UTI and no affection of the upper urinary tract were recorded in the operated patients.
Conclusion: Urethroplasty alone may be sufficient to restore continence in Davis type 1 and 2 IFE patients, while Davis type 3 patients usually need additional continence enhancing procedure.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 198U: Management of urinary incontinence in girls with congenital pouch colon by bladder neck closure, bladder augmentation, and a Catheterizable Stoma - A Sustainable Solution

Category : UROLOGY-2

Kanu Kapoor, Subhasis Roy Choudhary, Partap Singh Yadav, Rajiv Chadha, Chandra Vibhash

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, New Delh Email ID : kanu.kapoor21@gmail.com
Aim: Urinary incontinence (UI) is a distressing problem in most of the female patients with congenital pouch colon (CPC) due to associated structural abnormality. This study hereby aims at reporting the initial results of complete bladder neck closure, bladder augmentation, and a catherizable stoma as a sustainable solution to this issue.
Materials and Methods: Records of female patients with CPC and UI who underwent the above procedure at LHMC, New Delhi were analysed.
Results: A total of 4 girls with CPC (type I and II ) with intractable urinary incontinence were operated. Two of the four patients initially underwent an unsuccessful Young Dees bladder neck repair but continued to remain wet. Bladder neck closure along with bladder augmentation was done in four patients (age ranging between 12- 13 years, Mean age- 13.2 years) three with ileum and one with a segment of retained pouch. A catherizable stoma in the form of Monti was done in three patients and a Mitrofanoff in the fourth one for Clean Intermittent Catheterization (CIC). The complications encountered post operatively were vesicovaginal fistula formation-1, Urinary bladder calculus in the augmented bladder-1, anastomotic leak-1, and temporary blockade of the stoma-1. The former three required additional surgical intervention and the latter was managed conservatively. The mean follow up period was 2 years. All patients achieved dryness with improved quality of life. No upper tract deterioration was observed.
Conclusion: Bladder neck closure, bladder augmentation and a catherizable stoma is an effective way of achieving sustainable dryness in female patients with CPC and UI with improvement of their quality of life.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 205U: Transvesicoscopic Ureteric Reimplantation for Vesicoureteric Reflux: Lessons learnt with 400 ureters

Category : UROLOGY-2

R. Satyanarayana, Khyati Kiran J, VVS Chandrasekharam

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Ankura Hospitals For Women And Children, Hyderabad, Telangana Email ID : khyati.kiranj@gmail.com
Aim: Transvesicoscopic ureteric reimplantation (TVUR) is a technically demanding operation. We describe our experience with TVUR for vesicoureteric reflux (VUR) in children.
Methods: We performed TVUR for VUR in 234 children (401 ureters) between February 2012-November 2022. The mean age was 3.2y (0.3-14). The majority (82%) ureters had grade 3-5 VUR, 29 ureters had paraureteric diverticulae, 23 ureters had prior failed Dx/HA injection, and 8 were duplex ureters. Tapering was done in 19 ureters. All ureters were reimplanted using Cohen cross-trigonal technique. Follow-up consisted of clinical evaluation, ultrasound and MCUG/DRCG.
Results: TVUR was successfully completed in all cases. Mean duration of surgery (vesicoscopy only) was 43.8 (+/- 6.1) minutes per ureter. Double J stents(DJS) were used in 76% ureters and removed after 3-4 weeks. Median post-operative catheterization and hospital stay was 2 days. There were 23 (12%) post-operative complications (20 DJS induced UTI, 1 large bladder clot requiring cystoscopic evacuation, 1 intestinal perforation requiring laparotomy, and 1 ureteric obstruction requiring re-operation). Follow-up MCUG/DRCG was available in 161 children (289 ureters, 72% of total ureters) with 96% success; 11 (4%) ureters (all initial grade 4-5 VUR) had residual low-grade (grade 1-2) VUR, but none required further antireflux surgery. The cosmetic results were very good in all children.
Conclusions: To our knowledge, this is the largest experience of TVUR reported till date. Special care should be taken in children with prior lower abdominal surgery to avoid bowel injury during port placement. In selective cases, the use of post-operative DJS may be avoided.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 237U: Management of Wilm’s tumor in Tertiary Care Centre in Eastern India

Category : UROLOGY-2

Manita, Manoj Kumar Mohanty, Aditya A Manekar, Bikasha Bihary Tripathy, Subrat Kumar Sahoo, Santosh Mahallik, Sonali Mohapatra, Debashish Sahoo, Mukund Sable

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Bhubaneswar, Odisha Email ID : manitatamang1992@gmail.com
Background: Wilms’ tumor (WT) is the most common primary renal malignancy in children where persistently innovating multimodal strategies have led to the conversion of almost uniformly fatal disease to one with excellent survival. With optimized use of current treatment strategies, including chemotherapy, surgery, and radiotherapy (RT), the 5-year overall survival (OS) of 90% can be achieved.
Aims: To present our experience over the last 7 years of treating Wilms’ tumor in our tertiary care institute.
Methods: A retrospective study was conducted on 54 consecutivepatients diagnosed with nephroblastoma between 2016 and 2023. SIOP protocol was followed at our institute. The mean age was 3.64 years with a median of 3 year. Male 25 (46%) to female 29 (53%) ratio 0.86.The most common presenting complaint wasmass, seen in 49 patients, 90% of patients. Most commonly the children presented in 4 stage, 29 (53.7%). Seven (12.9%) patients did not opt for any treatment. Neoadjuvant chemotherapy was given in 50 (92%) patients. Surgery was done in35 (64%) patients with an intra-operative spill in 2 (0.03%) cases. Radiotherapy was initiated in 2 (0.03%) patients owing to the upstaging during surgery and histopathology.Recurrence was seen in 5 (0.09%) with earliest recurrence occurring 11months.
Conclusion: Wilms’ tumor in children is a disease with an excellent survival rate. However, the sub-optimal outcomes in some cases can be reduced with an increased awareness, early presentation, apt referrals and early initiation of the treatment.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 199U: Graded blood hormonal levels following serial testosterone injections and their impact on phallic length and Preputial Vascularisation in children with Hypospadias

Category : UROLOGY-2

Meghna Kinjalk, Simmi K Ratan, Shashank Shekhar Panda, Nita Khurana, Binita Goswami

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : mkinjalk@gmail.com
Background: Many studies have proven that testosterone treatment can favorably affect outcome of hypospadias repairs while others have claimed an increased rate of complications with its use. This prompted us to analyze the effect of intramuscular testosterone injections on outcome of repair in hypospadiac with unfavourable local factors.
Materials and methods: Thirty three children aged upto 12 years of with isolated hypospadias and unfavourable anatomical factors were enrolled and were divided into two groups ; Group A : < 15 degrees of chordee underwent single staged repair and received three doses of intramuscular testosterone at three weekly intervals beginning from to first presentation.
Group B : >15-degree chordee, underwent staged repair .Testosterone were administered after chordee correction. Both groups followed by surgery 48 hours after last injection.
After 48 hours of each injection, measurement of hormonal levels, penile anthropometric measurements, platelet, and hematocrit were done Prepuce biopsy was sent before hormone stimulation and per operatively during definitive surgery. The patients were followed up for results and complications.
Results: The mean (SD) age of subjects was 4.33 +- 2.96 years.with Group A(n=22) and Group B (n=12) . A significantly increase in mean serum testosterone were noted at baseline (0.96±1.60 ng/ml) and after third injection (11.43±16.89 ng/ml) with significant uniform rise (2.2 folds). following each injection. Maximal significant rise of DHT was noted after first injection and overall after third injection (peak :628.72±1493.17pg/ml vs basal :29.05±27.27 pg/ml). Significant SPL increase(upto 15%) after three injections was noted. Whereas Significant increase in SPL occurred after each injection of testosterone’. Significant change in glans width was noted only after first injection (17%).
Though no significant change seen in the levels of hematocrit, platelets, and red cell mass all throuhout,an increased bleeding intraoperatively was noted , probably due to significant increase in preputial vascularisation by 50%. The only complication observed at three months follow up was Urethro-cutaneous fistula (3%) which was observed in group B.
Conclusion: Preoperative testosterone administration(three doses) leads significantly to improve local factors of the hypospadiac patients and ensures better cosmesis and functional results mainly by increasing local vascularity.

## Thursday, 26th October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “THORACIC”: ABSTRACT NO. : 173T: Complicated “pleural effusion”: Missed Hydatid cyst

Category : THORACIC

Varsha Verma, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Garvita Singh, Chandrika Kalagotla

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Gangaram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : varsha_verma18@yahoo.com
Introduction: Pleural effusion is common. Often a chest tube is inserted once a white out is noted on Chest X ray. Often pathologies like Hydatid cyst and the cystic lesions are missed. We present three such cases and their successful management through lung saving surgery. Materials and Methods: Case records of 3 such patients over the last two years were reviewed for demographics presentation, investigations and management.

Cases details:

A 6 year old boy came with an ICD inserted elsewhere for Pleural effusion. He developed respiratory distress and cough with watery fluid. CT revealed air filled cyst with air fluid level. Hydatid serology was positive. Through thoracotomy the cyst was opened, germinal layer removed, air leaks repaired and partial capitonage done.
An 8 year boy with pleural effusion was on ATT empirically. An aspiration led to an allergic reaction with fever. He soon developed distress. CT revealed a complicated Hydatid. He also had a left renal hydatid. Thoracotomy, cyst excision and capitonage was done. Later laparoscopic excision of renal hydatid was done.
A 2 year old had been treated for an infected lung cyst - Hydatid. An ICD resulted in bronchial communication. Thoracotomy and lobotomy was required.

Conclusion: complicated effusions and cysts should be imaged properly and serology should be done to diagnose Hydatid - which is not so uncommon. Lung saving surgery is possible and curative.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 164T: Bilateral Pulmonary Hydatid Cyst In Children : A rare entity

Category : THORACIC

Vidisa Bose, Abhishek Ranjan, Abhishek Kumar Singh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Rajendra Institute Of Medical Sciences, Ranchi, Jharkhand Email ID : vidisa.bose042@gmail.com
Aims: To describe the clinical course of bilateral lung hydatid cyst in 2 patients who attended Pediatric surgery department of our institute . Echinococcosis (hydatid disease) is a parasitic infection of sheep and dogs that is transmissible to humans. 20% of pulmonary hydatid disease in children are bilateral.
Methods: Two patients presented to our Pediatric Surgery OPD, 4 yr old female and 4.5 yr old male with complaints of persistent fever and non productive cough for 4 and 3 months respectively. Chest radiographs and HRCT Chest in both cases showed large cystic lesions in bilateral lung fields. Medical therapy with Albendazole was given for 2 cycles of 21 days each with a gap of 7 days in between . Both the patients have undergone Left posterolateral thoracotomy with cyst removal and pericystectomy. The male child has undergone posterolateral thoracotomy with cyst removal and pericystectomy on Right side 3 months after the first procedure whereas the female child is waiting for the same.
Results: Both the patients had Bilateral Hydatid cyst of lung, confirmed subsequently by post operative histopathological examination. Post operative period was uneventful. There was no problem in follow up of 4 months.
Conclusion: Bilateral pulmonary hydatid disease is a rare occurrence, affecting only a trivial section of Pediatric population. For the surgical management of hydatid cysts, care during intra-operative and post operative period is essential to yield satisfactory results.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 170T: Management of bilateral congenital lobar emphysema

Category : THORACIC

Ravi Himaja, Ravi Himaja

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Rainbow Children Hospital, Visakhapatnam, Andhra Pradesh Email ID : himaja@ymail.com
Aim : To discuss the management of a case of bilateral congenital lobar emphysema
Methods : A four-month-old child presented with severe respiratory distress, unable to feed, and severe subcostal and intercostal retractions. Baby had similar episodes from the 7th day of life for which he was treated outside. CECT chest and flexible bronchoscopy were done outside.
CECT showed hyperinflation of the left upper lobe with herniation and segmental atelectasis of the right upper lobe and B/L lower lobes.
Results : He was started on HFNC support @ 15lt/min with nebulisation. Viral panel was sent, suggestive of adenoviral pneumonia.Child was started on OG feeds and the distress worsened. Surgery was planned in view of increased distress. Left thoracotomy done, showing hyperinflated left upper lobe which is delivered out of the thoracic cavity and excised. Left upper lobectomy was done and ICD placed. Later baby developed right pneumothorax for which ICD was placed. Baby recovered and right CLE was managed conservatively.
Conclusion : If the baby is asymptomatic surgical intervention is not needed in congenital lobar emphysema. In cases of bilateral CLE it is very morbid to perform bilateral thoracotomy, so it is advisable to perform a unilateral thoracotomy and wait for the baby to improve.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 174T: Carcinoid tumour of bronchus - Lung sparing resection

Category : THORACIC

Chimmani Sai Kiran, J K Mahajan, Komal Aggarwal, Shailesh Solanki, Preethy J Mathews

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute Of Medical Education And Research, Chandigarh Email ID : kingkiran1521@gmail.com
Background : Bronchial carcinoids, comprising of 0.5 to 2.5 % of primary lung tumors, are slow growing, low grade, malignant neoplasms frequently misdiagnosed as benign condition. They arise from kulchitsky cells of bronchial mucosa. Lung sparing resection is a challenge. We present one such child with short term follow-up.
Case details: A 9-year-old male child presented with complaints of fever and cough for 1 year. On examination, there were decreased breath sounds on left side of the chest. Chest x ray and contrast enhanced CT scan chest were suggestive of left lung consolidation. Rigid bronchoscopy showed a polypoidal shiny mass occluding the lumen of left main bronchus 1.8cm distal to carina while CECT chest revealed a rounded homogenous enhancing soft tissue lesion 2x3 cms with abrupt cut off at left main bronchus and mediastinal shift. Child underwent surgery with bronchial sleeve resection with complete excision of 2.5x3.5 cm lesion and sparing of the lung parenchyma. The lesion tumour was attached to bronchus with a 2 cms base. Histopathological examination of specimen showed features of typical carcinoid tumour with negative resection margins and Lymph node sampling. Patient required postoperative ventilation for 4 days, chest drain was removed on 7th days and was discharged home on 8th day of surgery. After 3 months of follow up, the child has improved exercise tolerance and general health.
Conclusions: Bronchial carcinoid, tumours though rare, are the most common primary lung tumour in children. Lung sparing surgery should be attempted in children for better quality of life. Typical carcinoid tumours have good prognosis following definitive surgery.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 176T: Spontaneous resolution of acquired TEF - Is it possible?

Category : THORACIC

Bhanu Khokhar, Pinaki R. Debnath, Amita Sen, Atul Kumar Meena, Vijay Kundal, Shalu Shah, Tanmay Anand, Rishabh Patel, Babita Chhetri, A. L. Shabnoor, Supriya Kumari

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Atal Bihari Vajpayee Institute Of Medical Sciences & Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : drpinaki_mch@yahoo.co.in
Aim: Acquired Trache-esophageal fistula (TEF) is a common complication of button battery ingestion, caustic injuryand iatrogenic perforation while endoscopy. Surgical exploration and repair has been long prescribed as healing is often absent or delayed.
Methods: In this retrospective case series, which included 3 patients over a 2-year duration during 2020-21 shows that spontaneous resolution is however possible. Conservative approach should be tried when there is no severe sepsis, pneumothorax or mediastinitis. Initial management of such cases includes nil per oral, oral suctioning, pharmacological suppression of gastric acid output and enteral nutrition through feeding jejunostomy or gastrostomy.
Results: The surgical management was delayed due to COVID pandemic. Yet with adequate conservative management, fistula healed within 2 to 3 months confirmed by upper GI contrast study.
Conclusion: Our findings emphasize the potential for spontaneous resolution and identify patient characteristics that predict favorable outcome and this may guide clinical decision making in management of acquired TEF.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 178T: The Plight of Bleeding Lung

Category : THORACIC

Soumya Kashiv, B Satya Sree, Enono Yhoshu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Rishikesh, Uttarakhand Email ID : soumya.comely@gmail.com
Introduction: Spontaneous amputation of lung can result from inadequate blood flow resulting in necrosis, bacterial overgrowth and abscess formation. Reduced blood flow decreases antibiotic concentrations within the affected lung tissue, leading to persistent infection and tissue necrosis followed by decomposition into slough. Following is a case of Chronic necrotizing pulmonary aspergillosis leading to complete lung necrosis.
Case report: A 16 years female, with recurrent respiratory tract infections requiring multiple hospital admissions since 6 months of age presented to outside hospital with complaints of shortness of breath and left sided chest pain. CXR showed left hydropneumothorax. ICD was inserted which drained haemorrhagic fluid. She was then referred to higher centre with ICD in-situ. On presentation she had tachypnea, tachycardia with decreased chest expansion and air entry on left side. Broad spectrum IV Antibiotics were started and pleural fluid was sent for analysis which was negative for CBNAAT and bacterial growth. CECT thorax/ CTPA showed complete collapse of left lung with hemopneumothorax and pleural thickening without any pulmonary thromboembolism. Bronchoscopy showed pooled-up secretions in left bronchial tree with mucosal edema. BAL fluid c/s was positive for Aspergillus flavus after which patient was given IV Amphotericin-B for 14 days followed by oral Itraconazole. ICD had persistent haemorrhagic output and hence planned for Thoracotomy. Intra-operatively, decortication with total necrosectomy and pneumonectomy was done. Post operatively,ICD was removed as the output became nil on day-2. Patient was discharged on oral Itraconazole and is doing well on follow up.
Conclusion: Aggressive surgical treatment results in significant clinical improvement. Despite severe illness there is less chance of mortality. Early detection and aggressive management of Pulmonary Aspergillosiswill facilitate abetter outcome and prevent the debilitating complications in these patients.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 180T: Thoracoscopic pneumonectomy for destroyed lung in a child

Category : THORACIC

R Bhaskar, Tarun Jacob, Sundeep Kisku

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : drbasu0202@gmail.com
AIM: Destroyed lung in children is an uncommon clinical condition that may be treated surgically by thoracotomy and pneumonectomy, when indicated. We describe a thoracoscopic pneumonectomy in a 6 yr old child with a destroyed lung. The challenges and innovations are elucidated.
METHODS: A 6 yr old child presented with chronic cough, recurrent pneumonia and failure to thrive. A provisional diagnosis of primary ciliary dyskinesia was made. HRCT chest revealed a destroyed left lung (collapse, consolidation and bronchiectasis) with mediastinal shift and a relatively well-preserved right lung. The child underwent a left thoracoscopic pneumonectomy. The video describes the challenges and innovations employed in executing with this difficult operation.
RESULTS: The child had a minor air leak which settled on D2. He recovered well and was discharged on D11. He has been followed up for 8 months. His cough is occasional, and he is thriving. He has since had one episode of admission for LRI.
CONCLUSION: Thoracoscopic pneumonectomy for a technically challenging procedure that is both safe and feasible, when done by experienced personnel.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 251T: Thoracic Foregut Duplication Cyst with Anterior Meningocoele

Case Report: Foregut Duplication Cyst with Anterior Meningocele
Introduction: Foregut duplication cysts are rare congenital anomalies.Foregutduplication cyst with an anterior meningocele are extremely unusual.
AIM: To describe an infant with shared constellation of a thoracic foregut duplication cyst with concomitant extradural intraspinal neurenteric cyst and an anterior thoracic meningocele and discuss management dilemmas.
Case report: A Male baby with antenatally detected intrathoracic lesion presented late at 6 months of age.
