## Short Paper Session (Virtual): “UROLOGY-3”: ABSTRACT NO. : 208U: Diagnostic utility of pre-operative retroperitoneal core-needle biopsy in suspected Malignant Renal Tumours

Category : UROLOGY-3

Priya Singh, Yogesh Kumar Sarin

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : priyasingh301@gmail.com
Aim : We study the diagnostic utility of pre-operative retroperitoneal core-needle biopsy in suspected malignant renal tumours.
Methods : We reviewed our experience with core-needle biopsy done in 20 patients with suspected malignant renal tumours who underwent nephrectomy after receiving neo-adjuvant chemotherapy in the case of Wilms’ tumour. We observed the different subtypes of Wilms’ tumour, chemotherapy-induced changes in the resected specimen and the concordance between the histopathology of pre-operative core-needle biopsy and excised specimen.
Results: Twenty patients underwent core-needle biopsy pre-operatively in which 18 patients were diagnosed with Wilms’ tumour. 1 patient developed abdominal distension along with respiratory distress after core-needle biopsy. After resection, 16 patients had a definitive diagnosis of Wilms’ tumour while 2 patients who were previously diagnosed with Wilms’ tumour turned out as clear cell sarcoma of kidney. The concordance between core-needle biopsy and resected specimen to diagnose Wilms’ tumour was 85%. In all 16 patients of Wilms’ tumour who received pre-operative chemotherapy, only 1 case was blastemal predominant.
Conclusion: Pre-operative core-needle biopsy is a sensitive tool to differentiate Wilms’ tumour from non-Wilms’ renal tumour.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 191U: Microbial colonisation pattern of indwelling Double-J Stents in Children

Category : UROLOGY-3

Ramesh Babu, Ashwin Rajkumar J., S. Nishanth, Arun Prasad, M Shanthi*, Uma Sekar*

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Department of Pediatric Urology & Microbiology, Sri Ramachandra Institute of Higher Education & Research, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : drrameshbabu1@gmail.com
Aims: Double J stents (DJ) are widely used in pediatric urology practice. This study aims to determine: the microbial colonisation pattern of indwelling DJ stents in Children
Methods: Patients under the age of 18 admitted for DJ stent removal following pediatric urological procedures were enrolled into the study. On the day of DJ stent removal, a mid-stream urine sample was collected for culture. The DJ stents were removed in aseptic manner under GA. DJ stents were separately inoculated into culture media and incubated up to 48 hours. When growth was observed, an antibiogram was obtained using a standard panel of anti-microbial agents.
Results: The study group consisted of 22 patients (M:F = 18:4) with a median age of 2 years (1 month – 10 years). The commonest indication for stent placement was pyeloplasty (15) followed by reimplantation (6) and ureteroscopy/lithotripsy (1). Five out of 22 patients (22%; 95% CI 9-43%) had positive culture: Three had growth in both stent & urine while two had growth in urine alone. The most common organism isolated was Escherichia coli (3 urine and 2 stent culture). Klebsiella pneumoniae (stent), Enterococcus faecalis (stent) and faecium (urine) were isolated as a second organism. Amikacin, was the antibiotics that became sensitive in all cases. All patients were called back for clinical evaluation and repeat urine sample; none had fever and in only one a repeat urine culture was positive. Therefore only one of them warranted a full course of therapeutic antibioitics.

Conclusions :

1)Stent colonization/ culture positivity is noted in as high as 22% of patients with indwelling DJ stents;
2)Following stent removal the urine often becomes sterile and the patients become asymptomatic;
3)Knowledge of culture & sensitivity pattern helps to cover the patients with appropriate antibiotic on the day of stent removal;
4)In the rare scenario of post-stent removal urine infection a prior knowledge is handy to start a therapeutic regimen.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 202U: Validation of pyeloplasty prediction score – An ultrasound-based tool in predicting the need for surgery

Category : UROLOGY-3

Sugandh Chadha, Aditya Shikar Battacharya, Arun Prasad, Ramesh Babu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Kanchi Kamakoti CHILDS Trust Hospital, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : sugandhchadha@gmail.com
Background: While hydronephrosis severity scoring systems mainly use diuretic renogram parameters, Pyeloplasty Prediction Score (PPS; 2022) was based exclusively on USG parameters. In this study, we aimed to determine the accuracy of the PPS as a tool in predicting the need for surgery
Methods: Patients who presented with unilateral hydronephrosis were divided into two groups; non-obstructive dilatation (NOD) and ureteropelvic junction obstruction (UPJO). Conventional indications for pyeloplasty were worsening of renal function on a renogram in the presence of obstructive drainage and severe hydronephrosis on a sonogram. PPS was retrospectively determined based on three ultrasound parameters, Society of Fetal Urology (SFU) grade, transverse anteroposterior diameter (APD), and the absolute percentage difference of ipsilateral and contralateral renal lengths. The accuracy of PPS in predicting the need for surgery was analyzed using a 2x2 table.
Results: Among a total of 127 patients, 96 were managed conservatively (NOD) while 31 underwent pyeloplasty (PUJO). Among the patients who underwent pyeloplasty the mean PPS was 10.25 (1.6) while in the NOD group, it was 4.16 (1.3). This difference was statistically significant PPPS of 8 warranted pyeloplasty. However, two patients with PPS of 7 needed pyeloplasty due to a drop in renal function.
Conclusion: This study supports PPS as a useful non-invasive tool to predict the need for surgery in UPJO. A PPS cutoff of > 8 is likely to include all patients with significant UPJO. However, among those with a PPS of 7, some may still warrant a pyeloplasty as periodic functional assessment with a renogram is likely to be crucial in this group. The limitations of PPS are 1) Difficulty in application in cases of bilateral UPJO and

2)Difficulty in application in those with poorly functioning (shrunken) kidneys;
3)Extra-renal pelvis influencing PPS and
4)Inter-observer variability /uniformity in reporting sonograms.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 204U: Correlation of Hand Digit Length Ratio, Anogenital Distance and severity of Hypospadias amongst pre-pubertal boys

Category : UROLOGY-3

Chandramouli Goswami, Gaurav Prasad, Anjan Kumar Dhua, Prabudh Goel, Vishesh Jain, Devendra Kumar Yadav, Minu Bajpai

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : chandramouligoswami1234@gmail.com
Introduction: Antenatal androgen exposure has been suggested to play a role in etiology of hypospadias. Anogenital distance (AGD) and index finger-to-ring finger ratio (2D:4D) ratio have been demonstrated as indices of prenatal androgen exposure. Our study aimed to determine the correlation between AGD, 2D:4D ratio, and hypospadias severity.
Methods: Pre-pubertal boys (<8 years old) with hypospadias presenting to our OPD were included. During the time of clinical visit, still images of the genitals, perineum and palmar aspect of both hands were taken using a digital camera. Measurements were made using Image J software.
Results: Data was collected for 100 cases of hypospadias (26 proximal, 74 distal) and 50 controls. There was no significant difference in the mean age of cases (54.14 ± 28.78 months) and controls (66.3 ± 51.81) (p=0.06). The AGD was less in cases (3.44 ± 1.13 cm) than in controls (3.70 ± 1.43) (p=0.27). The Right-Hand 2D:4D ratio was more in cases (0.93 ± 0.09) than in controls (0.91 ± 0.06) (p=0.67) and the Left-Hand ratio was also more in cases (0.92 ± 0.09) than controls (0.91 ± 0.06) (p=0.25). There was a negative correlation between AGD and hypospadias severity (t=-1.38, p=0.15) and a positive correlation between 2D:4D ratio and hypospadias severity that was statistically significant (Right Hand-t=3.12, p=0.003 and Left hand-t=3.73, p=0.005).
Conclusion: This study shows that an increased 2D:4D ratio and potentially shortened AGD are associated with proximal hypospadias in the north Indian population, similar to findings in other ethnicities.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 210U: Our experience of various continent catheterizable conduits in the management of urinary incontinence

Category : UROLOGY-3

Surendra Singh, Sushma A., Rahul Deo Sharma, Anant Bangar, Santosh Karmakar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lilavati Hospital And Research Center, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : drsurendrasinghkgmu@gmail.com
Aim: Retrospective Analysis of our experience of various continent catheterizable conduits in the management of urinary incontinence. The paper will highlight the pros and cons of the various options.
Materials and methods: 43 patients, with mean age of 8 years (6 years to 21 years) in whom urinary continence surgery was performed were included. The parameters analysed were patient demography, indications, the type of conduit and the technique used, success rate and complications.
Results: Out of 43 children, 24 males and 19 females, the appendix was used as conduit in 36 patients, the Monti channel in 4 and a bladder tube in 3. Among the complications, 1 had stomal stenosis of the appendicovesicostomy. A stitch abscess was seen in four patients. Reflux of urine was seen in one case of bladder tube. On follow up, in all cases (follow up from 1 to > 10yrs) the continent channel functioned well.
Conclusion: A catheterisable conduit is an important adjunctive procedure for the successful management of Urinary incontinence
• The commonest and ideal conduit is the appendicovesicostomy with anti-reflux mechanism. However if the Appendix is not available then the Monti is an excellent option. The Monti has the added advantage of being a robust channel and can be much longer in length. Thus, a Monti may be preferable in obese patients. The bladder tube is an easily performed option – especially when redo-surgery is required. Most of these channels have minimal complications (which can be easily managed) and work well on long term follow up

## ABSTRACT NO. : 196U: Outcome assessment of pediatric pyeloplasties of 51 cases on the basis of ultrasonography and renal scintigraphy

Category : UROLOGY-3

Deepak Kumar Garnaik, Rajat Piplani, Intezar Ahmed, Enono Yhoshu, B. Satya Sree

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Rishikesh, Uttarakhand Email ID : drdeepak6683@gmail.com
Objective: To assess the changes on renal dynamic scan and ultrasound KUB before and after pyeloplasty in Pediatric PUJO. Materials and Methods: This study was done in the Department of Pediatric surgery, All India Institute of Medical sciences, Rishikesh, India. A total of 51 consecutive patients of age 0-18 years were included in this study who underwent (open/ laparoscopic/ robotic) Anderson Hynes pyeloplasty for unilateral PUJO over 4 years (July 2018 to July 2022). Pre-operative USG KUB was done on all patients to look for size of the kidney, cortical thickness, APPD. A 99mTc-DTPA or EC scan was done to measure GFR, split function of the affected kidney, T1/2. All the patients underwent A-H pyeloplasty (open/ Laparoscopic/ Robotic). Further, follow up with USG KUB after 3 months and renal scans after 6 months post pyeloplasty was done. The pre-operative parameters were then analysed with the post-operative findings following repair of PUJO.
Results: In our study, there was a significant change in most of the parameters of USG at 3 months and DTPA scan at 6 months post-operatively. The APPD and T1/2 significantly decreased (p-value <0.01) while cortical thickness, GFR, Split renal function of the affected kidney were significantly increased postoperatively. However, none of the parameters were seen to improve renal function (DRF 5%) independently (p-value >0.05). Low baseline DRF (DRF<30%) had shown higher chances in improvement in percentage of DRF post-operatively as compared to DRF>30% (p-value<0.01). Moreover, no significant difference between was seen between the pelvis reduction vs non reduction group with regard to APPD, DRF and GFR.

## Friday, 24th October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “MISCELLANEOUS-1”: ABSTRACT NO. : 116M: Ruptured myelomeningocele associated hydrocephalus: A comparative study of outcomes in early and delayed insertion of ventriculo-peritoneal shunt

Category : MISCELLANEOUS-1

Kiran Chayagol, S. Ramesh, Murali G. S.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute Of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : kiranchayagol4@gmail.com
Aim: To compare the outcomes of early and delayed treatment in ruptured MMC associated hydrocephalus with respect to timing of VP shunt placement.
Methods: This is a Retrospective study in which data of 30 patients treated for ruptured MMC associated hydrocephalus from 2019 to 2022 was collected. Early treatment included VP shunt placement along with ruptured MMC repair in symptomatic patients at the time of presentation. Delayed treatment of hydrocephalus included VP shunt placement after four weeks of previous ruptured MMC repair with reservoir insertion. Patients with asymptomatic form of hydrocephalus with MMC repair are excluded.
Results: Of the 30 patients enrolled in the study,15 patients each underwent early and delayed treatment. Mean age (SD) at presentation of early treatment group was 15.6(+/-10.7) days, and that of delayed treatment group was 132.9(+/-84.3) days. Incidence of Shunt infection requiring revision was 33% in early treatment group and 13% each in the delayed group. Mean duration of hospital stay was found to be higher in early treatment group, accounting for 17.6(+/-2.9) days as compared to delayed treatment group in which it was 8.8(+/-4.4) days.
Conclusion: Delayed treatment has better outcomes than early treatment in ruptured myelomeningocele associated hydrocephalus, in terms of length of hospital stay, shunt infection and shunt revision.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 89M: To timely diagnose OT acquired infection in neonates undergoing emergency surgery by using clinical and haematological parameters in Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi

Gaurav Jha, Mamta Sengar, Vivek Manchanda

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : gauravjha6@gmail.com
Aim: To timely diagnose OT acquired infection in neonates undergoing emergency surgery by using clinical and haematological parameters in Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi.
Objective: To improve the sepsis-related morbidity and mortality rate in surgical neonates.
Introduction: Neonates require utmost care during transfer to and from operation theatre (OT). A team of doctors, nursing staff and OT technicians handle the neonate, so hand hygiene needs to be followed at many levels. Physiological changes during surgery also make them prone to acquire infection early.Mortality and morbidity due to sepsis and related complications was high in neonates underwent surgery. As high incidence of sepsis was observed during postoperative period, an audit for OT acquired sepsis was planned. All the neonates (<28days) who underwent emergency surgery, irrespective of any clinical features of sepsis, were investigated for their sepsis profile after 24- 36hrs hours of surgery.

Methodology:

Retrospective study.
Neonates requiring emergency surgery between (1-12-2017 to 20 -1-2018) were included. Patients were monitored for sepsis by using the following parameters:-

Clinical parameters:
Hematological parameters (done 18-24 hours post-operative):
Blood culture (done 24- 36 hours post-operative)

Results: Out of 16 neonates, 5(31.2%) had sepsis at admission, 8 (50%) showed no signs of infection and 3(18.8%) developed sepsis after surgery.
Conclusion: By this study, we found that post-op handling, hand hygiene technique, cleaning of transport incubator, clean scrubbing and draping practices from anaesthetist, surgeons, OT technicians, nursing orderly led to low mortality in post-op cases of neonatal surgeries.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 113M: Navigating high-output enterocutaneous fistula in children: Strategies and outcome

Category : MISCELLANEOUS-1

Gaurav Jha, Mamta Sengar, Abhijit Kumar, C.R. Gupta, Niyaz Ahmed

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : gauravjha6@gmail.com
Aim: To assess the course and outcome of high-output enterocutaneous fistula in Pediatric age group
Materials and Method: All patients with high output enterocutaneous fistula managed in our institute from November 2016 to April 2023 were included in the study. Demographic details, index operation which resulted in fistula and the final diagnosis were noted.
Admission weight, number of days of total parenteral nutrition and central line access, duration of fever spikes/sepsis, duration of antibiotic use, mean hospital stay and outcome (discharge or death) were recorded.
The data was compiled and tabulated on Microsoft Excel. Mean and standard deviation for different variables were derived and the findings were compared with other indexed studies.
Results: A total of 15 patients were included, with mean age 8 years (Range 3 to 11.6 years). Abdominal tuberculosis was the most common diagnosis. Mean weight at admission was 15.6 ± 4.3 kg, with a mean nadir weight of 12.4 ± 3.25 kg, which improved to a mean of 14.9 ± 4.01 kg at time of discharge. Enteral feeds were continued in all patients, and partial parenteral nutrition was added. Sump suction drain was used to contain peri-fistula skin complications. Intravenous antibiotic was required for prolonged periods to control sepsis. The mean hospital stay was 59 ± 42.1 days. With the above management all patients were discharged and are doing well on follow up.
Conclusion: Managing high output enterocutaneous fistula presents a unique challenge due to limited resources and socioeconomic factors. However, with tailored interventions like enteral nutrition, sump suction and a comprehensive multidisciplinary approach, favourable outcomes can be achieved.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 104M: Encaphalocele - Is the outcome good?

Category : MISCELLANEOUS-1

Shital Narendra Bhaisare, Dasmit Singh, Dhvani Shah, Aamer Iqbal, Abhay Joglekar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Byramjee Jeejeebhoy Medical College, Pune, Maharashtra Email ID : shitalbhaisare756@gmail.com
Introduction: Encephalocele is a congenial neural tube defect in which there is an extension of intracranial structures outside the normal confinementof the skull. It is a less common anomaly than spinal dysraphism with an incidence of 1per 10000 live births. The common sites are occipital(75%) and frontoethmoidal(15%) whereas parietal and sphenoidal are extremely rare.
Aim: To report our experience in surgical management of different types of encephalocele & to study their outcome.
Materials and Methods: Retrospective observational study of all encephaloceles operated in the last five years in our institute was done. Out of 10 cases, 7 were occipital encephalocele & 3 were frontoethmoidal encephalocele. They were investigated with a CT scan or MRI of the brain. All were operated within 1 month of age.
Results: 7 cases of occipital encephalocele had undergone surgical excision of the dysplastic brain tissue followed by repair of the dural defect under general anaesthesia, 3 cases of frontoethmoidal required only repair. One had an associated Lymphangioma of the overlying skin which needed excision. The post-op period was uneventful in all patients. No mortality was encountered. They were followed up at 1 week and at 1 month and later one monthly for 6 months to look for development of any hydrocephalus or neurological deficit. The longest follow up is for 5 years. There was no requirement of post-op Ventriculo-Peritoneal shunt or any deficits on follow-up.
Conclusion: Treatment of Encephalocele can be rewarding if managed early and appropriately. It requires a careful clinical and radiological evaluation followed by surgical planning. In our series, acceptable neurological outcome was achieved with careful surgical management in all patients.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 85M: Antenatal anomalies: Maternal and Foetal Outcomes - A Pediatric Surgical perspective in the districts

Category : MISCELLANEOUS-1

Pingali Gopal Ramana Rao, Neha Reddy

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sri Krishna Chidren’s Hospital, Hanamkonda, Hyderabad, Telangana Email ID : pingaligopi@gmail.com
Aims: We intended to study the outcome of pre-natal counseling and the maternal attitudes towards antenatally detected anomalies related to a Pediatric surgeon in a semi-urban town of South India.
Methods: Over a 6-year period (2012-2018), a Pediatric Surgeon in a district of South India saw 1000 pregnant women referred by Obstetricians and Radiologists to counsel the antenatally detected anomalies. The counselling proceeded regarding the various options and the follow-up was till the later neonatal outcomes in case of a surgical problem. The data analysis assessed the epidemiological parameters, maternal attitudes, and the practical challenges faced.
Results: The mean gestational age at diagnosis was 25.9 weeks with over 50% presenting beyond 24 weeks of gestation. The spectrum of anomalies was: Renal 40%, CNS 29%, Chest 7%, GIT 7.7%, and Others 16.3%.
Conclusions: There are still a lot of practical hurdles in our country, particularly in Tier 2 places. More than 50% of these pregnancies presented beyond the present legal age for termination in the country-24 weeks of gestation. In a previous study over four years when the legal limit was 20 weeks, more than 85% of the pregnancies presented beyond 20 weeks of gestation. The increase in the legal limit gives more time and information regarding the foetal condition and sets the ground for better counselling.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 103M: Shunt surgery in neural tube defects with associated hydrocephalous: What should be the optimal timing of shunt surgery??

Category : MISCELLANEOUS-1

Shipra Sharma, Nitin Sharma, M A Memon, Mini Sharma, Arti Pandey, Sushma Ramteke

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Du Kalyan Singh Postgraduate Institute And Research Center, Raipur, Chhattishgarh Email ID : nitinsharma4you@gmail.com
Background: Management of neural tube defects is challenging. Most of them are associated with hydrocephalous. The optimal timing of shunt surgery has varied consensus.
Aims:To analyse the difference in outcomes in neural tube defects with hydrocephalous with respect to timing of surgery and shunt infection.
Material and methods: The retrospective data of Neural tube defects operated between June 2017- May 2023 was analysed. Those with incomplete data, not giving consent, shunt not required or lost to followup were excluded. All underwent closure using primary closure, Z plasty or Rhomboid flap. Shunt surgery was done either prior to, Along with or 6 weeks after the repair of the neural tube defects. Thus patients were divided into three groups. Shunt infection rate, requirement of shunt revision was compared in all the groups.
Result: 146 out of 243 cases formed the study group. Mean age at presentation was 4 days (Range 0-28days). Mean duration of surgery was 2 days( Range 1-3 days). Mean operating time was 1.15 hours (Range 0.45-3.15 hours). Shunt was done prior to in 33, along with in 48 and six weeks after in 65 cases. Skin closure was achieved by primary closure in 23, Double Z plasty in 58and Rhomboid flap in 65 cases. Shunt infection was seen in 5, 7 and 8 cases respectively in all the three groups. (p=0.6) Shunt revision for shunt blockage was required in 4, 6 and 2 cases respectively. (p=0.07) Lower end pseudocyst formation requiring lower end revision was seen in 2,3 and 1 cases respectively.
Conclusion:Neural tube defect with associated hydrocephalous often require shunt surgery. There is no difference in outcome in terms of timing of shunt surgery.

## Short Paper Session (Virtual): “G.I. SURGERY-1”: ABSTRACT NO. : 21G: Management of Anorectal malformations - Our experience

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Lucky Gupta, Lucky Gupta

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Science (AIIMS), Vijaypur, Jammu, Jammu & Kashmir Email ID : lucky.gupta@aiimsjammu.edu.in
Aims: To highlight our retrospective findings of the patients who underwent management for anorectal malformations.
Methods: This is a retrospective study of seventy patients of anorectal malformations treated by a single pediatric surgeon between 2011- 2021. Data regarding demographic details, age at presentation, type of anomaly, other associated anomalies, age at the definitive procedure, type of procedure, either primary definitive or conventional three-staged approach, and surgical outcomes were collated and analyzed. Patients with Common cloaca anomalies were excluded from this study. Statistical analysis was done using the software Stata version. Data were presented as number (%) or mean +- standard deviation (range) as appropriate.
Results: Out of 70 children with anorectal malformations, 32 were males and 38 were females. The age at the time of presentation ranged from 1 day to 4380 days, with a mean of 161.93 days. Among the malformations, 36 were rectovestibular fistulas, 25 were rectourethral fistulas, 6 were recto-prostatic fistulas, and two were perineal fistulas. Associated anomalies were seen in 58.6% of patients. Among the associated anomalies, urological anomalies constituted 14.3%, vertebral anomalies -14.4 %, cardiac -7.1%, gastrointestinal - 4.3%, and others 4.3%. Among urological anomalies, hydronephrosis was the commonest. The age at the time of definitive surgery was 3-4560 days, with a mean age of 353.17 days. 52.9% of patients underwent primary definitive procedures, and the staged procedures were performed in 47.1% of patients. In the postoperative period, one patient died of respiratory and circulatory failure, and one patient had a recurrence, perhaps due to inadequate mobilization, and underwent redo surgery later because of retraction of the mucosa. The most frequent bowel function complications were constipation (22.9%), wound infection (11.4%), fecal incontinence (4.3%), mucosal prolapse (4.3%), and urinary incontinence (2.9%).
Conclusion: The incidence of postoperative complications was more in the anorectal malformation patients who were having associated anomalies. Wound-related complications were almost the same in both the primary definitive and staged procedures patients. Long-term complications like constipation were seen in 22.9 %; which might improve with time.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 32G: Fourchette preserving PSARP : How to do it?

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Vijai Datta Upadhyaya, Tarun Kumar, Nishant Agarwal, Basant Kumar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : upadhyayavj@rediffmail.com

Abstract body:

Purpose: Vestibular fistula is the commonest anorectal malformation (ARM) in the female child. This study aimed to share our experience with modified technique of Posterior sagittal anorectoplasty (PSARP) for treatment for vestibular fistula without opening the fourchette.
Method: In the period between January 2018-June 2021, ten patients of vestibular fistula admitted after the neonatal age. All were treated with PSARP without opening the fourchette. The results were evaluated for anal continence and cosmetic appearance. Median follow-up was 12 months (range 6-24 months).
Results: A total of 20 patients were included in the study. Operative time ranges from 80 to 160 minutes. Post operative appearance of the perineum was satisfactory in all patients except minor superficial wound infection in 20% cases which was managed conservatively. Anal continence was good in 78% cases and fair in 14% cases. Around 16% percent of patients had minimal constipation and 10% patients had minor mucosal prolapse
Conclusion: In our experience Primary PSARP for vestibular anus without opening fourchette has a good cosmetic appearance and comparable outcome with standard PSARP

## ABSTRACT NO. : 40G: Presentation and management of pancreatitis in children – A retrospective observational study

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Keshava Pavan K, Raghunath B. V., Veerabhadra R.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College And Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : kpavank123@gmail.com
Aims: To determine etiological factors and clinical presentation of pancreatitis in children. To determine management of pancreatitis in a tertiary care centre
Methods: This was a retrospective observational study conducted in a tertiary care centre in patients less than 18 years of age presenting with pancreatitis. Data was collected from medical case records from May 2013 to April 2023. Acute pancreatitis, acute recurrent pancreatitis and chronic pancreatitis were defined as per standard definitions by revised Atlanta classification.
Results: A total of 220 patients were included in the study. 10 (12%) of acute pancreatitis occurred in children <5 years of age. Acute pancreatitis was found more in boys 58 (68%), while chronic pancreatitis was more in girls 48 (52%). Most of the patients presented with pain abdomen (99%) and vomiting (71%). An identified etiological factor was found only in 18 (8.2%) of the cases where choledochal cyst 8(3.6%) was the most common cause. 33 (17%) of patients had serum amylase and lipase of <100. 37 (30.6%) of the patients had hypocalcemia. 67 (72.8%) of the chronic pancreatitis patients had chronic calcific pancreatitis. 55 (25%) of the patients required surgical intervention, including lateral pancreatico-jejunostomy (LPJ) in 27 (62.8%), ERCP with sphincterotomy or stenting in 22 (40%) and pseudocyst drainage in 9 (16%). 22 (81.5%) of patients had relief of pain after LPJ.
Conclusion: Most of the cases of pancreatitis in children are idiopathic (91.8%). Drainage procedures were required in 25% of the patients.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 15G: Meckel’s Diverticulum - A ten year review of presentation, diagnosis and management

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Arani Sen, Arun Kumar, Tarun Jacob, Susan Jehangir, John Mathai

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : aranisen@hotmail.com
Background and aims: Meckel’s diverticulum is a common Pediatric surgical condition with a wide variation in presentation from incidental detection to massive hematochezia. The absence of a single specific gold standard investigation compounds the diagnostic difficulty. The aim of this study is to analyse the presentation, evaluation, management and outcomes in children with Meckel’s diverticulum.
Methods: Retrospective, observational study was conducted in a tertiary care hospital for children treated for Meckel’s diverticulum between 2011 to 2020. Relevant clinical data was obtained from electronic medical records and statistical analysis done.
Results: Total of 103 patients were recruited for the study out of which 83% were male. The median age was 4 years with three children presenting in neonatal period. Intestinal obstruction was the most common presenting complaint seen in 33 patients while 27 patients had painless bleeding per rectum. Ultrasound and Contrast enhanced CT were done in 41 and 8 patients respectively with none diagnosed with ultrasound and 25 % accurately reported as suspicious of Meckel’s diverticulum with CT scan. Technitium 99m Meckel’s scan was done in 23 patients with 100 % reported as positive. 67 patients underwent laparotomy while laparoscopic assisted resection was done in the remaining. Histopathological examination showed ectopic gastric mucosa in 45 patients and heterotropic pancreatic tissue in 11 patients. 78% of patients with hematochezia had ectopic gastric mucosa.
Conclusion: Boys were approximately 4 times more commonly affected. In most cases, the initial diagnosis was acute abdomen and the presenting symptoms on admission were intestinal obstruction and rectal bleeding. None of the imaging modalities are conclusive with surgical exploration on suspicion still the mainstay of management.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 22G: Evaluation of Protocol Based Management of Liver Abscess in Pediatric Age Group - A Prospective Study

Methods and material :

Type of Study :Prospective observational study. Duration of Study- 1st July 2021-22nd November 2022
Sample size: A total of 105 patients were enrolled in the study from 1st July 2021 to 15th October 2022 after confirming that they met the inclusion criteria and did not meet the exclusion criteria..
Results: Size of the liver abscess is an important prognostic indicator of treatment outcomes. Out of the 105 patients included in the study, 76 patients of size which were less than 6 cm in size. Out of these 76 patients, 7 (9.2%) had persistent symptoms at the end of 4 weeks. Out of the rest 29 patients, 5 (17.2%) had persistent symptoms at the end of 4 weeks. While statistically analysing the data using Chi-Square test, this difference did not turn statistically significant. A larger sample size may be requried to see any difference.
Conclusion: Liver abscesses in children are a rare entity Current standard of management of Pediatric Liver abscesses are based on prognositc factors which are obtained from ultrasonogram performed at presentation. The patient outcomes of the current standard of management causes significant morbidity and mortality. A protocol-based management of the Liver abscess can offer better outcomes in terms of lower failure rates and lower mortality rates.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 47G: An International survey among Pediatric surgeons on the role of appendectomy in malrotation

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Revathy Menon, Kirtikumar Rathod, Arvind Sinha, Lilly Saldana, Gallo, Purushottam Gera, Milind Chitnis, Zainab Balushi, Ashish Minochia, Carlos Garcia Hernandez, Enaam Raboei, Jiaoyang Cai

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : revathy13smn@gmail.com
Introduction: Ladd’s procedure was first described in 1936 for the treatment of malrotation. The original description of the surgery does not include appendectomy. We sought to understand the current consensus on the role of appendectomy in patients of malrotation.
Materials and methods: An anonymous online survey was circulated among Pediatric surgeons on a multinational level. Questions focussed on demographic details, preferences of clinical practice pertaining to surgical management of malrotation. Open ended questions were put forward to assess the perceived role of appendectomy, factors influencing decision making, as well as complications encountered.
Results: A total of 343 responses were obtained from 46 countries of which 319 (93%) were consultants and 24 (7%)were residents/trainees. When the preference for open versus laparoscopic Ladd’s was put forward, 292 (85%) preferred open over laparoscopy. Overall 184 (53%) agreed for appendectomy in both open and laparoscopic Ladd’s procedure. 172 (50%) agreed with the necessity of appendectomy in all patients, the rationale being potential delay in diagnosing future appendicitis due to abnormal position of the appendix. The factors influencing decision to preserve the appendix were the risk of post operative complications related to appendectomy, inability to utilise it as a surgical conduit. Post operative complications were reported by 42 including surgical site infection in 14 (33%), adhesive obstructionin 13 (31%), intrabdominal abscess in 10 (24%), and fecal fistula in 5 (12%).
CONCLUSION: Surgeons mostly intend to perform appendectomy in all patients of malrotation keeping in mind the potential risks and benefits.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 28G: Gastrointestinal duplications: A discussion on the management of hindgut duplications associated with Anorectal malformations

Category : G.I. SURGERY-1

Garvita Singh, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Varsha Verma, Shubham

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : garvitasingh429@gmail.com
Aim: To highlight unique aspects of managing GI duplications associated with Anorectal Malformations (ARM). Duplications can occur throughout the gastrointestinal tract and present differently, necessitating individualized management approaches.
Materials and Methods: We discuss 14 illustrative cases from our records, each with distinctive features warranting discussion.
Cases details: 14 patients (newborn to 9 years) (6 girls, 8 boys) (3 oesophageal, one each of gastric, duodenal, pancreatic and ileal, two jejunal and five hindgut duplications of which 4 were associated with ARM). Oesophageal duplications were straightforward managed by thoracoscopic excision. Patient of gastric duplication presented with malena and drop in haemoglobin, upper GI endoscopy was normal, but Meckel’s scan revealed gastric activity in the thoracic region, confirmed by CT as a duplication cyst. The child underwent thoracotomy and cyst excision, and the cyst was communicationg with pyloric antrum. Patient of pancreatic duplication presenting with features of intussusception underwent laparotomy and cyst excision.
Intramural duodenal duplication was discovered in a patient with persistent gastric outlet obstruction, which was resolved through duodenotomy and cyst excision. Laparoscopic-assisted excision was performed for jejunal and ileal duplications. Among the five hindgut duplications, four were associated with ARM. One patient had a rare caudal duplication involving the urinary bladder, uterus, vagina, and hindgut. This case required staged repair, including colostomy, cystogenitoscopy, posterior sagittal anorectoplasty (PSARP) to unify the distal hindgut, vaginoplasty, colostomy closure, and correction of the colon duplication.
In another case, a neonate with ARM and a rectoprostatic fistula on colostomy had two fistulae discovered during PSARP, with one connected to the hindgut and the other to a duplication. Mucosal excision of the duplication cyst and repair of the fistulae were performed. A patient with ARM, colonic and rectal duplication, and rectoprostatic fistula underwent transverse colostomy, followed by colonic duplication excision, PSARP, fistula closure, and later excision of the rectal duplication. One patient with cloaca experienced total colonic obstruction after colostomy closure. During re-exploration, it was found that a rectal duplication below the anastomosis had been pulled down during PSARP. The distal rectum and duplication were joined together to achieve recovery.
A 5-year-old girl with intramural rectal duplication underwent cyst excision via a posterior sagittal approach, which has not been previously reported for rectal duplications.
Conclusion: GI duplications can present in diverse ways and require tailored approaches for management. Hindgut duplications associated with ARM may be overlooked using the current investigation strategy (distal cologram) necessitating more comprehensive diagnostic imaging. A unified diagnostic strategy involving targeted MRI (and avoiding distal cologram) could be considered.

## Saturday, 28th October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “CONSULTANT SESSION”: ABSTRACT NO. : 235U: Tubularized incised plate with tunica vaginalis flap as primary repair in distal and mid penile hypospadias: The new normal

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Rupa Banerjee, Rupa Banerjee

Department of Pediatric Surgery, IIMSAR And B C Roy Hospital, Haldia, West Bengal Email ID : dctrrupa@gmail.com
Aim: The current purpose of hypospadias surgery is to improve functional and cosmetic outcomes and avoid reoperations. The commonest complication of hypospadias surgery is fistula formation. The tubularized incised plate (TIP) urethroplasty is the most commonly performed procedure for distal and mid-penile hypospadias. Additional urethral coverage is now routinely used, dartos fascia being the commonest. Harvesting dartos may be difficult for beginners as we tend to work in compromised conditions. The tunica vaginalis flap (TVF) has a separate blood supply and is not dependent on penile skin vessels. The aim of this paper is to acknowledge that TVF is easy to harvest and better than dartos as a soft tissue cover.
Methods and materials: Records from 42 boys who underwent hypospadias surgeries between January 2022 and May 2023 by a single surgeon were analyzed. Patient demography, type of hypospadias, rates of complications, and outcomes were noted.
Results: A total of 42 cases were analyzed. The age range was 2 years to 11 years (median – 3.4 years). Twenty-nine cases were mid penile and 13 were distal penile hypospadias. There were no cases of fistula, the initial 10 cases had a mild twist approx 5 degrees towards the side of the flap raised, 5 cases had mild degree ascent of the testis, and 2 cases had UTI. One case done under spinal anesthesia had complications of intraoperative bleeding later hematoma and infection and complete wound dehiscence. Cases of mid-penile hypospadias had mild skin necrosis which was managed conservatively.
Conclusion: To conclude for beginners and others TVF is easy to harvest and reduces complications and reoperations and helps build practice as Pediatric Urologist.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 27G: Analysis of clinical outcomes of intussusception in pediatric patients: Outlook from a tertiary centre

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Purva Nirlesh Kothari, Rakesh S. Joshi, Jaishri Ramji

Department of Pediatric Surgery, BJ Medical College, Ahmedabad, Gujarat Email ID : prettypurva@gmail.com
Aim: Intussusception is one of the very common problems encountered in Pediatric surgical practice. This paper aims to analyse the clinical presentations and outcomes to better select patients for pneumatic reduction, surgery and association with morbidity and mortality.
Method: This is a retrospective review of intussusception patients aged 2 months to 12 years, admitted in our department between January 2020 and January 2023 with a diagnosis of ileo-colic intussusception confirmed by ultrasound. The patients were managed with either pneumatic reduction or surgical intervention. Clinical outcome notably successful pneumatic reduction, need for surgery, recurrence and death were assessed.
Results: A total of 113 cases ranging from 2 months to 12 years were included. In 65 patients (57.5%) pneumatic reduction was successful. Of the 48 patients(42.47 %) who underwent surgery, 24 (21.23%) underwent intraoperative reduction without resection, 16 (14.16%) required intestinal resection and anastomosis, and 8 patients (7.07%) underwent resection with ileo-colostomy. Out of 49 patients who presented in first 24 hours of symptoms, 14 required surgery(12.38%), and out of the 64 patients who presented after 24 hours, 34(30.08%) underwent surgery. Mortality rate was 5.3%.
Conclusion: Despite marked improvement in awareness and referral facilities, late presentation of intussusception is still very common at tertiary centres leading to higher rates of failure of pneumatic reduction, need for surgical intervention, intestinal resection and increased morbidity. Early presentation and referrals improve the outcomes significantly.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 231U: Rare presentation of Mid ureter ectasia

Sarita Chowdhary, S P Sharma, Mahima Yadav, Kanika, Rajat Kumar, Pranay Panigrahi

Case report: We are presenting 3 month/male baby, having complaints of abdominal distension diagnosed antenatally @ 6 month of age. Patient had complaints of abdominal distension with delayed history passage of meconium. Baby present in emergency with intestinal obstruction with Barium enema film which was suggestive of dilated colon and significant dye retention. So planned for exploratory laparotomy with colonic biopsies and colostomy.
