We resected dilated and ectatic portion middle part and left uretero-ureterostomy with DJ stent placement and specimen was sent for histopathological examination. Histopathological was suggestive of hypoplastic muscular layer with fibrosis of stroma.
Conclusion:Mid ureteric ectasia with stenosis is rare variant and presentation as large bowel obstruction is not reported.Infant have small space so it may present as obstruction as reported in case.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 56U: Neonatal GI Mucormycosis: Review of 5 Years’ Experience in a tertiary care centre

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Simmi K. Ratan, Nitin Jain, Sujoy Neogi, Prafull Kumar, Mehak Sehgal, Meghna Kinjalk, Nitesh Kumar Sharma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, Delhi Email ID : drjohnsimmi@yahoo.com
Background: Mucormycosis, a serious angioinvasive infection caused by common filamentous fungi, mucormycetes. GI involvement associated with high mortality in neonates. Here, we report our 5 years experience of neonatal GI mucormycosis in 7 neonates excluding COVID pandemic period. Although, stomach is among the common sites for GI involvement but in our series colon was found to be commonly involved.
Aim: Neonatal GI Mucormycosis: Review of 5 Years’ Experience in a tertiary care center.
Material and methods: It is a 5 years (2018-2023) retrospective study, where 7 neonates diagnosed with mucormycosis among 26 cases operated for NEC, were included. Factors were noted including age, sex, term or preterm (Gestational age), birth weight, sign & symptoms including abdominal distension, bleeding per rectum, sepsis, operative findings, and post-operative follow-up.
Results: In our series, mean age of presentation was 16 days and M:F was 5:2. All of them were born preterm. Mean birth weight was 1.9 kgs. 6 neonates were referred from outside hospital after being admitted for few days ranges from 2 to 9 days. At our hospital all were clinically diagnosed to have NEC. The most common initial manifestations were abdominal distension and bilious aspirates 7 (100%). All neonates displayed features of sepsis suggested by Low TLC (100%) and Metabolic acidosis (85.7%), blood in stools (42.9%, all 3 neonates with blood in stools died). The overall mortality was 6 (85.7%%). 5 neonates found to have positive pre-operative period cultures. All neonates diagnosed as NEC and had continuous bilious aspirate with abdominal distension & associated pneumoperitoneum. On exploration, T-colon was involved in 4 cases while Caecum/ascending colon, descending/sigmoid colon and ileum was involved in one case each. Biopsy was taken from perforation or resection segment. Biopsy revealed ulceration along with extensive transmural involvement and numerous broad hyaline fungal hyphae displaying branching suggestive of zygomycosis. All the neonates were started on Inj Amphotericin B. Six babies died and only one survived. Only single surviving neonate had negative blood culture at admission, absence of metabolic acidosis and bleeding PR, involvement of ileum and less intraperitoneal contamination.
Conclusion: Neonatal GI mucormycosis should be considered in all cases of NEC with perforation having associated unfavourable factors like preterm, positive preoperative blood cultures, sepsis, multiple antibiotics etc. unresponsive to conventional therapy and gross peritoneal contamination. In that case biopsy of perforation/resected segment for mucormycosis is important. If gastrointestinal mucormycosis is present, parenteral amphotericin B is the appropriate treatment.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 137N: Neonatal gastric perforation: Tertiary care centre experience

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Kanika Sharma, Pranaya Panigrahi, Sarita Chawdhary, S P Sharma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Institute Of Medical Sciences, Banaras Hindu University, Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : kanika_medico@yahoo.com
Aim: To analyze the etiology and outcome of neonates diagnosed with gastric perforation.
Method : An observational study was done in Department of Pediatric Surgeryin SSH-BHU, Varanasi from November 2020 to December 2022. Records of neonates who were managed with gastric perforation were reviewed. Their age, gender, maturity, need for resuscitation, operation type, perforation location and outcome were recorded.
Results: Sixneonates with mean age of 5.67±1.63days with male-to-female ratio of 4:2were included in the study. Three of the pre-term newborn required resuscitation at birth, while one term newborn required resuscitation (p= 0.0045). Anterior wall involvement was seen in 4 neonates, lesser curvature in one and one patient had 2/3 of stomach wall sloughed-out. Two associated anomalies were noted; one neonate had associated jejunoileal atresia while other had acynotic heart disease. Three newborns succumbed; all 3 had low birth weight and were pre-term(p<0.05).
Conclusion: Vigorous bag and mask resuscitation can be responsible etiology for gastric perforation in both preterm and term newborns. Preterm newborns and low birth weight have relatively higher mortality with gastric perforation. Early diagnosis and operative intervention can help with better survival.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 60G: Management of Giant Omphalocele in 2 older children

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Satish Kumar K. V., Keerthana Shivaram Bangalore, Naveen Thomas, Ramya, Lakshmi, Pushpa, Joel Chakravarthi

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Baptist Hospital, Vinayaknagar, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : satishpushpa33@gmail.com
Introduction: Management of giant omphalocele is a challenge as the eviscerated contents have lost the right to belong inside the abdominal cavity (Loss of Domain). Giant omphalocele are a challenge as the reduction of eviscerated contents can be a challenge due to development of abdominal compartmental syndrome. Management of 2 older children who had giant omphalocele is presented.
Case report: Two older childrenpresented with giant omphalocele with liver and bowel as contents. Imminent compartmental syndrome following attempted primary repair in both was expected and the patients received botulinum toxin to abdominal musculature under GA. The abdominal capacity had visually improved with reduced protuberances of contents at the end of 1 month.. Both patients underwent successful primary closure with elective PO ventilation for 2-4 days respectively and successfully weaned off ventilator and discharged on full feeds 1 and 2 weeks respectively.
Discussion: The challenges with giant omphalocele management would be reduction of contents, complete fascial closure and avoidance of compartmental syndrome. The options would be staged closure using silo, use of preoperative pneumoperitoneum, botox to abdominal musculature and also use of tissue expanders. Botulinum toxin has been shown to imrove the abdominal capacity by 20-40% and stretch and relaxes the abdominal musculature. The technique has been used successfully in large adult hernias and also has been proven effective in children with giant omphaloceles.
Conclusion: Successful closure of giant ompahloceles is possible with botox injection to abdominal musculature and effectively prevents development of abdominal compartmental syndrome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 43G: Too many polyps - Rare case of multiple intussusception

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Tanmay Anand, Pinaki R. Debnath, Atul Kumar Meena, Vijay Kundal, Shalu Shah, Rishabh Patel, Bhanu Khokhar, Babita Chhetri, Al Shabnoor, Supriya Kumari

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Atal Bihari Vajpayee Institute Of Medical Sciences & Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : drpinaki_mch@yahoo.co.in
Aim: Here we present case of 13-year-old female who presents with pain abdomen for 3 days.
Methods: On ultrasound evaluation doughnut sign was observed with abrupt cut off was seen in abdominal x ray for which urgent exploratory laparotomy was planned. It revealed multiple polyps scattered throughout the jejunum with three different sites of intussusception in jejunal segments at 40, 70 and 90 cm from duodenojejunal junction. Reduction of each segment was carefully done and no necrotic area in bowel was observed. Three Enterotomies around base of each large polyp was done and closed in single layer with interrupted sutures. Post-operative period was uneventful.
Results: Histopathological examination revealed hamartoma which demonstrates epithelial elements in the submucosa, muscularis propria and subserosa and they frequently surround mucin-filled spaces. Multiple polyps must be followed up with routine endoscopy and it may involve genetic testing or screening for various conditions such as familial adenomatous polyposis (FAP) or hereditary nonpolyposis colorectal cancer (HNPCC).
Conclusion: Polyps are a common cause of intussusception in children. The combination of surgical and endoscopic polypectomy is an efficient and practical choice in the management of multiple jejunal polyps.

## Short Paper Session (Virtual): “HEPATO BILIARY”: ABSTRACT NO. : 66H: Antenatally diagnosed choledochal cyst vs. Duodenal duplication cyst: An intra-operative surprise and its management: a case report

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Anjan Kumar Dhua, Sheetal Uttaray, Kaushal Kulkarni, M Jana1, Devendra Kr. Yadav, Prabudh Goel, D. Kandaswamy 1, Madhavi Tripathi 2

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Dept of Pediatric Surgery, 1 Dept of Radio Diagnosis & 2 Dept of Nuclear Medicine, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Delhi Email ID : dhuaanjan@gmail.com
Aim: Duodenal duplications account for 7% of all duplications and may be asymptomatic or manifest with bleeding, intestinal obstruction, or obstruction of the biliopancreatic ducts causing jaundice or pancreatitis. We encountered an antenatally detected choledochal cyst (CDC) which was taken up for laparoscopic choledochal cyst excision. However, the intraoperative findings were that of a duodenal duplication cyst. The case is being presented to highlight that a duodenal duplication cyst can mimic a CDC, and surgeons should be aware of this possibility.
Case presentation: This is a case of an antenatally diagnosed CDC at 28 weeks of gestation. Postnatally, the female baby was asymptomatic, with no jaundice or lump. The child underwent ultrasound and MRCP that was suggestive of CDC (type 1b). The child was taken up for excision of the cyst electively at four months of age. Intraoperatively, no cystic lesion was present in the porta. To our surprise, a 4 x 4 cm duodenal cystic lesion was found between the duodenum’s second part and the pancreas. It was proximal to the ampulla, with a common wall between the cyst and D2 without communication. Open exploration was performed, and the integrity of the biliary tree (traversing posterior to the cyst) was confirmed by the free passage of methylene blue from the gall bladder to the ampulla of Vater without leaks along the biliary tree. The cyst was subsequently successfully excised by resecting the involved duodenum but sparing the CBD and the ampulla of Vater. A duodeno-duodenal anastomosis was done over a trans-anastomotic tube. The child had an uneventful postoperative recovery and is doing well after two months of discharge.
Conclusion: This case report will alert surgeons that duodenal duplication cysts can mimic a CDC. Preoperative evaluation should be aimed at ruling out this possibility to avoid intraoperative surprise, as in our case.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 69H: Uncommon complication following open cholecystectomy in pediatric age group - A case report

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Sravanthi Vutukuru, Shailesh Solanki

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Siddhartha Medical College, Vijayawada Email ID : vutukuru.sravanthi@gmail.com
Introduction: Cholelithiasis is uncommon in the pediatric age group, with an incidence of 0.13-0.22%. The standard of treatment is laparoscopic cholecystectomy (LC). Open cholecystectomy is being rarely performed in recent times. Complications in open cholecystectomy are less common than in laparoscopic cholecystectomy. Bile duct injury is a serious complication, with an incidence of 0.04-0.2% in open surgery and 0.01-1% in laparoscopic cholecystectomy. There is paucity of literature on the complications and their management following cholecystectomy in the pediatric age group.
Case report: Five-year-old male child presented to our institute with jaundice, abdomen distension, bile leak and deranged liver function test (LFT) and coagulopathy following open cholecystectomy done elsewhere. On arrival at our hospital, child was sick, deeply icteric, febrile and abdomen was distended with hepatomegaly of 3cm below the subcostal margin. Ultrasound and MRI abdomen with MRCP showed hepatomegaly with two loculated collections of size 1.4*3.4 cm is the GB fossa and 6*8cm in the sub-hepatic space, with bilateral IHBRD (L>R) with abrupt narrowing and cut off sign in common hepatic duct (CHD). Right anterior duct is seen communicating with left duct. The posterior duct is seen coursing towards the primary confluence with narrowing at the site of communication. CHD and CBD are not seen for a length of 2.3cm. Distal CBD is seen and is normal in caliber. Percutaneous transhepatic biliary drainage (PTBD) was done and the child received 2 weeks of antibiotics, followed by Roux-en-Y hepatico-porto-jejunostomy. Intra-op, there were dense adhesions between the stomach, small bowel loops and porta, the anatomy was not clearly demarcated. On flushing saline through the PTBD, the area of leak was identified and thus the three ducts i.e., right, left and the middle hepatic ducts were traced and the PTBD catheter was seen protruding from the left hepatic duct. Retrocolic- hepatico-porto-jejunostomy (end to side) was done in single layer. Post-operative period was uneventful. The child was allowed orally on POD-5 and icterus has subsided. LFT were normalized and was stable at discharge. At 6 months follow-up, the child is doing well.
Conclusion: Early recognition and an adequate multidisciplinary approach are the cornerstones for the optimal outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 74H: Feasibility of donor left lateral hepatectomy in the setting of absent right hepatic vein: A case report

Category : HEPATO BILIARY

Arka Banerjee, Shailesh Sable, Pradnya S. Bendre

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital For Children, Mumbai Email ID : arkabanerjee6989@gmail.com

Background: One of the rare variations in the hepatic venous anatomy is the absence of a separate Right hepatic vein (RHV) where one must be extremely careful during a left hepatectomy in order to preserve the MHV trunk which drains most of the remnant liver. We report the case of a left lobe donor who had this rare variation detected preoperatively.
Case report: A 35-year-old female, liver donor for her son with end stage liver disease (Wilson’s disease) was evaluated and found to have an absent RHV, replaced by two inferior hepatic veins. The LHV was joining the MHV in the intrahepatic portion and later draining into the IVC via a common trunk. We did a modified left lateral hepatectomy. The MHV trunk was identified along the cut surface of the liver and used as a guide for the liver transection. Post-operative recovery was smooth and uneventful.
Conclusion: The most common variation in the RHV anatomy is the presence of Right inferior accessory veins (RIHV). A detailed knowledge and spatial orientation of the pattern of branching of hepatic veins is crucial for the transplant surgeon, especially for LDLT or cadaveric split liver grafts, in planning the planes of dissection based on the reconstructed CT images. In such cases, the surgeon’s experience is crucial to get good results without putting the donor at risk.

## Monday, 30th October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “G.I.SURGERY - 2”: ABSTRACT NO. : 51G: Outcome of Shunt and Non-shunt Operations for Extrahepatic Portal Hypertension in Children

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Meshram Rohit Bhashkar, Subhasis Roy Choudhury, Vikram Khanna, Chandra Vibhash, Pute Losu, Kanu Kapoor

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : rohitmesh2905@gmail.com
Aim : To evaluate the results of splenectomy with proximal lieno-renal shunt (SPLR) and splenectomy with esophago-gastric devascularization (SPDVS) in extrahepatic portal hypertension (EHPVO) in children.
Materials and methods : Charts of all children with EHPVO who underwent surgery in a tertiary care institution, between July 2001 to June 2022 were analyzed.
Indications of surgery were massive symptomatic splenomegaly (>15 cm below the costal margin) with severe hypersplenism (white cell count <2500, platelets <50000). Diagnosis was based on ultrasound with doppler, upper gastrointestinal endoscopy demonstrating esophageal varices, and CECT abdomen with portography. Patients with past history of variceal bleeding were earlier effectively managed with endoscopic therapy.
Results: Over the 21-y period, 33 children underwent surgery. M: F was 2:1, mean age at surgery was 10.8 y (range - 5 to 17 y). SPDVS was done in 26 patients while in 7 patients with a sizeable splenic vein (>6mm) and those with portal biliopathy on CECT underwent SPLR. All patients had symptomatic relief and reversal of features of hypersplenism. There were no major perioperative complications or mortality. The shunts were patent on follow-up doppler USG. During the follow-up period of 1 to 5y, patients in both the groups were doing well with improvement in their growth, general wellbeing and enhanced physical activity with no worsening of biliopathy.
Conclusion: Both SPDVS and SPLR were effective in the management of massive splenomegaly and severe symptomatic hypersplenism due to EHPVO in children with minimal morbidity and mortality. Patients with evidence of biliopathy merits a shunt surgery.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 53G: Is Hata’s Sphincteromyectomy and Sphincteroplasty Curative in cases of chronic constipation?

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

K. M. Sidharth Saleem, Mohan K Abraham

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Amrita Institute Of Medical Sciences, Kochi, Kerala Email ID : sidharthsaleem@gmail.com
Aim: Our aim was to find out whether sphincteromyectomy and sphincteroplasty as recommended by YoshinobuHata will give relief to these children without causing fecal incontinence.
Methods: A retrospective review of cases done between 2006 and 2021 was done. All cases, where conservative management failed and barium enema failed to show narrow segment, were taken up for the study. All patients who had previous anorectal surgery or proven Hirschsprung disease were excluded from the study. An incision was made at the pectinate line and a triangular piece of full thickness rectal wall with internal sphincter 6 cms in length was taken for biopsy. Apex of the triangular defect was brought to the pectinate line and sutured.
Results: There were total of 51 cases. 36 had ganglion cells in the biopsy while 15 did not show ganglion cells.
Five patients were lost to follow up after 3 months.
Out of the remaining 31 patients, 30 (96.77%) were cured of the constipation with an average follow up of 6 months.
Of those 15 children who did not show ganglion cells, five were cured of symptoms with an average follow up of 6 months (33.3%)
Conclusions: Hata’s technique of sphincteromyectomy and sphincteroplasty gives relief to chronic constipation in children without causing incontinence.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 7G: An observational study of Pediatric Oesophageal replacement by gastric pull-up – Our institutional experience

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Sriranganath H. S., Ramesh Tanger

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sawai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : ranga1989@gmail.com
Aim: The aim of this study was to report the results of Pediatric oesophageal substitution by gastric pull-up in pure oesophageal atresia(EA) and EA with distal trachea-oesophageal fistula from a tertiary care Pediatric surgery centre.
Materials and Methods: Retrospective analysis of the surgical techniques, results, complications, and final outcome of all Pediatric patients who underwent oesophageal substitution in a single institution.
Results: Fifteen substitutions were performed over a 2- year period. The indications were pure oesophageal atresia (EA), EA with distal trachea-oesophageal fistula. Mean age and weight at operation were 18 months and 9.5 kg, respectively. Posterior mediastinal and retrosternal routes were used in these cases. Major complications included postoperative cervical anastomotic leak. Perioperative and postoperative tachyarrhythmias were not reported. The postoperative intensive care stay was uneventful. Follow-up has been uneventful with no major growth-related problems.
Conclusions: The results of Gastric pull-up for oesophageal substitution in children are favourable in terms of less incidence of stricture formation and fewer postoperative hospital visits. The experience of the surgeon and the standard of institutional supportive care should play a vital role in the decision-making process.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 8G: Management of liver abscess in Pediatric age group patients – Our Institutional experience : An observational study

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Rohit Mittal, Pramila Sharma

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sawai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : haryanaboy123@gmail.com
Aim: Liver abscess is common in pediatric population in India. Children have unique set of predisposing factors and clinical features. Liver abscesses are infectious, space-occupying lesions in the liver; the two most common abscesses being pyogenic and amebic. Its severity depends on the source of the infection and the underlying condition of the patient.
Method: A total of 52 patients less than 14 years were assessed in a study from May 2022 to April 2024. Patients were assessed in terms of age of presentation, etiology, bacteriology, diagnosis, and modality of treatment.
Results: The mean age of presentation was 6.8 years. Average volume of abscess was 92 cc. Twenty patients (38.46%) underwent percutaneous needle aspiration under ultrasound guidance with wide bore needle (18 G disposable needle). Patients with volume more than 60 cc were treated with catheter drainage. Twenty four (46.15%) underwent ultrasound-guided single percutaneous catheter drainage. Six patients (11.53%) had ruptured liver abscess for which one patient (1.9%) underwent laparotomy and five (9.6%) patients underwent two ultrasound – guided catheter drainage.
Conclusion: Antimicrobial therapy along with percutaneous drainage constitutes the mainstay of treatment and even ruptured liver abscess can be managed by percutaneous drainage whereas open surgical drainage should be reserved for selected cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 10G: Revision Anorectoplasty in boys with Anorectal Malformation

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Nitin Pant, Anand Pandey, Rahul Kumar Rai, Nirpex Tyagi, Sudhir Singh, Piyush Kumar, Jiledar Rawat

Department of Pediatric Surgery, King George`S Medical University, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drnitinpant@gmail.com
Aims : Revision anorectoplasty (RA) in boys with anorectal malformation (ARM) is relatively uncommon. It is reported for a misplaced rectum, acquired rectal strictures/ stenosis, dehiscence, and recto urethral fistula. We aim to present 6 boys for whom such reoperation was done.
Method : A retrospective review over 8 years, (2015-2022). Boys who underwent RA for ARM were included. Cases with mucosal prolapse, neoanus stenosis, and mild wound dehiscence with a normally placed rectum were excluded. Their clinic-radiological profile, treatment, and outcome were analysed.
Results: Six boys had major problems post-ARM repair. Five were high ARM cases and one had a low ARM. Four were referred cases. All were managed by an initial diverting stoma, radiological imaging, subsequent RA, and stoma closure. The RA was done using posterior sagittal route (PSARP).
Three referred cases had undergone a laparoscopic-assisted anorectoplasty (LAARP). Of these, two presented after the completion of all three stages with intestinal obstruction and faecal soiling respectively. The third was initially suspected as a rectal stricture before stoma closure but was subsequently diagnosed as a blind-ending epithelialized tract secondary to erroneous dilatation of pararectal tissue, with lateral displacement of the rectum. Another was a mismanaged low ARM presenting with fecaluria. Two cases had complete wound dehiscence with retraction of the rectum post-PSARP. The overall outcome was satisfactory.
Conclusion: Morbid complications can occur following an improper pull-through in ARM. Moreover, some damage to the sphincter tissue occurs during RA. Thus, proper planning of the initial approach as well as meticulously performing the initial surgery is important.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 17G: Makeshift shunts in Extrahepatic portal vein obstruction (EHPVO) - A tertiary care hospital experience

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Tarun Gupta, Basant Kumar, Vijai D. Upadhyaya, Ankur Mandelia, Priya Mathews, Pujana K.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : tarun.gupta245@gmail.com
Background : Portosystemic splenorenal or mesenterico- caval shunting is a safe and effective method to relieve symptomatic patients with extrahepatic portal venous obstruction (EHPVO). Proximal splenorenal shunt (PSRS) is one of the most commonly performed portosystemic shunt (PSS). Sometimes with various anatomical and surgical factors related to the splenic vein (SV) and/or left renal vein (LRV), a makeshift / unconventional portosystemic shunt is required to tide over such conditions.
Aim of study is to share our experience with unconventional/makeshift shunts in cases of EHPVO.
Methods: From January 2019 to March 2023, 52 patients with EHPVO underwent portosystemic shunts (PSS), of which, the 5 (~10%) patients required unconventional/makeshift shunts for various reasons; form the study group.
Results: The five makeshift shunts included 3 proximal spleno-adrenal shunts, 1 inferior mesenteric vein to inferior vena cava (IMV-caval) shunt and 1 collateral-renal shunt. Median age was 10 years and one patient was female. The mean percentage drop in omental pressure was ~ 28.6 % post-shunt with a mean anastomotic diameter of 0.8 cm. All patients were asymptomatic and had patent shunts in follow up Doppler USG. None of the patients had variceal bleed; features of biliopathy or hepatic encephalopathy in follow up.
Conclusion: Makeshift shunts can be used safely and effectively with good postoperative outcomes in EHPVO during difficult/unwanted situations.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 20G: Predictors for surgical management of corrosive strictures in children - A retrospective review

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 2

Nanditha Ananthakrishnan, Niveditha Shama, Arun Kumar, Ravi Kishore, Susan Jehangir Homi

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College And Hospital, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : totogiffe@gmail.com
Background : Corrosive ingestion in children is a common Pediatric emergency with significant long term morbidity in the form of strictures and perforations. Endoscopic dilatation is considered as the first line of treatment for corrosive strictures. However oesophageal replacement or gastric surgeries are needed in some children with strictures that are long, multiple, refractory to dilatation or complicated by oesophageal perforation.
Aim: The main aim of our study is to identify predictors of surgical management in children undergoing endoscopic dilatation for oesophageal strictures secondary to corrosive ingestion.
Methods: We retrospectively reviewed the charts of children with corrosive injuries treated with surgery at Christian Medical College, Vellore between 2010 and 2022. Relevant clinical data was collected from the electronic medical records and statistical analysis was done.
Results: Total of 31 patients were included in the study of which 42 % were male. The median age was six years (range – 1 year to 15 years). The commonly ingested corrosive was acid. Endoscopy revealed isolated oesophageal strictures in ten children, isolated gastric involvement in eight and combined in the rest. The strictures were predominantly 15 – 20 cm from the incisors, > 3 cm in length. Multiple dilatations (>5) were required in 30% of cases. Triamcinalone injection was given in six children. Two children had oesophageal perforation. Pyloroplasty, gastric and colonic pull up were the most common procedures performed.
Conclusion: The presence of esophageal strictures > 3 cm, associated gastric involvement and the occurrence of oesophageal perforation during dilatation were predictors of surgical management in corrosive injuries.

## Tuesday, 31st October 2023: Short Paper Session (Virtual): “G.I.SURGERY-3”: ABSTRACT NO. : 30G: Factors associated with spontaneous passage of Ingested Foreign Bodies in children: A Prospective Study

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

H. Aneesh Shenoy, Veerabhadra, Keshava Pavan K.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College And Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : haneeshshenoy@gmail.com
Aim: To analyze the factors associated with the spontaneous passage of ingested foreign bodies in children.
Methods: A prospective study design was implemented, including children less than 18 years of age with a history of foreign body ingestion between June 2022 and May 2023.
Results: Out of 93 children with foreign body ingestion, 69 were included in the study. The mean age of the study group was 6.2 ± 4 years, with 39 being boys. Among the foreign bodies ingested, 26 were sharp objects while the remaining were round. The median time duration at presentation since ingestion was 10.5 hours. Most of the children were asymptomatic. History of constipation was reported in 15 children, and radiological evaluation revealed a loaded colon in 46 children. Three children required intervention, including one with the ingestion of double magnets and two with chronic impacted foreign bodies. Amongst the 66 cases with spontaneous passage, mean time duration for spontaneous passage was 5.3 days ± 5.3 days. Children without constipation had a faster passage of the foreign body (2.1 days ± 1.3 days vs. 7.6 days ± 5.4 days; p < 0.001; Student t-test). Children with the ingestion of sharp foreign bodies presented earlier (4.5 vs. 27 hours; p = 0.02; Mann Whitney U test). Furthermore, sharp foreign bodies tended to pass spontaneously at a faster rate compared to round foreign bodies (76.4 hours vs. 159.1 hours; p = 0.007; Student t-test).
Conclusion: Constipation in children is associated with delayed passage of ingested foreign bodies. Children with the ingestion of sharp foreign bodies tend to present earlier and experience faster spontaneous passage compared to those with round foreign bodies.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 55G: Ileal pull through procedure in patients of Congenital Pouch colon (CPC) and Total Colonic Aganglionosis (TCA) : A retrospective study

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Revathy Menon, Manish Pathak, Kirtikumar Rathod, Avinash Jadhav, Shubhalaxmi Nayak, Arvind Sinha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All Institute of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : revathy13smn@gmail.com
Aim: The study was conducted to evaluate results of Ileal pull throughin patients of CPC and TCA.
Materials and methods: We included all patients who underwent Ileal pull through at our center for CPC and TCA from June 2015 to June 2023. Data collected included age at time of surgery, surgical approach,time to passage of stool, time to full feeds,status at follow up.
Results: Eight patients were included of which5 had type 1 or 2 CPC and 3 were cases of biopsy proven TCA. Median age at presentation was 12 months with range of 5 to 24 months.
Two patients underwent laparoscopic and 6 underwent open pull through without any overing stoma.
At follow up of 1 month, all patients were passing semisolid stool at frequent intervals anddeveloped perianal excoriations.The frequency of stools reduced gradually. On follow up at 15 months after surgery all patients passed formed stool with little or no excoriations.
Conclusion:The ileal pull through procedure can be done without the burden of a covering stoma, and the abnormal stooling post pull through is expected to resolve with time and proper care.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 19G: A Novel Technique of Minilaparotomy for Massive pancreatic pseudocysts in Pediatric patients

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Ratendra Singh, Rahul Gupta

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Swai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : docrattu@gmail.com
Aim: To describe our novel technique of Minilaparotomy for Massive pancreatic pseudocysts in Pediatric patients
Method: All patients were optimized preoperatively. Pre op incision marking on the most prominent part of pseudocysts were done by ultrasound guidance.
Incision of about 4cm was made over the marked site. We make minilaparotomy and inserted a Deaver’s retractor and exteriorized the anterior wall of stomach after a tranverse gastrostomy and performed the procedure.
Result: We performed the above mentioned method in pseudopancreatic cyst patients. All the patients in this case series did not have any post operative complications and all were allowed orally in POD4 and discharged in stable condition at POD7. Conclusion: We concluded that the minilaparotomy for pseudopancreatic cyst is a good option and with minimal complications. This is a good minimally invasive and technically feasible option for pediatric patients with pseudopancreatic cysts.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 34G: Neonatal Gastric Perforation : Review of 5 years’ experience in a Tertiary Care Centre

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Deepak Goyal, Nitin Jain,Simmi K Ratan, Nitin Jain, Sujoy Neogi, Prafull Kumar, Chiranjeev Kumar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, New Delhi Email ID : kdnsrs@gmail.com
Aim: Gastric perforation among neonates is a rare but frequently fatal condition of uncertain aetiology . Although historically gastric perforation has often been described as “spontaneous”,some cases are non spontaneous. The aim of this study was to present the experience of managing gstric perforation at tertiary care centre over last five years.
Material and methods: It is a retrospective study analysing data of 13 neonates who were diagnoses with gastric perforation over a 5 year period(2015-2021). The variables noted included age,sex,birth weight , mode of delivery,sign and symptoms, associated anomalies,respiratory distress at birth,mechanical ventilation, site if perforation, total leukocyte count and clinical outcomes.
Results: In our series,there were 10 male and 3 female neonates with mean age of presentation as 18 days. Eight of them were born full term while 5 neonates were pre term . Mean birth weight was 2.1kg.
Most common anomaly was cardiac anomaly seen in 3 neonates and club foot with digital anomaly was seen in one neonate. The most common initial manifestations were abdominal distention 12(92.3%), fever 6 (46.2%) and vomiting 5 (38.5%). The overall mortality was 6 (46.2%). Most common site of gastric perforation was greater curvature 10 (76.9%). We have also seen that mortality was high among preterm neonates (80% mortality among all preterm neonates);those requiring mechanical ventilation in pre op period( 71.4% mortality among all neonates requiring ventilation); those with sepsis as suggested by low TLC counts(70% mortality among those with low TLC) and metabolic acidosis (80.33% mortality among those with metabolic acidosis); blood in stools(mortality in only neonate who presented with blood in stools) and those with perforation at lesser curvature (70% of those with perforation at lesser curvature).
Conclusion: In conclusion, neonatal gastric perforation is associated with high mortality . Those presenting preterm, requiring mechanical ventilation , with sepsis ,blood in stools/coffee ground vomitus and those with perforation at lesser curvature had worse outcomes.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 36G: Surgical outcomes in delayed primary closure of omphalocele major

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Vinay Prasad M H, Arun Kumar, Susan Jehangir, Ravi Kishore

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamilnadu Email ID : drvinayprasadmh@gmail.com
Background: Omphalocele major is a complex anomaly with varied management options. Primary phased closure in neonatal period is recommended but is not always possible due to patient factors or socio-economic reasons. In such scenarios, safer method is conservative care with initial epithelialization followed by a delayed closure of the ventral herniation.
Aim:The aim of the study was to analyse the surgical techniques and long term outcomes in children undergoing delayed primary repair of major omphalocele.
Methods: A retrospective review of all children who underwent delayed repair of omphalocele between 2010 - 2022 was done. Data was collected from electronic medical records and statistical analysis done.
Results: A total of 23 children consisting of 57% boys were included in the study. The mean age at surgery was 2.5 years with the oldest child being 6 years. Contrast CT was done in 65% of patients to assess the domain loss and vascular anatomy. Liver and small bowel are the most common contents. Primary closure was possible with 91% of the patients with staged repair done in the remaining. Absorbable sutures were used in 70% of cases. Mesh was used in four children with two of them suffering from mesh infection requiring removal. Elective post operative ventilation was required in four children. The mean length of hospital stay was 6.7 days. There were no recurrences and one mortality.
Conculsions: Primary closure of late presenting omphalocele without mesh is a feasible option with no significant morbidity. Staged repairs and elective ventilation may aid in select cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 48G: Role of biochemical markers in predicting the severity of acute appendicitis and correlation with histopathology in children: A prospective observational study

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Gaurav Sharma, Satyasree B, Intezar Ahmed, Enono Yhoshu, Rajat Piplani

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Rishikesh, Uttarakhand Email ID : sgaurav513@gmail.com
Objective: To evaluate the role of Biochemical markers in predicting the severity of acute appendicitis and its correlation with histopathology in children.
Materials and methods: A time bound prospective observational study was done in Department of pediatric surgery, All India Institute of medical sciences, Rishikesh. A total of 85 patients of age 2-18 years, diagnosed with acute appendicitis were included in this study. All patients were scored based on acute appendicitis inflammatory scoring (AIR score) and underwent ultrasound abdomen to diagnose it as complicated and uncomplicated appendicitis. In all cases irrespective ofdiagnosis complicated or uncomplicatedbaseline (Day 0) samples were sent for CRPH, Procalcitonin, Total leucocyte count, Neutrophil and Bilirubin. In non-operativegroupserial biochemical markers were followed up (Day 3, 5, 7….) to detect the progression of disease. In operative group before appendectomy baseline (Day 0) biochemical markerswere assessed and compared with histopathology findings.Telephonic follow-up was also done after 6 months in all patients of non-operative group and questions were asked about persisting symptoms (abdominal pain, fever and vomiting), need of analgesic and surgery done outside.
Results : In our study, baseline (Day-0) cut-off value evaluated for each biochemical marker in predicting complicated appendicitis were, CRPH (mg/L) (Baseline) ≥187, TLC (/mm³) (Baseline) ≥12700, Neutrophils (%) (Baseline) ≥74%, and bilirubin (mg/dL) (Baseline) ≤1.12.Best parameter in terms of AUROC is procalcitonin (ng/ml). In terms of sensitivity, TLC (/mm³) (Baseline) is the best parameter with sensitivity of 95% and Procalcitonin is the most specific with specificity of 69%. Among all parameter bilirubin baseline was least specific with specificity of 38.5% in predicting histopathology as necrosis. CRPH is best parameter in terms of diagnostic accuracy.
Conclusion: In already diagnosed case of appendicitis CRPH (C-reactive protein), Procalcitonin, TLC (total leucocyte count) and neutrophil count have significant role in predicting severity of acute appendicitis. They are also useful tools in guiding the non-operative management in uncomplicated and complicated appendicitis during the hospital stay.However, raised serum bilirubin is not consistent with severity of acute appendicitis.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 50G: Bowel mucormycosis: A retrospective study of surgical patients

Category : G.I.SURGERY - 3

Parveen Kumar, Mamta Sengar, Vivek Manchanda, Chhabi Ranu Gupta, Niyaz Khan, Arti Khatri

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : parveenkumar_maan@yahoo.co.in
Aims: To study clinical profile and outcome of surgical patients operated at our centre who had bowel mucormycosis on histology.
Methods: It was a retrospective observational study done at the department of Pediatric surgery,Chacha Nehru BalChikitasalaya, New Delhi. The data was collected from medical records department. The files of surgical patients with tissue diagnosis of bowel mucormycosis operated at our centre from June 2018 to May 2021 were retrieved. The clinical case files were screened for incidence, age, gender, clinical presentation, biochemical and microbiological investigations, surgery done, and outcome. The data was tabulated on Microsoft Excelsheet®. The clinical profile was calculated as mean, standard deviation, percentages as appropriate.
Results: A total of 11 surgical patients with bowel mucormycosis on histology were traced. The study had male preponderance. Majority were neonates and had low platelet counts with raised CRP levels. All but one expired and severe sepsis and septic shock were major cause of death.
Conclusion: The invasive mucormycosis is more common among neonates (but older children are not immune). The counts and sepsis screen may be normal, may affect any part of GIT. Early surgery should be done to confirm the diagnosis by HPE, and diversion should be done as against anastomosis. It has high fatality rate.

Observations: Skin temperature above 34 degrees C maintained in all 5 neonates

## ABSTRACT NO. : 111M: Two cases of disorder of sex development with novel NR5A1 mutation in a family

Category : MISCELLANEOUS - 2

Sonia Thakur, Rajeev Redkar, Anant Bangar, Rahul Deo Sharma, Sushma, Surendra Singh

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lilavati Hospital And Research Centre Bandra West, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : thakursonia2910@gmail.com
Background: NR5A1 (SF1) is a key transcriptional regulator of many genes involved in Hypothalamic – Pituitary – Gonadal axis and its mutation can result in 46 XY DSD.
AIM: Rare case of novel NR5A1 mutated XY DSD.
Case presentation: Two siblings, one 14-years-old female and another 12 years old female, presented with similar complaints i.e. history of primary amenorrhea. No other significant history. Conclusion: Rare cases of novel NR5A1 mutation with 46 XY karyotype.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 112M: Intramuscular Lipoma in Children: Unusual sites

Category : MISCELLANEOUS - 2

Anju Verma, Basant Kumar, Ankur Bhatnagar,Tarun Gupta, Priya Mathew, Pujana K.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : anju_v2006@rediffmail.com
Background: Lipomas are the most common type of soft tissue tumors. Intramuscular lipomas are rare subset that originates within the muscle, accounting for less than 1% of all lipomas. They commonly appear on anterior abdominal wall butalso seen on arm, chest, head, neck, and thigh.
The Aim of the study is to report the intramuscular lipomas, presented at unusual site and their management.
METHODS: In this study, we report 5 children having intramuscular lipoma of the lower limb presented between January 2018 to December 2022. We screened and retrospectively evaluated them regarding clinical, radiological characteristic and treatment.
RESULTS : Out of five patients, 3 were female and 2 were male with median age of 11 years (range from 4-17 years). All lipomas were in the lower limb, two in upper one third of lateral compartment of leg, one in upper one third of anterior compartment of leg, one in lower one third of lateral compartment of leg and one in distal half of sole. The average size of lipoma was7.5cm. All were slow growing, non-tender mass. The sole lipoma patient had difficulty in walking, rest 4 have cosmetic concern. None of them had any neurological deficit. Plain radiographs showed a radiolucent soft-tissue image in all cases. MRI done in all, which showed the site of origin, lipomatous nature and benign characteristic of the mass. All underwent wide local excision. Histopathology was suggestive of benign lipoma in all. They are doing well on follow up (ranged from 6 months–4 years) with no recurrence till now.
CONCLUSION: Intramuscular lipoma has infiltrating tendency along with high recurrence rate as compare to usual lipoma. Proper evaluation needed for deep seated lipomas to rule out any malignancy. Wide local excision is the treatment principle as like malignant tumor to reduce the recurrence rates.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 82M: Giant benign renal lump

Category : MISCELLANEOUS - 2

Rohit Lal, Vinit Kumar Thakur, Zaheer Hasan, Saurav Shrivastava

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indra Gandhi Institute Of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : rohitraj7492@gmail.com
Background: angiomyolipoma is a benign tumor consisting of vessels, muscle and fat. Its prevalence varies between 0.2-0.6% with strong female predilection. Tumor larger than 10 cms are referred as giant angiomyolipoma, incidence of which is very less.
Case presentation: here we report a rare case of giant angiomyolipoma, in a 11 year old female, who presented with a large abdominal lump from 1 year which was not associated with any symptoms. CECT-W/A reported mass arising from right kidney of size 22x10 cms.
The patient underwent a radical right nephrectomy. Postoperative histopathological examination confirmed the diagnosis of an angiomyolipoma.
Conclusion: the prognosis of angiomyolipoma is generally good if the tumor is small and symptoms are absent, conversely if tumor is large or presents with haemorrage outcomes are variable. Benign cases can be completely cured by radical nephrectomy. Recurrence can occur in some variant (epithelioid angiomyolipoma) therefore follow-up is needed.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 83M: Intussusception of Patent Vitello Intestinal Duct

Category : MISCELLANEOUS - 2

Rohit Lal, Zaheer Hasan, Vinit Kumar Thakur, Saurav Shrivastava

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Indra Gandhi Institute Of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : rohitraj7492@gmail.com
Background: A wide variety of anomalies may occur as a result of the vitellointestinal duct (VID) failing to obliterate completely. VID is well known because of its various complication and presentation most commonly due to Meckel’s diverticulum. Small bowel prolapsed through patent VID is one of the rare presentations with bowel segment that have been intussuscepted and prolapsed through umbilicus.
Case presentation: A 2 month old male baby, known case of patent vitello intestinal duct presented to emergency opd with prolapse of bowel from umbilicus for 1 day. Patient underwent exploratory laprotomy with excision of patent vitellointestinal duct with gangrenous bowel followed by end to end ileoileal anastamosis, post op period was uneventful and patient was discharged on 8th day.
Conclusion: intussusception (prolapsed) of bowel through patent vitellointestinal duct is a rare presentation of the disease, with grave prognosis because of the avascular necrosis of the bowel leading to gangrenous changes and needs immediate or urgent management.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 175T: Foreign bodies in bronchus : A single institute experience

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Purva Nirlesh Kothari, Rakesh S Joshi, Jaishri Ramji, Aniz Ratani

Department of Pediatric Surgery, BJ Medical College, Ahmedabad, Gujarat Email ID : prettypurva@gmail.com
Aim: Accidental inhalation of organic and non-organic foreign bodies ( FB) is a common cause of childhood morbidity and mortality. We describe here the clinical presentations and varieties of foreign bodies removed from the bronchus at our institute.
Method: We reviewed the records of 10 patients who were admitted in our department during a 4-yr period for the treatment of aspirated foreign bodies into the tracheobronchial tree.
Result: In the last four years, ten patients with foreign body aspiration were treated at our institute. Of these, 50% were male,50% were female and age ranging from 1yr to 10 yrs, presenting with coughing, dyspnoea and wheezing. The onset and duration of symptoms ranged from acute (2 days) to chronic (1 month). Positive elicitation of history was seen in 6 (60%) cases with X-ray evidence seen in 4(40%) patients. All patients underwent rigid bronchoscopy. Foreign bodies were localized in the right bronchus in 8 (80%) of the cases, the left in 1(10%) and trachea in 1(10%) case. Peanuts, gravel piece, LED bulb, earrings, nails, board pins were the various items removed. Foreign bodies were removed in all but 1 (10%) patient, who did not finish treatment in our hospital. Postoperative recovery was smooth.
Conclusion: Foreign body aspiration are rapidly recognized from the patient’s history and use of radiography and computerized tomography and easily treated by bronchoscopy and extraction. A high index of suspicion is crucial for early diagnosis. However, education is the best preventive measure for decreasing the incidence of this matter.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 72H: Innovatively crafted Polyglycolic Acid (Vicryl) Mesh for making hepatic snood as a savior for badly lacerated left lobe of Liver in a child with Blunt Trauma to Abdomen

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Simmi K Ratan, Nitin Jain, Sujoy Neogi, Nitesh Kumar Sharma, Rajib Bora, Deepak Goel

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, Delhi Email ID : drjohnsimmi@yahoo.com
Background: Though most liver injuries cab be managed conservatively but badly lacerated liver may call for hepatic resection or hepatic snood to control bleeding. Here in our case, we used innovatively handwrought mesh using polyglycolic acid (Vicryl) suture material as commercial absorbable mesh was not available; thus, avoiding major resection. The case is being described to suggest a simple workable alternative in pressing situations
Case Report: An 8-year-old boy had fall of portion of ceiling over epigastric region and the impact caused grade 4 liver laceration involving left lobe, perforation of body and antrum of underlying stomach as well as of transverse colon. CECT revealed pneumoperitoneum and gross hemoperitoneum At exploration approximately 1Litre of blood was present in peritoneal cavity and besides hollow visceral injuries a badly crushed, loosely hanging yet viable, gently oozing left lobe of liver was noted. Since direct suturing was not feasible and viability of liver precluded liver resection, hepatic snood option was considered but absorbable commercial mesh was not available. Hence using 1-0 vicryl sutures a mesh was woven using knots in zigzag manner. This mesh ensembled together the lacerated liver parenchyma The apex of snood was fixed to the diaphragm to give additional support to lacerated liver and achieve tamponade.
Repair of stomach perforation was done along with decompressive gastrostomy and ileostomy was fashioned. Oral feeding resumed on post-operative day 10. Small bile leak from liver surface got settled gradually as healing ensued and child was discharged uneventfully.
Conclusion: We conclude that in pressing circumstances self woven absorbable mesh fashioned as snood can work equally good as commercially viable mesh to achieve tamponade and provide support to lacerated liver. This can help to preserve nearly normal architecture and obviate need for hepatic resection

## ABSTRACT NO. : 26G: A rare presentation of a Mesenteric cyst causing volvulus in a malrotated intestine: A case report

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Purva Nirlesh Kothari, Jaishri Ramji, Rakesh S Joshi

Department of Pediatric Surgery, BJ Medical College, Ahmedabad, Gujarat Email ID : prettypurva@gmail.com
Aim: The association of Mesenteric cyst (a rare anomaly) and malrotation with volvulus ( a common anomaly) has been rarely reported. We present a case of malrotation with mesenteric cyst causing volvulus.
Method: A 3 month female infant presented with history of bilious vomiting, abdominal distension and constipation since four days, and had tachycardia and diffuse abdominal distension. X-ray abdomen showed a few air fluid levels. The ultrasound was suggestive of malrotation with midgut volvulus and duodenal obstruction.
Intraoperatively, there was classical malrotation of 180 degrees and a large mesenteric cyst in jejunum. The cyst was causing volvulus as the jejunal loops were twisted around it, without gangrene. Derotation with a half anti-clockwise turn, Ladd’s procedure and resection of mesenteric cyst with bowel was done with single layer jejuno-jejunal anastomosis.
Result and Discussion: Malrotation is common congenital anomaly with an incidence of 1: 6000 who have clinical symptoms. Mesenteric cyst is a rare anomaly with incidence of 1:100000. Concurrent presentation of both is extremely rare. Complete excision of the cyst, detorsion and Ladd’s procedure is the treatment of choice.
Conclusion: Mesenteric cysts in association with midgut volvulus and intestinal malrotation is a rare entity. The surgical management is most often based on intraoperative findings.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 251 O: Uterine cavity embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma in a pre-school girl

Category : CONSULTANT SESSION

Pradeep Kajal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Pt BDS PGIMSs, Rohtak, Haryana Email ID : drpradeepkajal@gmail.com

Introduction: Rhabdomyosarcoma is a malignant soft tissue sarcoma originating from primitive mesenchymal cells. It is a rare solid tumor in childhood and adolescence. The higher incidence is predominant in first 2 decades of life.
Case report: A 3-yr-old-female-child presented with complaints of bleeding per vaginum and a per-vaginal protrusion of mass for one and a half year that was gradually progressing in size. On abdominal examination, there was a huge lump in suprapubic quadrant reaching well above the umbilicus. MRI showed it to be arising from and involving whole of the uterus. Wertheim-Meigs hysterectomy was done. Histopathological examination of the specimen confirmed it to be embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma, botryoidal variantwhich originated in the uterine cervix with vaginal externalization.
Discussion: Rhabdomyosarcoma is mesenchymal in origin with cell of origin rhabdomyoblast that is an immature striated skeletal myocyte. The initial clue to diagnosis is often gross appearance of polyp or multiple polyp-like masses protruding from vagina along with serosanguinous vaginal discharge. According to WHO, rhabdomyosarcoma divided into four categories- Embryonal, Alveolar, Pleomorphic, Spindle/Sclerosing. Overall, the embryonal subtype is more common in young children with better prognosis and alveolar is second most common with worse prognosis.
Conclusion: Survival rate of rhabdomyosarcoma depends on site of origin, size, extent of disease residual diseases after treatment and histologic subtype. However, vaginal lesions have better prognosis than cervical lesion.

## Poster Presentation Session : “G.I.SURGERY”: Poster Presentation : “G.I.SURGERY”: ABSTRACT NO. : 6G: Small bowel volvulus with large mesentric defect in pediatric patient : An unusual presentation of acute abdomen

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Rohit Mittal, Vinita Chaturvedi, Neeraj Tuteja

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Swai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : haryanaboy123@gmail.com
Aim: An internal herniation with small bowel volvulus due to a mesenteric defect is a rare cause of intestinal obstruction, especially in children. In children, trans mesenteric hernias are thought to arise from a congenital defect in the small bowel mesentery near the ileocecal region or ligament of Treitz.
Method: We report a case of 2 year and 8 month old child who came to emergency department with complains of abdominal pain, vomiting (Bilious), abdominal distension and not passing stools since 2 days. On X- rays multiple air fluid levels suggestive of small bowel obstruction. On exploration there was a large mesentric defect present from mid to terminal ileum and a segment of distal ileum volvulus with gangrenous changes . Resection of gangrenous ileum was done and end ileostomy with distal mucous fistula done.
Results: In our case report patient presented with the features of intestinal obstruction. The precise cause of a bowel obstruction related to a mesenteric defect with an internal hernia can be established during surgery. Treatment is based on the operative findings. Gangrenous bowel should be resected with an end-to-end anastomosis to restore bowel continuity or stoma formation.
Conclusion: Radiographic findings suggestive of internal herniation are gaseous distension of the bowel loops in the upper abdominal area with a paucity of gas in the lower abdomen. Internal herniation should be considered in differential diagnoses of acute abdomen in the Paediatric Emergency Department. We emphasize that early recognition of internal herniation warrants further evaluation and appropriate management.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 9G: Idiopathic double-site intussusception in an infant: Historical perspective with a review of pertinent literature

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Monish Raj, Rahul Gupta, Arvind K Shukla, Ramesh Tanger

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Swai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : monishraj007@gmail.com
Aim : Double-site intussusception is an unusual variety of intussusception. There are only 16 paediatric patients reported in the literature to date. We report a case of double ileoileal intussusceptions in an infant
Methods: An 11-month-old baby girl presented with complaints of abdominal pain, vomiting, abdominal distension, not passing stool and flatus for the last 72 hours. There was a delay of more than 2 days before seeking medical advice. Abdominal examination revealed abdominal distension with an absence of a palpable lump. Abdominal radiographs revealed multiple air-fluid levels with a paucity of distal gas shadows. Ultrasound showed a single target sign in the right lumbar region. Because of the delayed presentation of the index case and with the diagnosis of ileoileal intussusception, non-operative management was not attempted.
Result: At laparotomy an ileoileal (mid-ileal portion, 15 cm) intussusception was detected. During manual reduction, it was noted that 2 separate intussusceptions were present. The distal one was also ileoileal (non-transient, 4-5 cm) at the terminal ileum with its apex at the ileocecal junction. Both intussusceptions were successfully reduced per-operatively. No pathological lead point was observed for each intussusception. The outcome was favourable and the child recovered well.
Conclusion: A careful medical history and clinical examination combined with radiological evaluation may help in the diagnosis of double-site intussusception, although it is difficult preoperatively, as seen in our case. Implementing emergency surgery and proper postoperative management will result in a positive outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 12G: Diabetic keto-acidosis with colonic necrosis and perforation peritonitis - Report of a rare case with review of literature

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Rohit Kapoor, Sunita Singh, Mritunjay Kumar, Rini Dixit

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences (AIIMS), Raebareli, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : rohit.kapoor13081988@gmail.com
Background and Aim: Abdominal pain is a common presentation in patients of diabetic ketoacidosis(DKA). However, this pain generally resolves with resolution of acidosis. Rarely, it may not resolve even after appropriate management of DKA which gives us clue of existence of an associated abdominal pathology. We present such a rare case of DKA with perforation peritonitis due to colonic ischaemia and the challenges we faced in diagnosis and management.
Method: A 15-year-old diabetic adolescent female patient presented to us with complaints of fever, abdominal distension, pain, dyspnoea and oliguria. Based on her clinical features and investigations patient was diagnosed as Insulin Dependent Diabetes Mellitus(IDDM) with DKA with perforation peritonitis and appropriate fluid resuscitation was started. Post initial medical stabilization exploratory laparotomy was done, which revealed necrotic left transverse colon, splenic flexure and descending colon without any volvulus with multiple perforations with approximately 300 ml pus in peritoneal cavity. Left hemicolectomy was performed with excision of the necrotic portion of transverse colon, descending colon and sigmoid colon with end transverse colostomy with closure of distal end.
Results: Postoperativey, patient was shifted to PICU and had a stormy course. Issues during PICU stay included

Worsening catecholamine resistant septic shock with multi organ dysfunction syndrome(MODS); Antibiotics were upgraded to inj Meropenam and Inj Vancomycin.
Persistent and worsening metabolic acidosis with high serum lactate levels and falling platelet counts(70000/cc)
Worsening hypoalbunemia(1.25 g/dl) and deranged PT/INR(36.5 secs/2.95)

However, despite all efforts and possible treatment, her condition detoriated and she expired on postoperative day 3.
Conclusions: Hospital course of these patients is generally stormy with high mortality and morbidity. A high index of suspicion is thus needed if DKA is associated with septic shock and shows suboptimal response to treatment. In such cases, source of sepsis should be properly investigated, and treatment should be tailored as per the etiology.

Method and materials : We report a case of a 1 year 6 months female child with complaint of abdominal Lump noted since 7 days, due to this reduced oral intake. Passing flatus and motion normally. No any history of vomiting, fever and coughing. On examination single, around 5*5 cm size non-tender lump is situated in left hypogastric region. Result: this paediatrics patients admitted with complain with abdominal lump. On examination lump is non-tender and present in left hypogastric regions. ## ABSTRACT NO. : 14G: Rare causes of intestinal obstruction in pediatric population

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Priya Shalini Lakra, A.K. Singh, S.S. Sahu, A. Ranjan, H. Birua

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Rajendra Institute Of Medical Sciences, Ranchi, Jharkhand Email ID : preahlakra@gmail.com
AIM: To study the infrequent or rare causes of intestinal obstruction in paediatric population in post neonatal age group
METHODS: It was a retrospective observational study done at RIMS, Ranchi from May 2020 to May 2023.Cases of Intestinal Obstruction occurring in Paediatric population in postneonatal age group were studied. Cases of intestinal obstructions due to non surgical causes were excluded, patients who left before treatment completion were also excluded.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 18G: The Lost Sibling: A Rare case report of Fetus in fetu

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Swai Man Singh Medical College, Jaipur, Rajasthan Email ID : docrattu@gmail.com
Introduction: Fetus in fetu differs from other teratomas in its rarity, due to the presence of other components within the tumour itself. They tend to present as retroperitoneal masses, with clinical presentation as an abdominal mass. We present a patient who was diagnosed with fetus in fetu at our institution, and our experience in the management of this patient.
Method: A 5 month old female child presenting with a gradually increasing abdominal distension, discomfort and respiratory difficulties was admitted in our institution. Conclusion: Fetus in Fetu is a rare teratoma and acn present as an abdominal lump. Full evaluation before proceeding to operation is necessary.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 24G: Intraluminal ectopic gastric mucosa: Rare cause of melena

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Pattu Pogula Jagadish, Vishesh Jain, Sandeep Agarwala, Rakesh Kumar, Lavleen Singh, Shamim Ahmed Shamin, Prabudh Goel, Anjan Dhua, Devendra K Yadav

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : pattu.jagadish@gmail.com
AIM: Ectopic gastric mucosa is a common cause of gastrointestinal bleeding in children. However, this ectopic mucosa is often seen in associated with a Meckel’s diverticulum or a duplication cyst. Presence of intraluminal ectopic gastric mucosa is rare and difficult to diagnose.
Method: An 8-year-old boy presented with black tarry stool for the past 5 years. On examination there was no lump palpable and per rectal examination was normal. On evaluation patient was anemic. Patient was evaluated with colonoscopy and UGIE which revealed a small rectal polyp. Episodes of melena continued after removal of polyp. Meckel’s scan (SPECT CT) was performed which revealed presence of ectopic gastric mucosa suggestive of intestinal duplication cyst. Diagnostic laparoscopy was performed which revealed no significant abnormality. A laparotomy was performed and after repeated careful examinations, thickening on mesenteric border of a segment of small bowel was noted. Resection of the abnormal segment and end to end anastomosis was performed. On gross examination of the opened bowel, linear hypertrophy of ileal mucosa was noted. The child was discharged on POD4. Histopathology was suggestive of ectopic gastric mucosa with normal margins at both ends. On follow-up of 2 months the patient was asymptomatic.
Conclusion: In patients with suspected ectopic gastric mucosa, if no definite cause is apparent, careful palpation and examination of bowel is necessary to find the abnormal intraluminal site.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 31G: Omental Cyst - how to diagnose

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Vijai Datta Upadhyaya, Pujana K, Basant Kumar, Tarun Kumar

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sanjay Gandhi Post Graduate Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow , Uttar Pradesh Email ID : upadhyayavj@rediffmail.com
Purpose: Omental cysts are rare intra-abdominal masses that occur primarily in children. They often present with symptoms similar to other more common abdominal pathologies and are discovered incidentally on surgery. There are certain soft point which will help in making diagnosis and to diffentiate this entity when it is very large from gross ascitis
Method: All the consecutive cases of suspected omental cyst managed in last 5 year were included. . All suspected cases were subjected to routine hematological & biochemical investigation. All patient with gross abdominal distension with history or evidence of : blunt trauma abdomen or tuberculosis or pancreatitis were excluded. After initial management all cases were subjected to Plain X-ray Abdomen, Ultrasound abdomen and CT scan abdomen with intravenous contrast after assessing the renal function test. Finally all suspected cases were explored under proper anesthesia to confirm the diagnosis and further management.
Results: In last 5 year 5 such cases were managed at our centre. 4 were female and one was male child. Age ranged from 1 year to 8 years. Preoperative diagnosis was made in 4 out of 5 cases. Differential diagnosis for this condition were tuberculosis/ gross ascitis of unknown cause are sometime difficult to rule out.
Conclusion: Giant omental cyst are very rare disease and are very difficult to diagnose , usually diagnosed at time of surgery but with high index of suspicion and meticulous evaluation of ultrasound and CT scan these can be diagnosed preoperatively.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 35G: Disconnected Pancreatic duct syndrome in Children: A case series

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Sugandha Agarwal, Nellai Krishnan, Sampreeti Mukherjee, Anjan Kr Dhua, Sachit Anand, Devendra Kr. Yadav, Minu Bajpai, Rohan Malik

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : vnsv14@gmail.com
Background and Aims: Disconnected Pancreatic duct syndrome (DPDS) is characterized by disruption of the main pancreatic duct as a sequelae of severe acute pancreatitis. The aim is to present our experience with this rare entity.
Methods: Retrospective review of patients of DPDS managed by paediatric surgery between January 2022 and May 2023.
Results: Patients were 9, 10, and 12 years respectively, with an M: F ratio of 2:1. The etiology of pancreatitis was a road traffic accident, bicycle handlebar injury, and idiopathic, respectively. DPDS presented concurrently with acute severe necrotising pancreatitis in all the patients. The pancreatic duct was disrupted between the body and tail in one, at the body in other patients. Peripancreatic fluid collection size ranged from 8x7 cm to 11x10 cm. A pigtail catheter was inserted in all the patients for fluid collection. Pigtail reinsertion was required in two patients due to accidental removal and recurrence of collection. Two patients had pleural effusion, one of which progressed to empyema, requiring decortication. The same patient developed a pseudoaneurysm of the gastro-duodenal artery causing a life-threatening gastrointestinal bleed managed by the intervention radiology team. Later this patient also required an internal drainage procedure for treating DPDS. The walled-off necrotic collection was drained into the jejunum using the Roux-en-Y technique. Endoscopic retrograde stenting was not attempted in any patient. All the patients are asymptomatic at the last follow-up (follow-up duration three months to 12 months).
Conclusion: DPDS is a rare challenging sequel to severe acute pancreatitis, requiring multidisciplinary management. Surgical management is required in select cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 37G: Moore Type-2 Duodenojejunal AV Malformation in an Infant with Recurrent GI Bleed - A Case Report

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Nidhi S Patil, Kulkarni K, Anjan Kr Dhua, Jain V, Goel P, Yadav D K, Agrawal S, Malik Rohan

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : nidhi9498@gmail.com
Background: Arteriovenous malformation (AVM) in the small intestine is rare and is reported to make up 2.3% of all gastrointestinal (GI) tract AVM. It is even rarer if the duodenojejunal region amongst children is considered. Our index case was an infant with Moore type 2 AVM; the report showcases the flexible surgical approach and its successful outcome.
Case Presentation: A 7-months-boy presented to the pediatric gastroenterology department with complaints of recurrent episodes of the passage of black, tarry stools for the past three months requiring multiple blood transfusions with one episode of hematemesis. Abdominal examination was normal. The laboratory findings revealed a hemoglobin level of 8.1mg/dl but a normal platelet count and prothrombin time. He underwent upper GI endoscopy, suggesting a large vascular malformation at the second part of the duodenum occupying half of the circumference. Meckel’s scan was found to be negative. USG suggested wall thickening of the D3 duodenum with multiple septae seen within with mild lumen narrowing. The child was posted for excision with a Whipple’s procedure. Intraoperatively there was a vascular malformation noted in the second part of the duodenum (3 mm distal and sparing the ampulla), the third and fourth part of the duodenum, and the proximal part of the jejunum. Resection and duodenojejunostomy were performed. Surgery was well tolerated by the patient. The postoperative and follow-up course (8 months) were uneventful. The histopathology report was consistent with an AVM.
Conclusion: Surgical management of AVM of intestinal origin can be rarely encountered in infants as a cause of blood loss anemia. Based on the anatomical involvement, a tailored and flexible surgical approach should be offered for an optimum outcome.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 39G: A rare case of isolated Congenital microgastria
