Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : sidharth.ganesuni@gmail.com
Introduction: Congenital Microgastria is a rare congenital anomaly of stomach with less than 100 cases reported in English literature. It is characterised by a small volume stomach with minimal functional capacity and is usually associated with other anomalies.
Case: A 5-month-old male, presented with complaints of recurrent episodes of cough and cold and post tussive vomiting since 2 months. He was born pre term was kept in NICU for the same. He was resuscitated and given respiratory support. On initial Ultrasonography he was misdiagnosed as Infantile Hypertrophic Pyloric Stenosis but on further investigation by upper GI contrast study which showed a coiled NG tube in dilated oesophagus and small stomach with contrast flowing well into pylorus and distally.
Result: Patient was discharged after treating respiratory distress on 2 hourly feeds through NG tube at home. He has been planned for elective surgery at a later date.
Conclusion: Non bilious post tussive vomiting in infants usually directs us to a diagnosis of infantile hypertrophic pyloric stenosis. The initial investigations may also be misleading. Rarer diagnosis like Congenital Microgastria, Pyloric atresia, antral webs, duplication cyst of the antropyloric region, and other medical conditions like metabolic disorders should also be kept in mind while managing such babies. In this case one can observe controlled smaller amounts of feeds is preventing aspiration thus relieving respiratory distress which is usually not seen infantile hypertrophic stenosis. Moreover, in cases of recurrent pneumonia or respiratory distress in infants. One should look for surgical causes of aspiration.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 42G: Mucus blocking fistula – Not so rare

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Bhanu Khokhar, Pinaki R. Debnath, Amita Sen, Atul Kumar Meena, Vijay Kundal, Shalu Shah, Tanmay Anand, Rishabh Patel, Babita Chhetri, A L Shabnoor, Supriya Kumari

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Atal Bihari Vajpayee Institute Of Medical Sciences & Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : bhanukhokhar3@gmail.com
Aims:Type C Trache-esophageal fistula (TEF) can often present like pure esophageal atresia when the lower pouch is blocked.
Methods:The initial X-ray shows absence of gastric air. In our case series of 2 such cases which were initially diagnosed with pure esophageal atresia based on initial x ray. Because of associated congenital heart defect and low birth weight it was decided to proceed with feeding gastrostomy and diversion cervical esophagostomy. However, when feeding gastrostomy was initiated it was observed that milk was oozing out of esophagostomy. Immediate upper GI dye fluoroscopy and dye study with dye in gastrostomy was done. This confirmed the presence of lower esophageal pouch being in communication with the trachea.
Results:It was concluded that the lower pouch fistula would have been blocked initially, mostly likely with a mucus plug which gave away when gastrostomy feed was started. In both cases a redo laparotomy was done and lower esophageal stump was ligated and closed. Both children are under follow up and being prepared for subsequent gastric pull up.
Conclusion:A pre operative bronchoscopy is bound to detect such hidden fistula and prevent such complications.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 46G: Meckel’s Diverticulum : Timely and tailored approach

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Kriti Mittal, Satish Kumar Aggarwal, Chandrika Kalagotla, Garvita, Varsha Verma, Shubham, Clovey Singhal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, Delhi Email ID : kriti.genius@gmail.com
Introduction: Meckel’s diverticulum can present in many ways, exanguinating bleed being most scary. It may be a cause for intestinal obstruction, internal herniation and sometimes with inflammatory phlegmon. Quick diagnosis and planned management is the key to success. We present an experience of 26 cases highlighting the importance of laparoscopic approach for diagnosis and excision.
Materials and Methods: Medical records of 26 cases (5 females, 21 males) operated for Meckel’s over two years were reviewed. , age ranging from 8 weeks to 13 years, evaluated with USG, CECT, Meckel’s scan and diagnostic laparoscopy, and some even required gastrograffin enema study, colonoscopy, MRI, Barium meal follow through and UGI endoscopy where diagnosis was misled due to associations of other anomalies.
Cases details: Age range: 8 weeks to 13 years. Seven cases presented with exanguinating hemorrhage, 3 with intestinal obstruction, 10 with acute inflammatory process and 6 incidentally detected. Laparoscopy assisted resection of diverticulum with adjacent ileum was the most common surgery.
Conclusion: Early laparoscopy is helpful in saving time for cases presenting with bleed. Meckel scan may help but is not necessary in acute bleeds. Whether to do a wedge resection or complete resection with ileum is debatable.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 52G: Gastric teratoma in infancy: Our experience with 5 cases

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Manita Tamang, Aditya Manekar, Manoj Kumar Mohanty, Subrat Kumar Sahoo, Bikasha Bihary Tripathy, Santosh Mahallik, Mukund Sable, Sonali Mohapatra, Debasish Sahoo

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Science, Bhubaneswar, Odisha Email ID : manitatamang1992@gmail.com
Background: Teratomas are germ cell tumours composed of tissues derived from ectoderm, endoderm and mesoderm and have been described in various locations either as gonadal or extra-gonadal. Gastric teratoma is a rare tumour, accounting for less than 1 % of all teratomas in infants & children. However, these rare tumors have excellent prognosis after a curative resection.
Materials and Methods: We retrospectively collected the data of all cases of gastric teratoma managed at our institute over a period of 4 years (Jan 2018 to Jan 2022).
Results: A total of 5 cases were included. All patients except one were females, with a minimum age of 2 months at presentation and a maximum age of 12 years. The mean AFP level was 229ng/ml. Lesser curvature of the stomach was the most common location of the tumor seen in four cases with posterior wall of the stomach in one case on imaging. All patients underwent excision of the mass with a rim of gastric wall, one patient requiring a repair of GE junction. Grossly, the lesion appeared as a solid and cystic teratoma with histopathology showing a mature cystic teratoma most commonly. The cases showed no recurrence on followup over a period of 1 year.
Conclusion: Gastric teratomas are the rare causes of abdominal lump in paediatric patients. Mostly, they are benign and surgical excision is curative.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 54G: Intestinal malrotation in older children is more missed than found- case series of five consecutive cases.

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Tanvir Roshan Khan, Shrikesh Singh, Anand Dixit, Saurabh Srivastav

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia Institute Of Medical Sciences, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : doctrk@gmail.com
Aims: Intestinal malrotation is a congenital disorder of rotation and fixation. Majority of the cases present in neonatal period and infancy. Its presentation beyond this period raises suspicion of ignorance or inability to diagnose thisdisorder. The present case series identifies the back-to-back presentation of five cases of intestinal malrotation with different presentations in which the diagnosis was delayed increasing the morbidity.
Methods: It’s a prospective study being conducted with an aim to identify any cases of intestinal malrotation referred to our OPD or being treated in medicine department who have been treated symptomatically for pain abdomen or vomiting for period of more than a year without Paediatric surgical consultations.
Results: A total of 5 patients were diagnosed of having intestinal malrotation either referred by the Pediatrician or visited our OPD due to other reasons in a year. These patients had been symptomatic for more than one year and were labelled as chronic abdominal pain and put on various pain killers. bilious vomiting was present in all patients at some point of time. However, diagnosis of malrotation was not considered in all these patients before coming to us. All patients were further investigated and treated surgically. They all were discharged and followed-up.
Conclusion: Despite advancement in medical education and various diagnostic tools, intestinal malrotation continues to intrigue us. The older children continue to be treated symptomatically for abdominal pain without being evaluated for intestinal malrotation. An awareness about this serious disorder is needed to diagnose these conditions at early stages to prevent morbidity and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 58G: Laparoscopic fundoplication in small infants: Our experience

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Ananya Kapoor, Manish Pathak

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan Email ID : ananyakapoor0311@gmail.com

Laparoscopic Nissen’s fundoplication has become the gold standard for the management of High grade or nonresponsive GER. We share our experience of laparoscopic fundoplication in infants with high-grade GER.
Case details : Two infants weighing 2.1 and 2.3 kg underwent Nissen fundoplication for severe GER.
Both patients were admitted to the pediatric medicine department for recurrent apneic episodes and pneumonia requiring mechanical ventilation. The Milk scan demonstrated high-grade GER. Case 1 was neurologically impaired and suspected to have an inborn error of metabolism. The second baby had associated laryngo-tracheo-bronchomalacia.
Both patients underwent laparoscopic Nissen fundoplication.
Five ports were inserted-5 mm infra umbilical for the camera, 3 mm ports in the left and right lumbar areas for instrumentation, 3 mm port in the left hypochondrium along the midclavicular line, just below costal margin for retraction of the stomach, 3 mm port in the epigastrium for liver retraction and floppy Nissen fundoplication was done successfully.
Postoperative course: Both babies started accepting feeds from POD1 without any regurgitation. Patient 1 developed VAP requiring prolonged ventilatory support, parents took the child home against medical advice. The second baby later underwent epiglottoplasty for severe tracheomalacia.
Conclusion: Laparoscopic fundoplication is feasible and can be performed safely and effectively in small infants.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 59G: Foreign Body (FB) retrieval: To do or not to do

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Kriti Mittal, Alpana Prasad, Chandrika Kalagotla, Garvita Singh, Varsha Verma, Shubham, Clovey Singhal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, Delhi Email ID : ananyakapoor0311@gmail.com
Introduction: Oral exploration of environment by infants often leads to accidental FB ingestion. Although majority pass out spontaneously, once it has gone beyond the cricopharynx. Rarely endoscopy and laparotomy may be required for their removal specially in infants and children. We report three such cases where surgical intervention was necessitated.
Materials and Methods: 3 cases of FB ingestion with unusual clinical course; first a case of chickpea ingestion stuck in mid esophagus; second a case of hair pin getting impacted at pylorus and third with button battery ingestion presenting with perforation peritonitis, will be discussed.
Cases details: First, a 16 month old girl, presenting with recurrent non-bilious, non-projectile vomiting after feeds for 15 days, with normal USG, NG in-situ, past history of betel nut ingestion and it’s successful endoscopic retrieval, FB in mid esophagus on gastrograffin study, underwent UGI endoscopic retrieval, revealing a tight stricture beneath it, and few surrounding ulcers, requiring gastrostomy, and subsequent serial bougie-dilatations. Second, an asymptomatic 5 month old boy with history of accidentally ingesting hair pin showing no distal movement on serial X-rays, requiring surgical removal, following a failed endoscopic retrieval as it was found impacted in duodenum. Third, a 5 year old boy who presented with perforation peritonitis following button battery ingestion who required laparotomy, closure of gastric perforation and stamm gastrostomy.
Conclusion: Children with history of foreign body ingestion need individualized approach. Early surgical removal prevents complications in impacted foreign bodies after failed endoscopic retrieval.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 61G: Unusual presentation of Rapunzel syndrome.. recurrent intussusception and gastric intestinal bezoar managed by laparoscopic assisted retrieval

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Satish Kumar K. V., Asha Thomas, Rachel, Nisha Girish

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Baptist Hospital, Bengaluru, Karnataka Email ID : satishpushpa33@gmail.com
Background : Gastrointestinal bezoars are a rare cause of intestinal obstruction in children. They are most often seen in mentally challenged children, but also known to occur in psychologically normal children. A 3 year old developmentally normal child with a gastro intestinal bezoar, presenting with intussusception is presented.
Methods: A 3 year old boy with history of Pica, presented with recurrent
Episodes of loose stools and vomiting for 2 weeks. Initial ultrasound showed transient jejunojejunalintussusception, which was managed conservatively. He represented to ER with sub acute intestinal obstruction and was resuscitated. Ultrasound showed no evidence of intussusception and a CECT was suggestive of long segment jejunojejunal intussusception with no lead point and evidence of gastric bezoar. He was subjected to laparoscopy.
Result: Initial laparoscopy showed long segment jejunojejunalintussuscuscption, which couldn’t be reduced laparoscopically as the lead point was due to bowstring effect of trichophytobezoar. The stomach was pulled out through the widened umbilicalport and scope was passed through gastrostomy to confirm gastric bezoar. The bezoar was completely removed from stomach along with its extension in duodenum. The stomach was closed extracorporeally and the jejunum was eviscerated – jejunotomy and removal of long lenth of bezoar was removed and intussusception rsolved. The jejunotomy was closed and wound closed.
Conclusion: Gastrointestinal bezoars presenting with jejunalintussuception is rare in children. Laparoscopy aids in diagnosis and laparoscopic assisted removal can be successful even in small children. A psyschological evaluation and psychotherapy is essential to prevent recurrent bezoars.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 253G: Colonic Atresia - A Rare entity

Category : G.I.SURGERY

Ankur Deshwali, Maneesh Joleya, RM Shukla, BK Lahoti, Manoj Joshi, SS Sharma, AK Laddha, Pooja Tiwari, Vinod Raj

Department of Pediatric Surgery, MGMMC, Indore, Madhya Pradesh Email ID : joshimanojkr32@gmail.com
Background: Colonic atresia (CA) is a rare cause of bowel obstruction in neonates. The present study was performed on CA cases to discuss its surgical management, association with Hirschsprung’s disease and its outcome.
Method: We retrospectively reviewed CA cases from 1st January 2017 to 31st December 2021.
Result: The study reviewed 7 new-borns with CA treated in our Centre (5 cases of type III, 2 cases of type II) with 1 having associated anorectal malformation (ARM). Initial surgical treatment of CA was done by performing a proximal stoma and distal mucous fistula and sending a biopsy above the peritoneal reflection to rule out Hirschsprung’s disease.
Conclusion: Due to the rarity of colonic atresia its diagnosis and management is a big challenge. Staged surgery by making a diversion colostomy first and then definitive procedure after ruling out Hirschsprung’s disease should be the rule. Association of Hirschsprung’s disease with CA is usually picked up after failure of primary anastomosis leading to high morbidity and mortality. Dye study (contrast enema) is very useful to clinch the diagnosis of CA.

## Poster Presentation Session : “MISCELLANEOUS SESSION”: Poster Presentation : “MISCELLANEOUS SESSION”: ABSTRACT NO. : 84M: Type-II NSTI with secondary fulminant fungal pneumonia in an infant

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Ankit Kasundra, Amit Gupta, Sujeet Kumar, Reyaz Ahmad

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Bhopal, Madhya Pradesh Email ID : drkasupdumc@gmail.com
INTRODUCTION: Type-II NSTIs affect mostly young individuals without underlying conditions with a recent history of trauma. Methicillin-resistant S. aureus (MRSA) NSTIs has become the second major species responsible for type-II NSTIs. There is an increased risk of late secondary pneumonia, which may be fulminant and lethal.
OBJECTIVES: To highlight the importance of close and vigilant monitoring with serial X-ray chest for early detection of evolving late secondary pneumonia during treatment of type-II NSTI.
METHODS: An infant girl presented with NSTI of chest and abdominal wall following trauma. The patient was lethargic in a state of septic shock, anemia, thrombocytopenia, liver dysfunction and acute kidney injury. She had tachypnoea but no other signs of respiratory distress. A bedside X-ray chest planned at admission could not be done due to non-availability of the facility. There was a significant improvement over 1-week with appropriate medical and surgical management. Tachypnoea also settled with no clinical indication for an X-ray chest. MRSA was grown in culture of the sample from surgically debrided tissue leading to a final diagnosis of Type-II NSTI.
RESULTS: The patient developed acute respiratory failure on day-7 and succumbed within 24-hours to fulminant pneumonia on two serial X-ray chest at an interval of 12-hours. Budding yeast cells grown in blood culture were finally identified as Candida albicans indicating secondary “fungal” pneumonia.
CONCLUSIONS: The release of exotoxins causes severe immune dysfunction in a previously healthy patient with type-II NSTI. A high index of suspicion may help in the early detection of late secondary pneumonia which may be fulminant.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 88M: Should we do ultrasonography in undescended testis?

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Gaurav Jha, Mamta Sengar, Vivek Manchanda, Ankur Bhardwaj

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : gauravjha6@gmail.com
Introduction: Undescended testis (UDT) is one of the most common congenital anomalies . Various associations have issued guidelines for surgery before child completes first year of life in order to preserve maximum fertility potential. But, the surgery is often delayed. After infancy, not only the fertility of the boy is believed to be affected but also the diagnosis of malignancy in intra-abdominal testis is often missed or delayed affecting the prognosis of the child.
Method: Case Report: A 2-year term baby presenting with right side UDT for 2 months with pain abdomen for 3 days. On examination, right scrotum was empty with palpable mass in right lumbar region. The child was explored to find torsion in testicular tumour. The histopathology confirmed yolk sac tumour.
Discussion: Probability of development of malignancy in UDT is 40% more than that of normal testis. Such a malignancy is frequently seminoma (74%) and is seen in third decade of life. However, the childhood yolk sac tumour can occur in intra-abdominal testis much earlier. In our case, the tumour reached large size before diagnosis because of its intra-abdominal location.
Conclusion: Due to poor sensitivity of USG to localize an intra-abdominal testis, the guidelines go against USG in boys with non-palpable UDT. However, if we are not able to operate the child before 1 year of age, we should follow the patients for occurrence of malignancy in intra-abdominal testis during the waiting period by clinical abdominal examination and USG.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 90M: Vaginal Atresia - Management options

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

K. R. Reshma, Beena S. V, Aravind C. S, Liya Joseph, Nidhi T. R., Sethunath S. S.

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Government Medical College, Trivandrum, Kerala Email ID : krreshmak@gmail.com
Aim: To describe the varied management of three cases of vaginal atresia in a tertiary care hospital in Kerala.
Method: Descriptive study-case series
CASE 1: 8 year old child with oro-facial-digital syndrome presented with pyocolpos. On evaluation, she was found to have absent lower third of vagina. She underwent pull-through vaginoplasty with placement of 20Fr Foleys catheter. Tract was dilated and the catheter changed monthly. After 6 months, the Foleys catheter was removed.
CASE 2 : 10year old child with Trisomy21 presented with severe abdominal pain. MRI showed haematometra with absent upper 2/3rd of vagina. Perineal approach through the vaginal dimple failed to drain haematometra. Hence proceeded with laparoscopy-assisted evacuation of haematometra which provided her temporary pain relief. The child was started on hormonal therapy for menstrual suppression. However, she continued to have episodes of severe pain. Gynaecology opinion was sought and a conclusion of cervical atresia along with vaginal atresia was made and proceeded with hysterectomy.
CASE 3: 18year old, evaluated for primary amenorrhoea, was found to have Mayer-Rokitansky-Kuster-Hauser syndrome, associated with vaginal atresia, hypoplastic uterus and normal ovaries. Secondary sexual characters were well developed. She underwent ileal vaginoplasty.
Conclusions: Varied management of vaginal atresia is described here, suited to the particular case scenario. Modification of pull-through vaginoplasty with placement of Foleys catheter is a simple procedure that can be done in limited settings. Hysterectomy done for the second patient resulted in drastic alleviation of pain and improved quality of life. Vaginal replacement done for the third patient was mainly for sexual function.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 92M: Double Trouble

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Ashwin Rajkumar J., Prakash Agarwal, Madhu R., Jegadeesh, Selvapriya

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sri Ramachandra Institute Of Higher Education And Research, Chennai, Tamilnadu Email ID : ashwindoc1994@gmail.com
Background: Hydatid disease is an anthropozoonotic disease caused by parasite, Echinococcus Granulosa, characterized by cystic lesion in the lung, liver, spleen and other parts of the body. In children, usually the lungs are commonly affected and liver in adults. Pediatric hepatic hydatid cysts are uncommon.
Case report: 15-year-old male came with complaints of haemoptysis for 2 years duration. Computed Tomography of Thorax and Ultrasound abdomen findings were suggestive of hydatid Disease of the left lower lung and segment 6 liver. Laparoscopic management of hepatic hydatid cyst was done in a safe, effective and controlled technique. PAIR technique ( Hypertonic Saline and 10% Betadine ) with partial pericystectomy, removal of the germinal layer using an endobag and obliteration of the cavity with omentum done laparoscopically. Hydatid Lung cyst was tackled via Left Posterolateral thoracotomy with left lower lobectomy in view of adherent nature of the lung cyst with possible cystobronchial communication. Anaphylaxis was treated with steroids and antihelminthics postoperatively. Patient was weaned off ICU care and discharged subsequently with no recurrence on follow up.
Minimally invasive laparoscopic techniques are feasible, safe and effective surgical technique as demonstrated in managing the Hepatic Hydatid cyst in the segment 6 of liver. In view of difficult access, long standing, dense adhesions, large cyst with cysto -bronchial communication – anatomical resection of the left lower lobe lung was done via open thoracotomy as standard surgical approach.
Conclusion: Proper preoperative evaluation, preparation and radiological imaging required for management of Hydatid disease affecting multiple organs in reducing morbidity. Minimally invasive techniques as safe as standard open surgical techniques. In case of intraoperative complications – resortment to standard approach should not be delayed. Antihelminthic pharmacotherapy with surgical treatment brings about the best results in such complex cases affecting pediatric population.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 96M: A Challenging Encounter: Cold Abscess Staphylococcus Infection with Neck Swelling Unveiling a Possible Immunodeficiency Connection

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Sauradeep Dey, Mamta Sengar, Niyaz Khan, Vivek Manchanda, Arpita Chattopadhay, Ashvin R. Damdoo, Ankur Bharadwaj

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Chacha Nehru Bal Chikitsalaya, Delhi Email ID : deysauradeep@gmail.com
Background: Primary immunodeficiency is a rare congenital condition. Children with primary immunodeficiency are predisposed to infections of skin and deep tissues, often due to unusual or resistant micro-organisms.

Case summary:

A 2-month-old female presented with history of progressively increasing right-sided neck swelling for one week, without any accompanying fever. Swelling was non-tender. Over 24 hours, patient developed worsening stridor with tachypnoea, with oedema over face and right arm. Body temperature measurements were normal.
Serial complete blood count revealed persistent leukopenia. CT Scan of neck revealed collections of fluid-density in multiple soft-tissue spaces on right side of neck and so, surgical decompression was planned. Thin serous fluid was drained. Difficult endotracheal intubation was encountered during surgery and patient remained intubated with ventilator support for one day post-operatively. Neck swelling resolved and facial and arm oedema decreased thereafter and patient was extubated after 48 hours.
Drain-fluid culture revealed mixed growth of methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA), Acinetobacter baumannii and Escherichia Coli. Tracheal aspirate had growth of Stenotrophomonas maltophilia.
Presence of clear fluid (instead of pus) and absence of clinical signs of inflammation supported the diagnosis of immunodeficiency. Detailed past-history taking revealed delayed falling of umbilical cord, history of consanguinity among parents and history of gluteal and leg abscesses one month back, which were missed earlier. Workup revealed HIV negative status, low IgA and IgG levels, decreased CD4 and CD8 cells. Genome sequencing came positive for MYD88 Pathogenic Gene (Immunodeficiency – 68)

Conclusion: Primary immunodeficiency should always be suspected whenever a young child presents with history of recurrent multiple-site abscesses without any clinical signs of inflammation, with poor response to usual antibiotics. Because of the rarity of this condition, delayed / missed diagnosis is common, which can lead to significant morbidity and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 99M: Extensive corrosive stricture of esophagus: Can esophageal replacement be avoided??

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Shubham, Alpana Prasad, Chandrika Kalgotla, Garvita Singh, Varsha Verma, Kriti Mittal, Clovey Singhal

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Gangaram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : shubhamkumar24058@gmail.com
Abstract body: Introduction: Accidental ingestion of a corrosive substance is a common cause of esophageal strictures in children. Management of these strictures may require extensive surgery like esophageal replacement. Combined antegrade and retrograde bougie dilatation is a safe and effective modality of treatment.
Materials and Methods: Medical Record of a patient reviewed over a period of 21 months (April 2019 to Dec 2021).
Cases details: we present a case of a a 6 year old boy admitted in PICU with respiratory distress following accidental corrosive ingestion 4 days back and required emergency Tracheostomy. He also underwent gastrostomy and feeding jejunostomy in view of extensive ulceration of pharyngeal and laryngeal inlet. Initially he was managed by pediatric gastroenterologist for 9 months with endoscopic dilatations without any significant improvement. Later he was referred to paediatric surgery and he underwent combined antegrade and retrograde bougie dilatation for next 6 months. This resulted in significant improvement in esophageal caliber. Presently child is under regular follow up for more than 2 years , he is accepting normal diet and thriving well.
Conclusion: Management of esophageal stricture after corrosive ingestion is difficult but it can be effectively managed by combined antegrade and retrograde Bougie dilatation. The optimal frequency and duration of dilatation needs to be individualized.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 100M: Unusual presentations of teratoma - Report of two cases

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Piyush Kumar, Nitin Pant, Anand Pandey, Sudhir Singh, J.D Rawat

Department of Pediatric Surgery, King George’s Medical University Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : drpiyushkashyapms@gmail.com
Aim: To report unusual clinical presentations of teratoma.
Method : Retrospective review of teratoma treated over 5 years. Two unusual cases of teratoma are highlighted.
Result: Case 1: 3-year-old male child presented with a congenital, brightly red, firm, pedunculated, gradually progressing mass in the perineum along with a perineal hypospadias. The anus was morphologically normal. The mass extended from behind the perineal meatus till just anterior to the anus. MRI suggested a soft tissue mass with extension onto the retro vesicle space with sparing of the rectum. It was completely excised through the anterior sagittal approach. Histology suggested a teratoma.
Case 2: Another male child underwent a colostomy for anorectal stenosis at 25 days of life. A subsequent distal loopogram also suggested a high anorectal defect. Hence a posterior sagittal pull-through was planned. Intraoperatively, we encountered a small mass of 2X2 cm. It was present at the site of rectal narrowing containing cheesy material and hair. Complete excision of the mass, excision of atretic rectum with rectal pull-through was done. Even though the distal loopogram was suggestive of a normal sacrum we discovered a median sacral defect intraoperatively. Histology suggested a dermoid cyst.
Conclusion : Even though both these teratomas were benign and present in male babies, they demonstrated a marked variability in their clinical presentation with one apparent in the perineum and the other hidden in the pre-sacral space to be discovered intraoperatively.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 101M: Extracorporeal testicular ectopia through femoral region: a variant of scrotoschisis - A rarity

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

B. Keerthana, Shreyas Dudhani, Bijay Kumar Suman, Amit Kumar, Amit Kumar Sinha

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Patna, Bihar Email ID : keerthana.bachala@gmail.com
Aims (Introduction): Scrotoschisis is an extremely rare congenital defect in which the testis eviscerates through an opening high on the anterior wall of the scrotum close to the median raphe.
Methods (case report): A 2-year-old male child presented to us with absent left testis in scrotum since birth. He was a full term male, born via normal vaginal delivery with uneventful antenatal history. Parents noted a pinkish white globular mass in medial aspect of left thigh. (figure1) The parents sought medical care at a local practitioner who advised a surgical consultation but due to restrained financial sources the parents could not follow it up. At the time of presentation (2 years old) he had a 2 x 2 cm, firm, subcutaneous swelling located on the medial aspect of the left thigh. It was lying in subcutaneous plane; had restricted mobility. Skin at the site was puckered. Scrotum was poorly developed on left side. Right testis was normal and located in right hemi-scrotum. Ultrasonography was suggestive of left ectopic testis of size 1 x 1.2cm in the femoral region. On Inguinal exploration, the testis was traced using the cord structures, dissected off its attachment to subcutaneous tissue and orchidopexy was done. Postoperative period was uneventful.
In our case the term scrotoschisis will not be appropriate as testicular extrusion has occurred through femoral region and not the scrotum. This can be considered as extracorporeal testicular ectopia or bubonoschisis, respective to the location of the defect.
Conclusion: We are reporting a case of extra corporeal testicular ectopia through the femoral region for the first time.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 102M: Blue rubber bleb nevus syndrome– An Uncommon entity

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Chimmani Sai Kiran, J K Mahajan, Komal Aggarwal, Shailesh Solanki

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute Of Medical Education And Research, Chandigarh Email ID : kingkiran1521@gmail.com
Background: Blue rubber bleb nevus syndrome is a rare clinical entity characterized by veno-lymphatic malformations, mainly involving the skin and digestive tract. It is a sporadic malformation that could be familial with an autosomal dominant inheritance. The diagnosis is based on suggestive clinical features and Imaging for the visceral lesions.
Case details: A 5-month-old female child presented with multiple bluish papules, macules, and nodules present, all over the body since birth. The lesions mainly involved palms, soles and trunk and it was associated with overgrowth of index and middle fingers of the right hand. On examination, there were multiple non -tender soft to firm, compressible, non-pulsatile, discrete nodules, and subcutaneous swellings of 0.1 cm to 2 cm in size. A large pedunculated bluish lesion involving the ventral aspect of right hand was seen to be arising from thumb, 2nd and 3rd fingers and extending on to dorsum of the hand. Complete blood counts, ultrasound abdomen and cranium were within normal limits and doppler of the right upper limb showed multiple clusters of anechoic /hypoechoic channels predominantly venous flow within the channels. Contrast enhanced MRI of right forearm and hand showed low-flow vascular malformation with no hypertrophied artery or draining vein in an infiltrative multilobulated lesion involving and replacing the soft tissue of right hand and forearm upto the level of elbow. Initially the patient was treated with propranolol without any success. As the lesion evolved, sirolimus at a dose of 0.5 - 2mg/m2 was started after a multidisciplinary discussion and lesions showed significant decrease within 2 months. Patient is continuing sirolimus and on follow up.
Conclusion: Due to its complexity and spectrum of multiple organ involvement, management of Blue rubber bleb nevus syndrome is difficult. Multi-disciplinary approach is required to reach the diagnosis and plan treatment. Sirolimus may be useful as first line of management.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 109M: A rare case of Fetus in Fetu : A Mature Cystic Teratoma

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Manita Tamang, Aditya A, Manekar, Manoj Kumar Mohanty, Bikash B Tripathy, Subrat Kumar Sahoo

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Bhubaneswar, Odisha Email ID : manitatamang1992@gmail.com
Fetus in fetu (FIF) is a rare (1/500,000 live births) cause of abdominal mass in children.In 1954, Lord claimed the presence of vertebral column and extremities and organs located atappropriate places around it as the basic diagnostic for FIF, which is still considered valid to a wideextent. The preoperative diagnosis of FIF is based on the observation of vertebral column or limbs in amass on imaging modalities. The presence of vertebral bodies reflects that FIF is a derivation fromprimitive streak. The formation of primitive streak and gastrulation starts during 3rd week that leads tonotochordformation and subsequently the vertebral column. In contrast, a teratoma consists ofpluripotent cells, without organogenesis or vertebral segmentation. The possibleassociation betweenFIF and highly differentiated teratoma is still controversial. It is important to differentiate betweenretroperitoneal teratomas and retroperitoneal FIF because the former have more than 10% malignancyrate.
We report a rare case of a five-month female patient, with abdominal lump, gradually increasing in sizefirst noticed by the mother at one month of age, referred to us after getting investigated elsewhere.CECT suggested a heterogenous well-circumscribed soft tissue mass approximately 10.5x11.5x11.3cm in the retroperitoneum with multiple bony densities resembling well organised vertebral column,ribs, scapula, extremity bones and deformed skull, enhanced bowel loops surrounded bynon-enhanced soft tissue with a branch of infra renal aorta traversing into it at L4 vertebrae level,displacing surrounding structures. The AFP levels was elevated, whereas, bHCG and CEA levels werenormal. A 15x11x10cm smooth walled mass in the left retroperitoneal space displacing surrounding
structures was excised, along with its capsule and the remnant at the bed of tumor was cauterised.
Post-operative period was uneventful. Post-operative AFP levels were normal and histopathologyrevealed a mature cystic teratoma.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 115M: CLOVES Congenital lipomatous overgrowth with associated vascular malformation

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Josy Thomas, Jai Kumar Mahajan, Khyati Kiran, Janap Reddy

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Postgraduate Institute Of Medical Education And Research, Chandigarh Email ID : josy.thomas@hotmail.com
Background: CLOVES syndrome is congenital lipomatous overgrowth with vascular malformations, epidermal nevi, and skeletal/spinal abnormalities. Truncal overgrowth or macrodactyly helps differentiate CLOVES from other overgrowth syndromes. Multisite involvement poses management dilemmas. We present one such case with staged correction.
Method: A 5-year-old girl presented to our center with multiple large lipomas over the lower abdomen, lower back, and right chest wall along with a large vascular malformation over the left anterolateral chest wall extending from midline anteriorly to medial border of Left scapula. She underwent surgical resection of the giant lipoma over the left lower back which was extending up to the anterior abdominal wall. The patient had an uneventful postoperative recovery and is on regular follow up. Further steps in staged management would include CT angiography for left chest wall lesion and embolization, further staged surgical excision of lipoma, for physical rehabilitation and aesthetic reconstruction, screening for Wilms’ tumor.
Conclusions: Management of complex multisystem malformations such as CLOVES syndrome is challenging due to the heterogeneity and rarity of the condition.Staged intervention maybe safe to improve the quality of life of such children.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 117M: Parosteal lipoma of ribs in a child: A rare case report

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Manita Tamang, Santosh Mahallik, Manoj Kumar Mohanty, Aditya A Manekar, Bikasha B Tripathy, Subrat K Sahoo

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Bhubaneswar, Odisha
Email ID : manitatamang1992@gmail.com
Parosteal lipomas are rare benign tumors,arising from the mature adipose tissue. They account for less than 0.3% of all lipomatous lesion.It’s been reported in the age ranging from 3 months to 8th decade; however, are typically seen in middle aged patients above 40 years of age and affect both sexes equally. They usually affect extremities and most common sites are femur, radius, tibia and humerus. However, the lesion has been reported originating in spine, scapula, ribs, clavicle, metacarpals, metatarsals, pelvis, mandible and skull. To the best of ourknowledge, only three cases have been reported in the pediatric age group, and there have been nonereported involving the rib.
A 14-year-old female child presented with swelling on the left upper back for the past four years. Gradually increasing in size, not associated with pain, no loss of function or history of any neurologic deficit. No history of previous trauma. On examination, a single, firm, non-tender, regular swelling but adhered to the deep planes. Distal pulse and neurological examination were normal.
Plain radiograph of the right chest revealed normal study. Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) of the left upper back revealed a hyper intense lesion on the left paravertebral region extending from D5 to D12, which is suppressed on STIR sequence. Excision and was found to be densely adhered to the underlying rib, inter costal space was widened and the mass was seen entering into the intercostal space but pleura was not breached. Histopathology of the excised specimen suggestive of lipoma. The patient is doing well at 3 months follow up and is asymptomatic.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 119M: Multiple Bowel perforations due to accidental toothpick ingestion - A rare case report

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

After adhesiolysis each of the three perforations were repaired with purse string sutures followed by peritoneal lavage,placement of drain in pelvis and a diverting ileal loop stoma was created.The patient had uneventful recovery and demonstrated clinical improvement with resolution of abdominal pain and functional stoma.Oral intake was gradually reintroduced, and the patient tolerated a regular diet. He was discharged from the hospital after seven days and was followed up in outpatient department.This case emphasizes the significance of high grade of clinical suspicion for correct diagnosis of this condition and early intervention in managing similar cases,ultimately improving patient prognosis and thus reducing morbidity and mortality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 144 O: The guiding light in darkness: Importance of AFP in early detection of Hepatoblastoma recurrence / residual tumour

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Sampreeti Mukherjee, Vishesh Jain, Anjan Dhua, Devasenathipathy Kandasamy, Manisha Jana, Rakesh Kumar, Sameer Bakhshi, Sandeep Agarwala

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : sampreetimukherjee@gmail.com
Aim: Recurrent hepatoblastoma (HB) is extremely difficult to treat and salvage, and it is a challenge to diagnose the time and site of recurrence before it’s too late. Recurrence of this disease is rare and there is a dearth of studies in its management and detection. We aim to present a series of two cases of HB wherein rise in alpha- fetoprotein levels (AFP) gave a forewarning about HB recurrence/ residual tumor much before the lesion showed up on scans.
Clinical Presentation: Two male children, aged 10 and 60 months, presented with Pretext 3 and 1 HB respectively. They were both given PLADO chemotherapy. Both children underwent successful primary surgery. Post operatively, child with pretext 3 HB initially had a falling trend of AFP which again started rising 4 months after primary surgery. The other child had normalized AFP levels, which however, started rising 8 months after primary surgery. In both the cases, the site of recurrence could not be detected initially in any form of imaging (CT/ FDG PET) but a suspicion of recurrence was kept high in view of slowly rising AFP levels. About 4-5 months after rise in AFP levels, the locally recurrent lesions were detected on CT.
Result: Residual and recurrent HB was suspected very early on by monitoring trends of AFP levels in these children. However, the challenge was in detecting the site of recurrence. Both the children received salvage chemotherapy and underwent second surgery for recurrence, and only one of them is alive at follow up after 6 months.
Conclusion: The importance of serial AFP monitoring is stressed upon. A high suspicion of residual tumor/ recurrence in HB should be made if AFP levels fail to reach baseline after surgery/ if there is progressive rise in AFP levels after reaching baseline, respectively.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 146 O: Case Report- Congenital perineal mass with genitourinary anomalies

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Sheetal Uttaray, C Goswami, Vishesh Jain, Devasenathipathy, Manisha Jana, Priyanka, Rajni Yadav, Sandeep Agarwala

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : drsheetaluttaray@gmail.com
Background: Congenital perineal masses are uncommon but usually benign masses such as lipoma, hamartoma, lipoblastoma, choristoma, perineal hernia, and vascular anomalies. It can occur along with other birth defects such as anorectal malformations (ARMs) and genitourinary anomalies.
Case: 15month female child presented with reddish colored mass protruding from perineum along with dribbling of urine. MRI pelvis was suggestive of prolapsing tubular mass anterior to anal canal along with bifid vagina and urinary bladder draining into the mass. The child underwent examination under anesthesia and panendoscopy along with high sigmoid loop colostomy . Four orifices found in the vestibular region-urethral opening and two vaginal openings anterior to prolapsing mass and anal opening on the undersurface of prolapsing mass. She further underwent excision of perineal mass along with reconstruction of perineal body. Histopathology report showed fibromuscular wall lined by anal and colonic mucosa.It showed moderate mixed inflammation along with reactive lymphoid aggregates. Child recovered without any complications.
Discussion: Etiopathogenesis of perineal masses remains unknown. During embryogenesis, the perineal area is formed by the tip of the urorectal septum, which divides the cloaca into a ventral part (the urogenital sinus) and a dorsal part (the rectum and proximal anal canal); this process may explain the anorectal or urogenital anomalies associated with perineal mass, which may be caused by the abnormal development of urorectal septum.
Conclusion: Congenital perineal mass are rare diagnosis but needs thorough evaluation for urogenital and/ or anorectal anomalies.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 149 O: Unusual Presentation of Retroperitoneal Teratoma

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Neha Masrani, Shravya Shetty, Suraj Gandhi, Neha Sisodiya Shenoy, Hemanshi Shah

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Topiwala National Medical College And Nair Hospital, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : masranineha@gmail.com
Abstract: Background –Mature cystic teratomas are benign tumours that contain tissues derived from all three germinal layers namely ectoderm, mesoderm and endoderm. They are composed of cyst or cysts filled with fluid or thick, yellow sebaceous material lined by simple ciliated or stratified squamous epithelium, skin appendages and also show presence of other tissues such as cartilage, bone, nerve cells and intestinal mucosa.
Teratomas can affect individuals at any age, any location with variable clinical presentation .Retroperitoneal teratomas are rare tumours of childhood. They are divided into three categories – classical, fetiform and malignant teratoma- depending on the clinical presentation.
Case presentation: We report one such rare presentation of a 1 year old female patient with a palpable left hypochondrium lump. In view of sudden onset respiratory distress and rapidly progressing nature of the lesion and a suspicion of an intratumoral hemorrhage in mind, an exploratory laparotomy was done and the entire lesion was excised and sent for HPE. The mass was seen arising retroperitoneally adherent to the transverse colon abutting the renal vessels, kidneys, IVC and spleen and there was no intratumoral hemorrhage. The Histopathological examination of the excised lesion revealed the diagnoses of a mature cystic teratoma.
Conclusion: Most retroperitoneal teratomas are benign and diagnosed preoperatively .In a few unusual cases and presentations, there can be a dilemma in establishing the diagnoses so in such cases intra operative findings or even histopathology reveals the final confirmatory diagnoses. Surgical excision of retroperitoneal teratoma in toto is curative and definitive treatment modality.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 150 O: Melanotic neuroectodermal tumor of infancy : A rare case to report

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Priyanka Warde, Rakesh Joshi, Jaishree Ramji

Department of Pediatric Surgery, B. J. Medical College, Civil Hospital, Ahmedabad, Gujarat Email ID : wardepriyanka2@gmail.com
Melanotic neuroectodermal tumor of infancy (MNTI) is a rare expansile rapid growing osteolytic benign tumor with a high recurrence rate. Less than 500 cases have been reported worldwide as of now. The tumor originates from the neural crest cell which produces melanin. The tumor is usually in the head and neck region, most commonly arising from maxilla. One such rare case was managed in our department. A five and a half month old baby boy with previously excised upper gingival swelling elsewhere presented with biopsy report of MNTI with recurrence. The tumor was resistant to chemotherapy. A MDCT scan revealed a expansile heterogenous osteolytic lesion of 4.5*4*4cm3 involving left mid maxilla in alveolar region with peripheral irregular margin of sclerosis. The tumor was completely excised with the alveolar region involving the palate. Histoplathology confirmed it as MNTI with IHC positive for CD 99 and HMB4. After 7 days of nasogastric feeds, the baby was able to take breast feeds well and discharged. Patient is referred for chemotherapy in follow-upfollow-up they are planning to start cisplatin based chemotherapy in this patient after CTscan.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 158 O: Paratesticular malignant ectomesenchymoma (MEM) in infancy – A case report

Category : MISCELLANEOUS

Siddhi Sanglikar, Arka Banerjee, Pradnya S. Bendre

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital For Children, Mumbai, Maharashtra Email ID : sanglikarsiddhi@gmail.com
Background: Previously known as gangliorhabdomyosarcoma, a malignant ectomesenchymoma (MEM) is an extremely rare pediatric soft tissue tumor, currently included in the category of skeletal muscle malignancies and characterized by co-existence of neuro-ectodermal and mesenchymal components.
Case report: We present the case of a 2-year-old boy with an ulcerating left scrotal mass. After a high inguinal orchiectomy, histopathological examination revealed a paratesticular (arising from epididymis) malignant ectomesenchymoma (MEM). The tumor cells expressed desmin and myogenin while S-100 protein and SOX-10 were focally expressed in Schwann-like cells. There were ganglion cells as well that expressed synaptophysin.
Discussion: Most of the reported cases are in adolescents. There are only 19 previously published reports of paratesticular MEM in young infants (mean age 12.18 ± 8.86 years). Ectomesenchymoma most commonly manifests in the pelvis/perineum. There is no associated specific molecular abnormality. Mesenchymal element (rhabdomyosarcoma) is the dominant component and is usually of embryonal morphology. The neuroectodermal component demonstrates primitive neuroectodermal tumor-like morphology. Treatment is complete surgical excision followed by adjuvant chemotherapy (IVA – Ifosfamide, Vincristine, Adriamycin). The outcome is favorable with a 94.74% survival after a follow up of 5.98 ± 6.23 years.

Here we report a case of 10 years old male child presented with hypertension and CNS symptoms, was diagnosed to have hypertensive encephalopathy secondary to bilateral pheochromocytoma with genetically confirmed VHL disease. ## Poster Presentation Session : “UROLOGY”: Poster Presentation : “UROLOGY”: ABSTRACT NO. : 200U: Preservation of colonic pouch for bladder augmentation in girls with congenital pouch colon

Category : UROLOGY

Chandra Vibhash, Subhasis Roy Choudhury, Pratap Singh Yadav, Rajiv Chaddha, Kanu Kapoor

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College & Associated Kalawati Saran Children Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : chandravibhash@yahoo.com
Aims: Complete urinary incontinence is a distressing problem in girls with congenital pouch colon (CPC) due to small capacity bladder and wide-open bladder neck. Moreover, they have short length of large bowel. We hereby present the use of residual colonic pouch for later bladder augmentation in cases of CPC in girls with urinary incontinence.
Methods: Female patients with CPC underwent preservation of their residual pouch during initial pouch tapering and bowel pull through procedure at infancy. Tapering was done dividing the pouch into two parts; a tubularised colon in continuity with the proximal bowel for pull-through anorectoplasty and the residual diverticular part of the pouch retained for future bladder augmentation (Fig. 1). The vascular supply coming from above and below the bowel was sufficient for both the segments. A small cutaneous stoma was made in the residual pouch on the anterior abdominal wall for the purpose of cleaning bowel mucous (Fig. 2).
Results: Five girls with CPC underwent the above procedure during infancy. In 2 girls with urinary incontinence, bladder augmentation was done using the portion of the retained residual pouch at the age of 11y and 18y (Fig. 3). The assessed capacity of the retained colonic pouch was about 100 cc at the time of augmentation surgery.
Conclusion: Urinary incontinence in girls with CPC often requires bladder augmentation at a later age due to small capacity of the native bladder. Retained colonic pouch was found to provide an effective substance for augmenting the bladder in girls with CPC.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 214U: Diagnostic and management challenges in Cloaca: Our experience with eleven cases

## ABSTRACT NO. : 187U: Renal impairment and its risk factors in children with PUV- JIPMER experience

Category : UROLOGY

Naseera Koya, Bibekanand Jindal, Bikash Kumar Naredi, Sriram Krishnamurthy, Jayaprakash Sahoo

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Jawaharlal Post Graduate Medical Education And Research, Pondicherry Email ID : naseerakoya@gmail.com
Aim: To study renal impairment in children with posterior urethral valve and identify the risk factors (mode and timing of presentation, UTI, VUR, VURD) for chronic kidney disease.
Method: An ambi-directional cohort study of PUV with minimum of 2years post fulguration follow up and data related to renal function (blood urea, creatinine, height, weight) collected and eGFR calculated to detect deterioration of renal function at the earliest along with various other modifiable and non-modifiable factors.
Results: Sample size of 85 cases of post surgical intervention PUV, of which 10% were prospective and rest 90% were retrospective cases. Median years of follow up was 5yrs with 2 to 15yrs of follow up. Mean age at present is 6.6yrs. 52% were antenatally detected, with 50% cases operated during neonatal period. 100% children underwent cystoscopy fulguration and 15% had in addition diversion procedure. Antenatal detection, early surgical intervention, and Nadir creatinine of more than 1 mg/min/1.73m2 at presentation were significantly related to poor outcome. Fifty six (56%) had various grade of CKD, of which 9% having grade 4/5 CKD. Eighty (80%) showed improvement in eGFR. Thirty cases (35%) cases had VUR with 100% of them being high grade and 26% of then were bilateral with left VUR more than right. 19% cases of VUR found to have resolved post PUV ablation. 65% cases had renal scars of which 40% were bilateral. 10% cases showed worsening of renal function and no case had improvement in function or scar. VURD noted in 15 cases with 8 right sided and 7 left side.
Conclusion: Antenatal detection, early surgical intervention, Nadir creatinine >1 mg/min/1.73m2 at presentation in our study with small sample size and shorter follow-up. Studies with longer duration of follow up with larger sample size are recommended to validate the findings and to overcome limitations.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 181U: Analysis of the efficacy of elastography in comparison with renal nuclear scan for unilateral pelvi-ureteric junction obstruction

Category : UROLOGY

Anand Pandey, Nirpex Tyagi, Anit Parihar, Shashwat Verma, Nitin Pant, Piyush Kumar, Sudhir Singh, Jiledar Rawat

Department of Pediatric Surgery, King George’s Medical University, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh Email ID : dranand27@rediffmail.com
Aims: In Hydronephrosis due to pelviureteric junction obstruction (PUJO), ultrasonography (USG) is the initial imaging modality used, and renal nuclear scan is the main diagnostic tool for detecting the presence of obstruction. Recently, there are reports on renal pelvic assessment using ultrasound elastography (USE). This study was conducted to see if USE can evaluate PUJO and if it can be correlated to the findings of the dynamic renal nuclear scan.
Methods: In this study, only patients with unilateral PUJO were included. The patients underwent acoustic radiation force impulse (ARFI) elastography. For ARFI measurements, a rectangular region of interest (ROI) measuring 5 x 10 mm was positioned on the cortex region of the upper, mid, and lower poles. Three valid measurements were obtained, from which a mean value was calculated. A dynamic renal nuclear scan using Technetium-99m ethylene dicysteine (EC or TC99m EC) was obtained for comparison and split renal function (SRF) was used for comparison.
Results: - In the group of 20 patients, the mean age was 3.37 years. The mean SRF of the affected kidney was 26.65% and the corresponding USE value was 0.45 kpa. The Spearman’s rho Correlation Coefficient for SRF and USE was 1 and 0.672, respectively (p=0.001). Elastography was not feasible if SRF was less than 20%.
Conclusion:USE may have a potential for assessment of PUJO. Even if USE is reported as non-feasible, it may suggest that renal function is grossly compromised. It may be used as a supportive investigating modality for nuclear studies.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 206U: Lead better Politano technique for the treatment of VUR: Technical modifications and Outcomes

Category : UROLOGY

Clovey Singhal, Satish K. Aggarwal, Chandrika Kalagotla, Garvita Singh, Varsha Verma, Kriti Mittal, Shubham

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi Email ID : clovey.singhal@gmail.com
Introduction: Leadbetter Politano technique involves mobilization of ureter behind the bladder, creation of a new hiatus by extravesical manipulation and re-routing the ureter through the new hiatus. Psoas hitch is often necessary to fix the bladder to prevent kinking during filling. A modification of technique is presented that makes it simpler, avoids kinking and Psoas hitch is not required.
Materials and Methods (Technique): The ureter is mobilized intravesically. Peritoneum is pushed upwards and vas is identified and saved. Detrussor is cleared behind the bladder for some distance keeping the vas and peritoneum down. A submucosal tunnel is made above the hiatus thus clearing the detrussor on both sides. Detrussor is then incised above the hiatus for about 1 cm. Ureter is brought up and detrussor is repaired behind it thus shifting the hiatus up and lateral. The orifice is shifted further down by creating additional tunnel towards bladder neck.
Cases details: Out of 22 ureteric units, 13 were reimplanted by this technique. The technique is best suited for orifices situated fairly laterally. For more medial location of orifice an extravesical reimplant is preferred. Tapering is done whenever required.
Results: All surgeries were uneventful with no recurrence of reflux or obstruction.
Conclusion: Modified LB Politano technique is easy and applicable for most cases of VUR. Case selection depends on ureteric meatal location. Since the dissection behind the bladder is limited and the entire procedure is intravesical. Psoas hitch is not required. Tunnel length can be controlled depending on the ureteric diameter.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 194U: Is ultrasonography a useful tool for screening complete androgen insensitivity syndrome in prepubertal females with inguinal hernia?

Category : UROLOGY

Nidhi S Patil, Yadav D K, Jana M, Agarwala S, Jain V, Dhua A, Goel P, Anand S

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : nidhip9498@gmail.com
Aim : The aim of the study was to determine whether high resolution ultrasonography (HRUSG) can be used as a screening tool for Complete Androgen Insensitivity Syndrome (CAIS) in prepubertal girls presenting with inguinal hernia by identifying the mullerian structures.
Materials and Methods: A prospective study was done to include consecutive prepubertal females presenting with inguinal hernia. They were evaluated by clinical examination, abdominal HRUSG and karyotyping.
Results: One hundred twenty-nine cases (median age -41 months) were identified from June 2014 to May 2019. All the cases had an external appearance of normal looking female genitalia. None of them had attained menarche. HRUSG evaluation showed uterus in 122 of 129 cases (94.6%). Karyotype evaluation showed 46XX in 126 patients and 46XY in 3 of 129 patients (2.3%, CAIS pts).
Conclusion: The preliminary findings suggest that the HRUSG is a good modality to rule out CAIS in female patient with inguinal hernia in a limited resource scenario.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 185U: Ipsilateral scrotal fullness - A surrogate indicator for testicle location in unilateral impalpable undescended testis

Category : UROLOGY

Sugandha Agarwal, Vishesh Jain, Prabudh Goel, Anjan Kumar Dhua, Sandeep Agarwala, Sachit Anand, Devendra Kumar Yadav, Manisha Jana

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, New Delhi Email ID : vnsv14@gmail.com
Introduction and Aim: Location and palpability of undescended testis predicts the surgical approach (open and laparoscopic). The study aimed to assess the correlation between the position of unilateral undescended testis (UDT) and the ipsilateral scrotal fullness.
Methods: Consecutive cases of unilateral UDT were evaluated prospectively. The location of testis [palpable (P), non-palpable (NP)] & ipsilateral scrotal fullness was noted before the operation and under the anaesthesia.USG had been done for all cases with scrotal fullness. The testis was palpated by two different paediatric surgery residents and one consultant separately. All palpable testis were treated by open surgery, and non-palpable testis was considered for laparoscopic evaluation. The intraoperative location of NP testis was noted.
Results: Fifty-three unilateral non-palpable testes (11 months to 68 months) were registered from June 2014 to May 2022. 41 of 53 cases had intra-abdominal testis. In 12 of 53 cases, initially labelled as NP testis, the testis was found in the inguinal canal by ultrasound evaluation. These 12 cases had near normal scrotal fullness as compared to opposite site scrotum. The scrotum was poorly developed in 41 intra-abdominal located testes.
Conclusion: The ipsilateral scrotal fullness indicates the well-developed gubernaculum and thus act as an indicator of testicle descent beyond the internal ring. USG is a useful tool for localization of testis, if scrotal fullness is present at the ipsilateral side. The laparoscopy can be avoided, in these cases.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 186U: Assessment of the socio-demographic profile of Individuals with Disorders of Sexual Development and psycho-social concerns

Category : UROLOGY

Lucky Gupta, Lucky Gupta

Department of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute Of Medical Sciences, Vijaypur, Jammu, Jammu & Kashmir Email ID : lucky.gupta@aiimsjammu.edu.in
Aims: This is an attempt to assess the socio-demographic profile of patients with DSD and psycho-social concerns among them and their family members in the Jammu region.
Materials and methods: A retrospective study of 42 patients, birth to 18 years of age, with DSD, registered in the Pediatric Surgery Out-patient Department, and were on follow-up of a single Pediatric surgeon from 2010 to 2021 were analyzed.

Sociodemographic variables- age, sex at presentation, family history, socioeconomic status, consanguinity, and relevant antenatal data.
Clinical variables- dysmorphic features, pigmentation, investigations including karyotyping, abdominopelvic ultrasonography, basal or stimulated hormonal levels along with genitogram. Psychological assessments of patients along with their parents on denial, anxiety, guilt, worthlessness, self-destructive, homicidal ideations towards the individual, or acceptance.

Results: Forty-two patients were enrolled in the study. 26 (62%) were genetically male (46XY) and 16 (38%) were genetically female (46XX). 14 (33%) cases of ambiguous genitalia presented in infancy and 28 (67%) in the pediatric age group. Among 46XY DSD, the most common etiology was partial androgen insensitivity syndrome seen in 20 (47%) patients. Complete androgen insensitivity syndrome was seen in 2 (4%) patients and 5 alpha reductase deficiency was seen in 2(4%) patients. Among 46XX DSD, the most common etiology was congenital adrenal hyperplasia which was seen in 15 (36%) patients. Pure gonadal dysgenesis (2%) and Persistent mullerian duct syndrome (2%) were seen in one patient each. Positive family history was seen in five patients (12%) and the history of sibling death was due to salt-wasting syndrome. The psycho-social assessment showed the psychological trauma of parents and patients which decreased with effective treatment and proper counseling.
Conclusion: Successful outcomes of DSD depend on timely diagnosis of the condition, so that, potential sexual function and fertility can be preserved and cosmetic and functioning external genitalia can be provided along with dealing with the family’s concerns and anxiety effectively.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 221U: My innovations in hypospadias surgery

Category : UROLOGY

Sagar Jawale, Sagar Jawale

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Jawale Hospital, Jalgaon, Maharashtra Email ID : drsagarjawale@gmail.com
Introduction: Last 20 years I performed 1978 cases of hypospadias surgeries and made various innovations in the technique of surgery.Most innovations are reported for the first time in medical literature.

Materials and methods:

1)718 cases done with continuous interlocking stitches instead of simple and every 5 Th stitch a knot is taken. They are water tight and knot prevents unwinding of complete suture line.
2)1568 cases, IV amino acid solution was given which supplies essential amino acids which led to much better healing.
3)Glutaraldehyde Albumin induced laser tissue welding in 318 cases which reinforces skin suture line making it waterproof.
4)13 hypospadias fistulae closed without surgery by above technique.
5)Methylene blue dye induced laser tissue welding in 58 cases, stopped bleeding which did not with cautery.
6)Urethral pull up operation on 135 cases, free of complications such as fistula, meatal stenosis, meatal regression and residual chordee and one that does not require Dartos flap and suprapubic diversion
7)Modified urethral pull up operation on 78 cases with urethra pulled through “Glans Tunnel”.
8)Appendicular Mucosal Tube Implant with Dartos Wrap Operation in 23 cases of bad quality urethral plate.
9)Extended Urethral Plate Mobilization and reimplantation surgery in proximal hypospadias with severe chordee in 108 cases. Urethral plate lifted, chordee released and proximal mobilization till bulbar urethra.
10)Implanting delipated full thickness graft from inguinal area in stage 1 cases with bad quality urethral plates in proximal hypospadias.
11)Suprapubic diversion even in distal hypospadias cases as collagen is laid down in 3 weeks.
12)Low dose oral steroids in 38 cases for severe post op pain and in urethral stenosis requiring repetitive dilatations.

Discussion: With techniques described conventionally, success rate could not cross 60%. All above innovations improved success rate from 60 to 99%.Results: Results were much better and statistically significant compared to control group.
Conclusion: We need constant modifications in conventional techniques of hypospadias surgery for better results.

## ABSTRACT NO. : 224U: Comparing safety and efficacy of contrast-enhanced ultrasound versus micturating cystourethrography: Evidences and Obstacles

Category : UROLOGY

Siddhi Sanglikar, Pradnya S. Bendre

Department of Pediatric Surgery, Bai Jerbai Wadia Hospital For Children, Mumbai Email ID : sanglikarsiddhi@gmail.com
Background: Micturating cystourethrography (MCUG) is the primary imaging technique for demonstrating vesicoureteral reflux (VUR). Contrast-enhanced Ultrasound (CeUS) is an equally efficacious alternative for detecting VUR in children, but without the adverse effects of radiation exposure.
Aim: Efficacy of CeUS in diagnosing and grading VUR in children
Methods: Children (<5 years) with hydroureteronephrosis presenting between January 2022- December 2022 were enrolled in the study. After ruling out any urinary tract infection (UTI), bladder was catheterized and filled with diluted SonoVue® contrast in saline solution. Reflux was diagnosed by observation of microbubbles in ureter and/or renal pelvis and graded accordingly. Conventional MCUG was also performed for comparison. Children without VUR were excluded.
Results: Thirty-nine refluxing units were studied in thirty patients with VUR (19 boys, 11 girls). None of the VURs detected by MCUG were missed by CeUS. When CeUS-detected grades were compared with those detected by MCUG, 22 (56.41%) showed the same grade. CeUS demonstrated a higher grade in 8 (20.5%) units and a lower grade in 9 (23%) units.
Conclusion: CeUS is a sensitive and safer alternative to MCUG to detect VUR in children with hydroureteronephrosis. Despite the risk of UTI due to catheterization, it can be used in the long term for follow-up, considering the overall radiation exposure reduction. The cost and availability of a suitable water-soluble echogenic contrast remains a limiting factor.
